Issue:  RESOLVED, and it supersedes the hard stop below. The SAFE_HALT bypass is DEMONSTRATED against 694d570. At 22:26:37 a fresh Navigator attach inherited the both-zero inputs MT5 retains as last-used, refused on the config check, and its OnDeinit wrote a default-constructed model over the halt file: six session records reduced to zero, then reseeded by the next healthy attach at 22:26:54. Nobody staged it, and no evidence was lost, because the pre-state was already backed up, the journal recorded it, and the post-state proves the wipe. The discriminator is measured rather than argued: the erasing refusal logged timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0, an image whose globals had never been touched, while the harmless 22:35:29 input-edit refusal logged timer_armed=1 and timer_ticks=514 in an inherited running image, same inputs and same alert text, opposite consequence. Evidence: docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md addendum, backups halt_1200252169.dat.after-config-refusal-2026-07-30 (pre, md5 3209ABA4) and halt_1200252169.dat.post-erasure-reseeded-2026-07-30 (post, md5 3A98CFF4). Erasure half only, per the standing scope limit.
Action: The re-sequencing ruling the entry below asks for is no longer needed for the config path. 72ed960 deployed and compiled at 22:39:30, 0 errors 0 warnings, but the running instance still carries the 694d570 image because MT5 does not hot-reload an externally recompiled .ex5, so the fix proof needs a removal and a fresh attach. The SPEC threat-model wording was ruled by the owner on 2026-07-30 and applied to section 7 and to the section 9 threat-model addition: erasure requires a refusal inside a fresh program image, a refusal in an inherited image is harmless, the timer_armed and timer_ticks discriminator is stated in the wording so the boundary stays testable, the fresh-image routes are enumerated, and the original trigger sentence is kept because it was always the fresh-load case.
Status: DONE

Issue:  Fresh-load audit, ordered by the owner and delivered in docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md. Governing rule, now measured: an input-change re-init reuses the loaded image and its globals survive, while a fresh attach, a recompile, and a terminal restart all reset globals, and MT5 seeds a fresh attach from last-used inputs that persist across restarts via the chart profile. Exposed rows: A1 (a malformed second chart refuses at the config check before ever reaching the mutex, testing nothing), A2, A3, A4, A6 and A7 (all restore inputs from the chart profile, and since AgHaltAppendSession sits downstream of every refusal return, a malformed profile makes crash counting unable to accumulate at all), A8 (only through its starting attach), and A9 in the opposite direction: a malformed row driven by editing a RUNNING instance inherits a populated model, so the fix's skip branch never executes and the row proves the refusal without proving the fix.
Action: Adopted for the rest of the matrix: every attach instruction names the exact input values to confirm before OK, the executor takes the halt baseline BEFORE the attach rather than after, and each A9 row runs as a fresh load beginning from a removal.
Status: FINAL

Issue:  SUPERSEDED by the entry above, kept for the record. HARD STOP, contact 1 halted after step 2. The bypass demonstration did not reproduce, and the reason narrows the bypass itself. On 694d570 the owner set both daily limits to zero in the properties dialog at 22:08:42; the refusal fired verbatim as predicted, but the halt file was NOT erased: all six session records survived and record 6 flipped clean=0 to clean=1, the legitimate outcome of the outgoing session's clean deinit. Measured cause: timer_ticks=40714 is identical in the reason=5 deinit and the reason=8 deinit, so the program image was never unloaded between them. An input change runs OnDeinit then OnInit inside the same loaded program, globals intact, so the refused init inherited the model loaded at 10:50:08 and the unconditional AgHaltSave wrote a POPULATED model rather than a default-constructed one. Erasure requires genuinely fresh globals, which means a fresh program load that then refuses. Evidence: docs/evidence/bypass-demo-2026-07-30.md, backup halt_1200252169.dat.after-config-refusal-2026-07-30 md5 3209ABA485948D39872B0181C06BE2D6. First, the re-sequencing: demonstrate the erasure on 694d570 via a genuine fresh-load refusal, either a second-chart mutex refusal (the SPEC's own trigger, and A1's setup) or an EA removal followed by a fresh Navigator attach carrying a malformed core input. Second, the wording precision in the SPEC threat model and in the bypass ISSUES entry, which is a SPEC change and therefore owner-ruled. The live halt file was deliberately NOT restored from backup: nothing was destroyed, and record 6's new clean flag is information a restore would overwrite.
Status: BLOCKED on owner

Issue:  Measured design input for Phases 1 and 2, recorded now and NOT acted on, per owner instruction. TimeCurrent freezes for the whole weekend, so everything derived from it freezes with it: the day anchor floors TimeCurrent, and expiry compares TimeCurrent to locked_until. A lock taken Friday therefore expires when quotes resume, not at its computed anchor. Q7 accepted that direction as erring locked, but accepted it as a rare dead-market edge case; this measurement shows it is a routine weekly condition lasting about two days, and the weekly measurement window inherits the same property.
Action: Specify day anchor and expiry semantics in Phase 1 and 2 against a clock that is normally frozen for 48 hours every week. The owner's threat-model reading is CONFIRMED for expiry and CORRECTED for the PnL window. Confirmed: Market Watch contents are owner-mutable and influence how long TimeCurrent stays frozen, removing symbols can only delay an unlock which errs locked, and no arrangement can push TimeCurrent ahead of real server time because it derives from server-supplied quote timestamps rather than the local clock, which is exactly why Q7 chose it over TimeTradeServer. Correction, flagged as analysis to verify in Phase 1 rather than as measurement: the PnL window is NOT safe in both directions. SPEC 4.2 selects history over (anchor, now); if now is a frozen TimeCurrent and a deal executes after the freeze point, that deal falls outside the upper bound and is not counted, which is fail-open under-counting rather than erring locked. A stale anchor lengthens the window and errs strict, but a stale upper bound truncates it and errs permissive. Phase 1 obligation: the history select upper bound must not be a frozen clock. Scope note per owner ruling 2026-08-01, applied 2026-08-02: classification downgraded from blocker class to a Phase 1 correctness obligation, not closed. The concrete crypto example that illustrated it was struck on 2026-08-02, pre-approved by the owner, as no longer operative: under the Market Watch ruling the selection is XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn only, all 24/5, so no instrument quotes while TimeCurrent is frozen at the weekend and no deal can execute inside the frozen window here. The obligation survives because the general defect needs no 24/7 instrument: an upper bound derived from a possibly stale clock truncates the window and under-counts realized loss on any quiet stretch, and the measured 5.4 minute midnight freeze is the same mechanism at small scale. Added by measurement 2026-08-01: TimeCurrent is not monotonic either. After BTCUSD.ecn left Market Watch, the 23:26:38 reconnect rewound it from 2026.08.01 23:02:41 to 2026.07.31 23:57:59, about 25 hours backward, so Phase 1 and 2 semantics must tolerate a backward-stepping clock; backward errs locked for expiry but truncates the PnL upper bound further, the same fail-open direction.
Status: OPEN, Phase 1 and 2 input

Issue:  SAFE_HALT BYPASS, found by source read on 2026-07-29 before any acceptance row ran. OnDeinit runs after a refused OnInit (reason 8) and saved the halt model unconditionally at AccountGuardian.mq5:234. On that path AgHaltLoad never executed, so the model was default-constructed empty, and writing it erased all session records and cleared the persisted halt flag on disk. GV AG_HALT_<login> still read 1, so the next init saw GV-set against file-clear and logged RESUMED_FROM_SAFE_HALT: a malfunctioning guardian back in service with no human act, violating the manual-restart-only ruling. Trigger needs no privilege: attach a second instance and let the mutex refuse it. Every config-refusal return at AccountGuardian.mq5:92-113 reaches the same path, so the A9 input matrix would have destroyed evidence too. Blocks A1, A2, A3, A4, A6, A9. Classified as a bypass, not data loss.
Action: Fixes written, not yet compiled or deployed, pending step 1 of the owner's sequence which must demonstrate the bug against the unfixed build first. Fix 1: g_ag_halt_loaded in Persist.mqh, set at every exit of AgHaltLoad, checked in OnDeinit, with an INFO line on the skip so it is never silent. Gating on g_owns_mutex was rejected as conflating mutex ownership with model validity, and it would have masked the config-refusal case. Fix 2 (A8, same build): TimeCurrent and TimeLocal on the LIFE line. Recorded in the SPEC threat model with the general obligation and the best-effort caveat on manual-resume detection. Halt file backed up first, two copies, md5 0f40c49bc67a3767b22b9491910e1ade. Closed 2026-08-04: both fixes shipped and were proven on 72ed960 on 2026-07-30 and re-proven on the Stage 5a build at 15:56:00, and the blocked-rows list this entry names is fully discharged, A1, A2, A3, A4 and A6 all closed by the kill session of 2026-08-04 and A9 gated only on the Load-browser check.
Status: DONE

Issue:  CLOSED AS PHANTOM. No defect was ever demonstrated. The reported blocker rested on two independent observation failures that together produced a convincing picture of a dead timer: the old build's liveness line routed through AgVerbose and was suppressed at NORMAL verbosity, so silence was guaranteed regardless of execution; and the F3 dialog does not refresh while it is open, so a variable that was updating the whole time displayed as frozen. Sampled correctly (open, read, fully close, wait, reopen, read) the mutex heartbeat advances by the elapsed seconds. The behaviour of build 3627886 is UNKNOWN and unprovable in retrospect, and no cause is backfilled here.
Action: The change set was still worth landing on its own merits, independent of the phantom: the EventSetTimer return was unchecked at all three call sites, and SYNCING was a state the EA could occupy indefinitely while emitting nothing. Amendment A3 is the reason the question became answerable at all. Corroboration from MQL5\Logs\20260729.log: 15 LIFE lines, spacing a consistent 30 s to within 10 ms, seconds_in_state advancing 1, 31, 61, 91, and deinit reporting timer_armed=1 with timer_ticks=212 across a 213-second session, which is one tick per second sustained.
Status: DONE

Issue:  Standing observation rules, learned the hard way, binding on every future session.
Action: (1) External reads of bases\gvariables.dat are never evidence while the terminal runs. (2) The F3 dialog is a snapshot; a GV reading is admissible only as open, read, fully close, wait, reopen, read. (3) Absence of log output is never evidence of absence of execution unless the emitting line is known to be unsuppressed at the active verbosity; verify the emission path before concluding anything from silence. (4) Added by owner order 2026-07-30: journal lines emitted at OS shutdown can be lost unflushed even when the code that printed them ran to completion, so the halt file, not the journal, is the authoritative witness for shutdown behavior; measured on the 2026-07-29/30 overnight event, where OnDeinit provably ran (clean flag written, mutex zeroed) and its deinit lines do not exist in the log. (5) Added by owner order 2026-07-31: a decoder that fails silently returns an empty result indistinguishable from a genuine absence of matches; measured 2026-07-30 when iconv returned empty on a UTF-16 CRLF chart profile that PowerShell read correctly. Any negative result from a text search must confirm the decoder actually read the file before the absence counts as evidence. (6) Added by measurement 2026-08-04: the metaeditor64.exe process exit code is NOT a compile status signal and is inverted in practice on this installation. Measured with a control: the real build logged "Result: 0 errors, 0 warnings" and exited 1, while a deliberately broken copy of the same source logged "Result: 3 errors, 0 warnings" and exited 0, producing no ex5. Any gate keyed on the exit code would therefore pass every broken build and fail every clean one. The authoritative signals are the log's Result line and the presence and timestamp of the ex5, and both must be read. (7) Added by measurement 2026-08-04: the ex5 SIZE AND HASH ARE NOT BUILD IDENTITY. Five consecutive compiles of byte-identical source, verified unchanged by md5 across all five, produced five different binaries: 40992, 39982, 39450, 40470 and 40224 bytes, every one a different md5, a spread of about 1500 bytes. Two ex5 files therefore cannot be compared to decide whether they were built from the same source, in either direction: equal size would not prove sameness and unequal size does not prove difference. This strikes the corroborating use of the "ex5 39642 bytes" style figure recorded in earlier entries; those entries' conclusions stand on their other evidence, the figures are simply not identity. Build identity rests on three things instead: the md5 of every source file against the committed tree, the ex5 modification timestamp, and the runtime behaviour of the loaded image in the journal, which is the only measure that speaks to what is actually running rather than what is on disk.
Status: FINAL

Issue:  SUPERSEDED, kept for the record. Reported as a blocker on the owner's F3 reading: the timer never fired. AG_HB_1200252169 exists in the live store with the value written at acquire (Persist.mqh:251) and frozen since, and AgMutexRefresh has never executed. Mechanism, and it is inverted from what was logged before: a frozen mutex heartbeat always reads stale, and staleness authorizes takeover under F8, so every second instance takes the mutex unconditionally. Single-instance protection does not exist in the 3627886 build. Acceptance rows A1 and A2 are both broken, not merely untestable. Static findings that pin the cause to timer arming rather than to an early return: EventSetTimer had three call sites (AccountGuardian.mq5:139, 152, 161), each immediately before a return INIT_SUCCEEDED, and the journal's boot transition proves the line-161 path was the one taken with g_timer_seconds=1; between OnTimer entry and the refresh write there was exactly one guard, g_owns_mutex, set true at line 113 on that same path; inside AgMutexRefresh the only guard compares the instance GV to g_ag_instance_id and logs an ALERT when it fires, and no such line exists. So no early return can account for the freeze, and the return value of EventSetTimer was never checked.
Action: See the phantom-closure entry above. The changes shipped and are verified; the premise did not survive.
Status: DONE

Issue:  SUPERSEDED by the entry above, kept for the record. Mutex heartbeat GV AG_HB_1200252169 did not appear in an external read of bases\gvariables.dat while the EA was attached and initialized, although AG_ID_1200252169 did. The claim "this is an absence, not a read artifact" was overstated and is withdrawn: that file is a snapshot owned by the running process, and a constant length across samples is equally consistent with a file that is simply not being refreshed. Static source read settles two candidates and clears both: every write uses GlobalVariableSet, the persistent form, and GlobalVariableTemp appears nowhere in the build, so the temporary-GV explanation is out; GlobalVariablesFlush is called on all five write paths including the per-tick refresh at Persist.mqh:265, so the missing-flush explanation is out. The leftover-from-an-earlier-session explanation for AG_ID is also out: the stale prior-project globals are unsuffixed, AG_ID_1200252169 carries our account suffix, and the only session of ours that ever executed is the 14:09:40 manual attach, which wrote it at Persist.mqh:250, one line before the mutex heartbeat write.
Action: Waiting on the owner's F3 reading of the live store, which is authoritative. No fix proposed until then. If F3 shows the mutex heartbeat live and incrementing, the defect was the external read method, and this entry closes with the standing rule that external gvariables.dat reads are never evidence while the terminal runs.
Status: OPEN

Issue:  External reads of bases\gvariables.dat while the terminal is running are unreliable as evidence, in both directions: the file is a snapshot the process owns, absence there does not prove absence in the live store, and a stale length does not prove a stable variable set. One wrong conclusion has already been drawn from it.
Action: Never cite that file as evidence again. The live store is read through the terminal F3 dialog or from inside MQL5 via GlobalVariableCheck and GlobalVariableGet.
Status: OPEN

Per the FINAL remote state ruling of 2026-08-04 this is a DATED OBSERVATION and nothing more: the executor read local remote-tracking refs, not the remote itself, and this line is stale the moment either ref moves. 2026-08-19, PHASE 2 STAGE 7 MERGED TO MAIN, merge commit `1ea7c2d`, parents `49ea967` and `6219dfe`, twenty seven branch commits carried, seventeen files changed, 273 insertions and 3 deletions. PERFORMED BY THE OWNER in the main checkout, from the command and the message file the executor dictated, because a worktree cannot check out a branch that is already checked out elsewhere and this session is isolated to its worktree by design. The executor verified it read only afterwards and did not perform it.

VERIFICATION, all three commands run against the merged tree rather than against this narrative, and their output quoted rather than summarised. `git log --oneline -1 main` returns `1ea7c2d merge: Phase 2 Stage 7, deploy and live acceptance`. `git diff --stat main worktree-phase2-stage7` returns EMPTY, so main's tree is byte identical to the branch and nothing was dropped in the merge. `git log 49ea967..main --format='%an %ae' | sort -u` returns exactly one line, `www8351 www8351@gmail.com`. An attribution scan over the same range for co-authored, generated with, anthropic, ai assistant and claude returns ZERO on all five.

TWO MECHANICS CHECKS THE EXECUTOR PROMISED AT PREPARATION TIME AND THEN RAN. The commit has TWO parents, `49ea9679d19c58bfadb9fc7e73b1b1c30bb018c4` and `6219dfe07d4e19437f7b7838b8938ec393bc98df`, so `--no-ff` held and no fast forward occurred despite the merge base equalling main's head, which is exactly the case where git would otherwise have fast forwarded. AND THE COMMIT CARRIES THE COMPOSED MESSAGE rather than git's bare default MERGE_MSG. That is the third consecutive merge to survive the window that lost the messages of `937c604` on 2026-08-08 and `d52f063` on 2026-08-11, and the reason is the mitigation adopted on 2026-08-17 and repeated here: merge with `--no-ff -F <message file>` in ONE command, never `--no-ff --no-commit` followed by a separate commit, so no prepared index sits uncommitted for a periodic committer to take. Still a mitigation and still not a diagnosis; the cause of the two original losses remains unestablished.

THE ANTICIPATED LEDGER.md HAND UNION WAS NOT NEEDED AND THE EXPECTATION IS NOW DISCHARGED BY MEASUREMENT. No conflict was possible on any file. This is the second time the anticipation has proved unnecessary, after 2026-08-17, and the lesson both times is the same one: re derive the base at merge time rather than trusting either the instruction or a prior entry.

WHAT THE MERGE CARRIED, and the count is only right because a defect was caught first. Git classifies the journals as binary because MT5 writes them UTF-16LE, so they archive faithfully and will not text diff. 2026-08-19, A REPORTED MACHINE SLEEP WAS INVESTIGATED AND NO SUCH EVENT EXISTS IN ANY ARTIFACT. Owner reported that the PC entered sleep by accident and was woken, describing it as a third clock shape after a disconnect and a kill, with an approximate window of 13:00 to 15:00, and asked for the surrounding lines and the state file comparison. THE INVESTIGATION RETURNED A NEGATIVE AND IT IS RECORDED AS ONE, because the 2026-08-05 rule binds an entry to what its own session measured and a reported event that leaves no trace must be written down as absent rather than assumed and narrated. THE WINDOW WAS FIRST READ AS 2026-08-19 AND THE OWNER CORRECTED IT TO 2026-08-18, so the first pass dismissed it on the wrong ground, that 13:00 to 15:00 had not yet happened when the check ran at 10:22 on the 19th. That ground is withdrawn and the correct window was then scanned directly at a tighter threshold. RESULT ON THE CORRECTED WINDOW: across 13:00 to 15:00 on 2026-08-18 there is exactly ONE discontinuity in the guardian's LIFE sequence over 35 seconds, and it is 69 seconds at `13:45:33 -> 13:46:42`, which is the owner's own P2-B terminal restart via Task Manager, already banked in this entry. The line count corroborates it: 239 LIFE lines across the 13:00 and 14:00 hours against 240 expected at a 30 second cadence, the single missing tick being the one that 69 second gap swallowed. A two hour suspension would have left a two hour hole; the largest hole is 69 seconds. THREE INDEPENDENT FACTS PLACE THE MACHINE AWAKE THROUGHOUT: the P2-C adapter disconnect at 14:58:54 to 15:05:12 produced CONTINUOUS LIFE lines carrying `DEGRADED: disconnected`, which is the disconnect signature and not a suspension; the executor read the live log at 13:41, 13:47 and 15:04 and each read returned fresh lines; and the broker liquidation at 13:38:07, the relaunch at 13:46:41 and the reconnect at 15:04 all fall inside the window and are all evidenced.

THE ARITHMETIC, which is stronger than an absence of notices. On 2026-08-19 the LIFE lines run at their 30 second cadence from `00:00:00` to `10:22:00` with ZERO gaps exceeding 60 seconds. The expected line count across that span is 37320 seconds divided by 30 plus one, which is 1245, and the ACTUAL count is 1245: every tick is present and none is missing. The counter agrees independently: `seconds_in_state=33720` on the `10:22:01` line, and 10:22:01 less the 01:00:01 entry into ACTIVE is exactly 33720 seconds, so it tracks wall clock with no jump. The only non LIFE lines in the entire day are the three expiry lines at 01:00. No `deinit`, no `init`, no `TRANSITION` beyond the expiry, no `DEGRADED`, no `quote_age` after the nightly break closed. THE FILES AGREE: `state_1200252169.dat` is unchanged at md5 `793A00F2931A3563EE890EDA20E30BFE` since 01:00:00, and `halt_1200252169.dat` has not been written since 2026-08-18 16:01:24, which is decisive on its own because `AgHaltAppendSession` writes that file at EVERY `OnInit`, so there has been no EA initialisation of any kind for eighteen hours. On 2026-08-18 the same gap scan finds exactly three, all already banked and all explained: 25788 seconds at 03:28:35, the unattended Windows Update reboot; 69 seconds at 13:45:33, the terminal restart; and 103 seconds at 15:59:42, the two forced kills. NOTHING between 13:00 and 15:00 on either day.

CONFIRMED FROM OUTSIDE MT5 AT THE OWNER'S REQUEST, WHICH IS THE STRONGER FORM OF THIS NEGATIVE. `Get-WinEvent` over the Windows System log for event ids 42 and 107, sleep and resume, across 2026-08-18 00:00:00 to 2026-08-19 23:59:59 returned "No events were found that match the specified selection criteria". So the operating system recorded no suspension either, from a source entirely independent of the guardian's own logging. Owner accepted the negative: no sleep occurred.

WHAT A SLEEP WOULD LOOK LIKE, recorded so a future session can recognise the third shape rather than re deriving it. A suspended machine cannot run the MT5 timer, so a sleep leaves TWO simultaneous signatures that neither of the other two shapes produces together: a gap in the LIFE sequence, like a kill, AND a `seconds_in_state` that JUMPS across that gap rather than resuming, because the counter is derived from a stored entry timestamp rather than accumulated tick by tick. A disconnect freezes the server clock while local advances and the lines keep coming. A kill ends the lines and starts a new session with a fresh `init` and a halt file entry. A sleep would keep the same session, skip the lines, and step the counter. Neither signature is present anywhere in the two days examined. The only machine level event on record remains the 03:28 to 10:38 Windows Update reboot of 2026-08-18, which is a reboot and not a suspension, and it is named here as the nearest thing rather than as an identification of what the owner recalls.

THE RECONCILIATION BELOW IS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN, CORRECTED 2026-08-19 BY THE OWNER'S DIRECT READING OF THE HISTORY TAB. THE DEPOSIT LANDED AT 2026-08-18 16:51:35, as the owner stated and as planned; the executor's claim that the 2133 was already present at 16:01:27 is FALSE. RECORDED AS OWNER READ FROM THE UI, because no artifact corroborates it and the executor states that plainly: a grep of the whole 2026-08-18 terminal journal for balance and deposit deal lines returns ZERO, and the journal after 16:30 carries only network lines, so MT5 never wrote a balance record anywhere the executor can read.
THE ERROR, NAMED PRECISELY RATHER THAN GLOSSED, and there were two faults stacked. FAULT ONE, the load bearing step. The executor read `tier=floor` as meaning the live limit MEANINGFULLY exceeded the floor. It licenses no such thing: the label comes from `limit_cmp < live_limit`, an EXACT double comparison, and the ratchet epsilon defect fixed the day before proves that exact comparisons in this codebase are routinely decided by a one ULP round trip artifact. `tier=floor` is satisfied by a margin of 1e-14 and cannot carry a quantitative conclusion. FAULT TWO, the arithmetic, and this is the one that should have been caught. Base is INVARIANT under trading deals, since a loss reduces `ACCOUNT_BALANCE` and the all deals sum by the same amount, so with `base=2133.13` and `realized=-1893.50` the balance at 16:01:27 was `2133.13 - 1893.50 = 239.63`, the liquidation residue. That is the SAME 239.63 the executor's own bound produced, and it was treated as a floor beneath a supposed pre existing 2133 instead of being recognised as the residue itself. The executor then compounded it by assuming the owner's 2133 would be ADDED to that figure, expecting about 2372.63, seeing 2133.00 at the 01:00 rollover, and concluding the deposit was absent. THE CORRECT READING: the balance operation at 16:51:35 took the account FROM 239.63 TO 2133.00, and today's `base=2133.00` is the post deposit balance rather than evidence of anything pre existing. Every artifact is consistent with the owner's timestamp.
ALSO NOTED FROM THE SAME JOURNAL PULL AND NOT PREVIOUSLY RECORDED: an unplanned connection loss on 2026-08-18 at 18:02:53, `connection to JustMarkets-Demo3 lost`, with reauthorization at 18:09:04 and resynchronisation reporting `0 positions, 0 orders`. About six minutes, while the account was LOCKED, nobody's doing, and it passed without incident.

THE WITHDRAWN RECONCILIATION IS PRESERVED BELOW AS WRITTEN, since a ledger that edits away its wrong turns cannot be audited.
A RECONCILIATION THE EXECUTOR CANNOT CLOSE AND IS HANDING TO THE OWNER. `base=2133.00` with `limit=106.65`, and the floor file storing `106.65000000` exactly, pins `DailyLossPercent` at 5 in the running EA beyond inference, since 2133.00 at five percent is 106.65 exactly. With percent 5 established, the 16:01:27 witness line, `realized=-1893.50` with `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor`, REQUIRES `base > 2133.13` at that moment and therefore `ACCOUNT_BALANCE > 239.63`. Had the owner's 2133 deposit landed at about 16:52 onto a zero balance, the balance at 16:01:27 would have been 0.00, base would have been 1893.50, the live limit 94.68, and that line would have printed `limit_cmp=94.68|tier=live`. IT DID NOT. So the 2133 now in the account was ALREADY PRESENT at 16:01:27, and no further 2133 landed after it, or today's base would read about 4266 rather than 2133.00. The executor cannot see balance operations at all: the terminal journal records no balance deal on any date this session. The owner is asked to check the timestamp of the balance operation in the account history, and the discrepancy is recorded unresolved rather than explained away.

A MEASUREMENT TRAP FOUND IN THE DICTATION ITSELF AND CORRECTED BEFORE A SINGLE PATH WAS NAMED. The instruction says to dictate md5 from `git show` at `3ad7ef4`. That value is WRONG for this purpose on five of the seven files, because `.gitattributes` sets `* text=auto` and `core.autocrlf` is `true`, so `git show` emits the LF normalized blob while the file the owner actually copies carries CRLF. `AccountGuardian.mq5` is the clearest case: blob `da184295730b31a994866d55ca7a46de`, file on disk `c1e12b30ae8fddd76ccaebcf2eef7069`. Dictating the blob hash would have made every landed file fail its own verification. What is dictated is therefore the md5 of the source file on disk, which is what `Get-FileHash` reports at both ends of the copy, with the blob hash recorded here only for provenance: `Clock.mqh` blob `6c74f366bf04f4376659465d7e53b9db`, `Persist.mqh` blob `6985e83a4189aad6264cf120c6bd197b`, `Pnl.mqh` blob `f26dd45fd7355374b836de71a9d56cc1`.

AN UNPLANNED CRASH SURVIVAL LANDED BETWEEN PART 1 AND THE HOTFIX, AND IT IS BANKED AS EVIDENCE BECAUSE NOBODY DESIGNED IT. The machine was restarted at about 03:29 by an unattended Windows Update. This was reported by the owner and then VERIFIED FROM THE JOURNAL rather than taken on report, which matters because a clean shutdown and a killed process look identical in a summary and completely different in the log. The 02:01:36 session's last line is `AG|2026.08.18 03:28:37|WARN|ratchet HOLDING ...` and there is NO `deinit` line after it, against three clean `deinit|reason=` lines elsewhere in the same file, so the session was killed rather than closed. A grep for SAFE_HALT across the whole day's log returns ZERO. The crash-loop chain reading 1 of 3 rather than 2 is correct and not a miss: the gap bound is 300 seconds and the outage was about seven hours, so the chain did not extend, which is the negative-gap discipline behaving as ruled. The floor survived the reboot and reloaded from file, which is the persistence half of the ratchet proven by an event rather than by a vector. Owner has since set the `NoAutoRebootWithLoggedOnUsers` policy and Active hours 02:00 to 20:00, so this is not expected to recur, and the observation is kept because an unplanned kill is evidence no scheduled test produces.

2026-08-18, PHASE 2 STAGE 7 HOTFIX, THE RATCHET EPSILON, committed on `worktree-phase2-stage7` under the owner ruling of this date recorded in DECISIONS. Two files, 92 insertions and 2 deletions. The two deletions are the whole of the behavioural change: `if(live_limit < g_ag_floor_currency)` becomes `if(live_limit < g_ag_floor_currency - AG_PNL_EPSILON)` and `else if(live_limit > g_ag_floor_currency)` becomes `else if(live_limit > g_ag_floor_currency + AG_PNL_EPSILON)`. Everything else added is comment and vectors. `Persist.mqh` gains `#include <AccountGuardian/Pnl.mqh>` because `AG_PNL_EPSILON` is defined there, and that include was checked for cycles before it was written rather than after: `Pnl.mqh` includes only `Clock.mqh`, declares its own globals and names `DailyLossPercent` and `DailyLossCurrency` only inside message strings, so it drags in no EA-only symbol and the vectors script, which includes `Persist.mqh` alone, still stands on its own.

THE COST OF THE FIX IS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED, since a one cent band is not free. A genuine limit change smaller than one cent now moves neither the floor nor the journal, and in the lowering direction that errs LOOSE by up to a cent, which is the first place in Phase 2 where a tolerance does. It is accepted on the same grounds the breach disjuncts already use `AG_PNL_EPSILON` for money comparisons, and it is named here so a later session weighs it instead of rediscovering it.

VECTORS, section K, eight new checks taking the set from 76 to 84. `k1` reloads a floor that was seeded from the live arithmetic exactly as the guardian computes it, `AgLimitCurrency(2133.13, 5.0, 0.0)`, rather than from a round number, because a round number does not exercise the bug. `k2`, `k2b` and `k2c` are the row the ruling asked for: one pass at the same live limit immediately after the reload, with no branch permitted to fire. The two assertions are chosen so they cannot false pass. A `g_ag_last_ratchet_warn` still at zero proves the HOLD branch was not taken, because that branch always warns when the stamp is zero; a bit identical `g_ag_floor_currency` proves the LOWER branch was not taken, because that branch is the only writer of the global. `k3` and `k4` then do the same thing WITHOUT depending on how any particular value rounds, by placing the floor half a cent above and half a cent below the live limit, which makes the row deterministic on any platform. `k4`'s direction is the mirror hazard the finding named and it is now covered: under the old exact comparison a floor a hair ABOVE the live limit selected the LOWER branch, and that branch calls `AgFloorSave`, so the guardian would have rewritten the file on every pass forever. `k5` and `k6` guard the fix from being a blanket mute, at two cents, the smallest move that clears a one cent band: a two cent raise must still warn and a two cent decrease must still lower the floor.

Zero trade API calls in the build, unchanged. NOT YET DONE AND NOT CLAIMED: the compile is the owner's and has not run at the time this paragraph was written, and the 84 vectors have not executed. PART 2, THE OBSERVABILITY ROW, CLOSED ON QUOTED JOURNAL. Owner performed the adapter disconnect and reconnected at about 12:13:40; everything below is measured from `20260818.log` rather than taken from that report, and it is measured against the fixed build `74D666E9` running since 11:29. The marker: NINE consecutive LIFE lines from 12:09:33 to 12:13:33, every one reading `waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions` with a `|DEGRADED|` field in the numbers block, and the first LIFE line after the exit, 12:14:03, back to `waiting_on=-` with no marker. This is the first time the RESYNC lines, the DEGRADED marker and the market closed distinction have been observed at all: the ISSUES entry of this date recorded them as a class no synthetic fixture can produce, closable only by a deploy and a deliberate disconnect, and that is what just happened.

THE SERVER CLOCK FROZE FOR THE WHOLE WINDOW AND THAT IS A SECOND INDEPENDENT WITNESS TO THE SAME EVENT. Every one of the nine DEGRADED lines carries `server=2026.08.18 12:08:42` while its `local=` field advances normally from 12:09:33 to 12:13:33, and the first line after the reconnect reads `server=2026.08.18 12:14:02`. So the disconnect is visible in the log twice over, once through the guardian's own gate and once through TimeCurrent standing still, which is the same freeze behaviour the 2026-08-13 and 2026-08-15 harvests measured. Duration measured rather than reported: about 5 minutes 1 second of frozen server time, and 4 minutes 37 seconds between the RESYNC lines. The owner's four minutes is the right order and the exact figures are these.

ONE ORDERING DETAIL IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT LOOKS LIKE A GAP AND IS NOT ONE. The LIFE line at local 12:09:03 already carries the frozen `server=2026.08.18 12:08:42`, yet it reads `waiting_on=-` with NO DEGRADED marker, and the RESYNC gate arms four seconds later. So for roughly one pass the guardian was working from a quote that had already stopped advancing while still reporting itself healthy. That is the ruled behaviour rather than a miss, and it is the first live sighting of it: the stale quote FINAL of 2026-08-18 put the whole boundary on the connection state and explicitly refused to gate on quote freshness, so a pass that sees `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` true is a normal pass no matter how old the quote is. Banked here so a later session reading this window does not mistake the four second lag for a defect in the marker.

WHAT THE ROW DOES NOT PROVE, stated so the evidence is not read as more than it is. No breach decision was suppressed during the window because there was nothing to suppress: realized and floating were 0.00 on every line and the account was flat throughout. The row proves the gate arms, the marker renders and the gate releases. The suppression itself is the row that needs a breach to land inside a frozen window, which is by design and does not run tonight. Also confirmed clean across the same window and the 48 minutes around it: zero `TRANSITION` lines after the 11:29 boot, so the EA held ACTIVE and RESYNC behaved as a gate inside ACTIVE rather than as a state of its own; zero SAFE_HALT; zero ALERT; and zero EA emitted `ratchet HOLDING` lines, which extends the hotfix's proof through a disconnect and a reconnect.

OPERATING CONSTRAINTS FOR PART 3, given by the owner before the live breach and recorded because they explain what this session did NOT close as much as what it did. The executor stops and waits for an explicit go before each of four owner actions: placing manual losing trades on the demo, restarting the terminal, disconnecting the adapter, and changing any EA input. The executor also stops AFTER the lock event and waits for the owner to confirm that positions are still open, rather than inferring the position state from the journal, which is the correct discipline for ruling TWO since the journal reports the guardian's decision and not the broker's book. Four things are NOT authorized: closing or modifying any position, writing anything under the MT5 Terminal folder, merging to main, and pushing. Three of those four were already standing, RULE A of 2026-08-17 for the folder and CLAUDE.md for merge and push, and the position one is moot from the executor's side since no trade API is reachable from a dictation. A FIFTH ITEM IS NEW AND IS THE ONE A LATER READER WILL NEED: the 01:00 row is NOT to be marked DONE today, whatever the expiry window does tonight. So if that row is found open in this ledger with the mechanism apparently working, that is an owner instruction being honoured and not an omission.

WHERE MAIN STANDS, CORRECTED BY THE OWNER AND THEN RE DERIVED RATHER THAN ACCEPTED. Owner confirmed that `49ea967` on main is the ledger record of the Phase 2 merge and accepted it, so main's head is `49ea967` and NOT `3ad7ef4`. `3ad7ef4` is the merge commit itself and it is main's second most recent commit, which is why the Stage 7 instruction naming main "at `3ad7ef4`" is right about the deployed tree and one commit short of main's head. Measured this session: `git rev-parse main` returns `49ea9679d19c58bfadb9fc7e73b1b1c30bb018c4`, its subject is "ledger: record the Phase 2 merge to main", and the deploy is unaffected either way because `git diff 3ad7ef4 HEAD -- MQL5` was empty when the copies were dictated, so the two commits carry the same source tree and differ only in LEDGER.md.

THE ANTICIPATED LEDGER.md HAND UNION IS RECORDED AS ANTICIPATED, AND THE MEASUREMENT AS OF NOW SAYS IT WOULD NOT BE NEEDED. The owner's instruction is that the Stage 7 merge will need the LEDGER.md hand union per the `ce6964d` precedent, and that is written here as the standing expectation for whoever performs that merge. What this session measured against it, in the same discipline the 2026-08-17 entry used when it recorded that an anticipated union turned out unnecessary: `git merge-base main HEAD` returns `49ea9679d19c58bfadb9fc7e73b1b1c30bb018c4`, which EQUALS main's head, so main has not moved since this branch was cut and there is no second side for a union to reconcile. On today's refs the merge would therefore resolve LEDGER.md with no hand work at all. THE EXPECTATION IS NOT DISCHARGED BY THAT and is deliberately left standing, because it becomes correct the moment main moves, which is exactly what happens if the owner commits to main independently before the Stage 7 branch merges, and because the merge is not being performed now. Re derive the merge base at merge time rather than trusting either this paragraph or the instruction; that is the whole lesson of the `ce6964d` precedent and of the entry that found it unnecessary.

NOT DONE, AND NOT DONE DELIBERATELY: no merge and no push. The owner's instruction on both is explicit, it agrees with CLAUDE.md, and per the FINAL remote state ruling of 2026-08-04 this entry makes no claim about where the remote stands. Branch `worktree-phase2-stage7` sits at `82e1fba` with a clean tree, carrying the Stage 7 ledger record, the ratchet epsilon hotfix and the banked journals, unmerged.

PART 3, THE LIVE BREACH. A LOCK WAS DECLARED ON THE REAL ACCOUNT AT 13:02:42 AND THE ROW AS SPECIFIED DID NOT CLOSE. Both halves of that sentence are the entry. THE SEQUENCE, quoted, because the interleaving is the whole story. At 13:02:33 the M1 session was at `realized=574.00|floating=-368.00|pnl_vs_limit=206.00 vs -106.66`, still in profit. At 13:02:37 the ACTIVE breach tail detected the breach and refused to act on it yet: `AG|2026.08.18 13:02:37|WARN|breach deferred one pass: no new deal visible, count=21`. That is the one deferral mechanism of the 2026-08-18 DECISIONS entry behaving exactly as ruled. At 13:02:39, two seconds later, the owner changed the chart period again and the session was torn down mid deferral: `INFO|deinit|reason=3|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=5586`. THE SECOND PASS NEVER RAN, so the declaration the deferral was waiting to make never happened on that path. Three seconds after that the replacement session reached the SYNCING exit and caught the same breach independently: `AG|2026.08.18 13:02:42|INFO|boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=0|realized=574.00|floating=-920.50|running_min=-7.00|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, then `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED|boot derivation: DAILY_BREACH|locked_until=2026.08.19 01:00:00`.

THE ARITHMETIC IS CHECKED RATHER THAN QUOTED. `realized=574.00` plus `floating=-920.50` is `-346.50`, which clears `-106.66` with room. `live=1` and `replay=0` is the correct pair and not an inconsistency: the replay folds CLOSED deals and its `running_min=-7.00` never approached the limit, while the entire loss sat in open positions, so the live disjunct is the only one that could fire. `tier=floor` because no state file existed at that moment, so the cascade skipped tier 1 and took the ratcheted floor, which equals the live limit today. `locked_until=1787101200` is 2026-08-19 01:00:00, exactly one day anchor forward, which is `AgNextDayAnchor` and nothing else.

COUNTS, ALL EXACT AND ALL FROM THE FILE: one `SYNCING->LOCKED`, zero `ACTIVE->LOCKED`, one `|ALERT|` event, one `Alert:` popup which is that same event surfacing to the terminal rather than a second declaration, one `boot witness` line, one `breach deferred` line. LIFE lines from 13:03:03 read `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until`.

THE OLDEST OPEN ITEM IN THIS LEDGER CLOSED BY ACCIDENT AND IT IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THIS ENTRY. The three boot witnesses have been unmeasurable since Stage 4 and have kept an ISSUES entry open through five stages, on the argument that each needs a real state file, a real GV or real broker deals that no synthetic fixture can produce. THE DERIVED HISTORY WITNESS, witness 3, HAS NOW FIRED ON A REAL BREACH with its arithmetic logged. It was not produced by the test plan: it was produced by the owner changing the chart period at the exact moment the ACTIVE path had deferred, which is a two second window nobody could have scheduled. The defense in depth argument that justified building three witnesses is no longer an argument, it is an observation: the primary path was interrupted mid decision and the account was still locked five seconds later.

A DEFECT IN WHAT THE LOCK PERSISTED, found by reading the file rather than the log. `state_1200252169.dat`, 79 bytes, mtime 13:02:42, quoted whole: `AGSTATE|1|1200252169`, `L|1|1787101200|0`, `N|0.00000000|0.00000000`, `C|2087926071`. THE Q6 SNAPSHOT IS ZEROS AND THE BREACH TIMESTAMP IS ZERO. Cause, from source at `AccountGuardian.mq5:393`: `AgEnterLockFromBoot` calls `AgStateSetBreach(until, g_ag_state_breach_time, g_ag_state_limit_snap, g_ag_state_base_snap)`, passing the EXISTING model values, which is right when the FILE witness fires and an earlier snapshot must be preserved, and wrong when the DERIVED witness fires with no file at all, because the model is then default constructed and the derivation's own freshly computed limit and base are discarded rather than recorded. SEVERITY, both halves stated. Not an enforcement hole today: the file witness re-fires on reason plus an unexpired `locked_until` and never consults the snapshot, so the lock survives restarts, and tier 2 supplies the same 106.66 on this account. It IS a permanent loss of record: the breach's timestamp is gone, and `have_snapshot`, which tests `g_ag_state_limit_snap > 0.0`, will be false on every future boot for this lock, so the tier 1 protection Q6 exists to give is absent for every boot derived lock rather than for this one only. NOT FIXED, and deliberately: the build under acceptance is the one that just locked a live account, and the executor does not change it mid row.

RULING TWO IS NOT VERIFIED AND IS DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN AT THIS POINT. The ALERT text asserts that open positions stay open, and the journal cannot corroborate that, because it reports the guardian's decision and not the broker's book. Per the owner's operating constraints the executor stops here and waits for the owner to confirm the position state by hand. The 01:00 expiry row is likewise NOT marked done, per the same constraints, even though `locked_until` is now set and observable.

RULING TWO HOLDS, AND THE EXECUTOR IS NOT THE WITNESS FOR IT. Owner reported that thirteen XAUUSD sell positions of 0.5 lot each were open at 13:02, corrected from an earlier count of four, that the broker force closed all of them on margin, and that the guardian closed nothing. What the executor can corroborate from artifacts, read only: the terminal journal records thirteen closing buy deals of 0.5 XAUUSD.ecn, tickets `#279906117` through `#279906181`, ALL at price 4397.35 and all inside 65 milliseconds at 13:38:07, which is consistent with a forced liquidation; and the broker's own resynchronisation line after the later relaunch reads `terminal synchronized with Just Global Markets Ltd.: 0 positions, 0 orders`. WHAT THE ARTIFACTS DO NOT SAY IS ALSO RECORDED: a grep of the terminal journal for stop out, stopout and margin returns ZERO hits, so the CLOSE REASON is nowhere in any log. The broker's reason is the owner's account and is not corroborated. NO ORDER WAS ISSUED BY THE EA and that rests on three independent artifacts, the strongest first: the build contains zero trade API calls, re-verified this session by the tree wide grep that returns only the `Sweep.mqh` header comment; the EA log carries no order or trade line of any kind after the lock, only LIFE lines; and the thirteen deals carry no expert attribution in the terminal journal.

AN OBSERVABILITY GAP FOUND WHILE ANSWERING THE OWNER'S QUESTION, recorded because it cost real evidence today. LOCKED-state LIFE lines carry NO numbers field: they print state, seconds_in_state and waiting_on and nothing else. Through the entire liquidation of thirteen positions at 13:38:07 the guardian's log said `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry` and not one figure. The owner asked for balance and equity at that moment and the executor cannot supply either from any readable artifact, because no artifact carries them. That is not a defect in enforcement and it is a hole in the record exactly where the record matters most.

HOW A `DEAL_TYPE_BALANCE` DEAL IS TREATED, answered from source at the owner's request and quoted rather than summarised, because a deposit was contemplated. IN THE DAILY SUM IT IS EXCLUDED, `Pnl.mqh:109`, the F12 whitelist: `if(type != DEAL_TYPE_BUY && type != DEAL_TYPE_SELL) continue;`, so a balance deal contributes nothing to `cumulative` and nothing to `running_min`, and a deposit does not reduce the day's realized loss. IN THE DAY BASE IT IS NEUTRAL BY CONSTRUCTION, `AgDayBase` returning `AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_BALANCE) - all_deals` where `AgDealsSumAll` counts all deal types: a deposit raises Balance and raises the all deals sum by the same amount, so Base is unchanged, which is the Q2 identity the function's own comment states. CONSEQUENCE, stated as fact and with no recommendation either way, per the owner's instruction: base and limit are unmoved by a deposit, the day's realized loss stays fully counted, and the lock holds to its expiry regardless. One thing DOES change: at 13:02:42 the derived witness fired with `replay=0` because the loss was floating, and after the liquidation made it realized the same witness fires with `replay=1` from broker history alone, independently of the state file and the GV mirror.

P2-B CLOSES AND TWO OF THE THREE BOOT WITNESSES FIRED ON ONE BOOT. Owner killed and relaunched the terminal while locked. Quoted: `INFO|lock state file loaded|reason=DAILY_BREACH|locked_until=2026.08.19 01:00:00|weighed by the boot derivation at the SYNCING exit`, `TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING|boot|weekly=on|timer=1s`, then at 13:46:44 `INFO|boot witness FILE fired|reason=DAILY_BREACH|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00` and `INFO|boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, then `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED` and LIFE lines at `state=LOCKED`. THE FILE WITNESS HAS NOW FIRED FOR THE FIRST TIME, joining the derived witness from 13:02:42, so two of the three paths the Stage 4 entry called unmeasurable are measured on artifacts. The `BOOT->SYNCING` label also settles a question this session raised: the `ACTIVE->SYNCING` lines seen earlier belong to the chart change path and not to a cold start, and a cold start does print `BOOT`.

THE `replay=1` FIRING WAS PREDICTED IN ADVANCE AND THEN MEASURED, which is why it is banked as a measurement and not as an expectation. Before P2-B ran, the executor stated that the derived witness would fire with `replay=1` this time, on the reasoning that the liquidation had converted a floating loss into a realized one and `running_min` folds realized deals only. The 13:02:42 firing had `live=1|replay=0|running_min=-7.00`; the 13:46:44 firing has `live=1|replay=1|running_min=-1893.50`. The owner later asked for this to be recorded as an expectation attached to the P2-B row; it is recorded as the stronger thing, a prediction made before the event and confirmed by the artifact after it. THE SIGNIFICANCE IS NOT THE FLAG BUT WHAT IT MAKES INDEPENDENT: with `replay=1` the lock is now derivable from broker deal history ALONE, so deleting both the state file and the GV mirror would not unlock the day.

THE KILL WAS NOT A KILL, AND THE EXECUTOR'S OWN PREDICTION IS WHAT CAUGHT IT. Before the row ran the executor predicted the crash-loop chain would advance past `1/3` and stated in advance that a reading of `1/3` would need explaining rather than shrugging at. It read `chain=1/3`. The halt file gives the reason: the killed session is recorded `S|1787058159|1`, MARKED CLEAN. Task Manager's End task sends a close message to a GUI application first, so MT5 shut down gracefully and `OnDeinit` ran. So P2-B proves the lock survives a RESTART and the crash path remains untested; a genuine test needs a forced termination. A SECOND RESULT FALLS OUT OF THE SAME EVIDENCE AND IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIRST: the journal has NO `deinit` line for that session, because the log buffer died with the process, while the halt file already carried the clean marker, having been flushed by the atomic write. The halt file's record survives a shutdown that the journal does not.

A FACTUAL CORRECTION AGAINST THE OWNER'S REPORT, derived from the log rather than asserted. The owner reported the demo balance as 0. The 13:46:44 witness line prints `tier=floor`, and that label is emitted only when `limit_cmp < live_limit`. Since `limit_cmp` is the ratchet floor at 106.6565, the live limit must EXCEED 106.6565, so `base > 2133.13`. With `base = ACCOUNT_BALANCE - all_deals` and the day's deals summing to `-1893.50` with no balance deals, ACCOUNT_BALANCE is above roughly 239.63. Had the balance truly been 0, base would be 1893.50, the live limit 94.68, and the line would have read `limit_cmp=94.68|tier=live`. It does not. So the liquidation left a residue. No exact figure is claimed here, only the bound the artifact forces, and the owner should read the terminal rather than take this number.

P2-C CLOSES, DISCONNECT AND RECONNECT WHILE LOCKED, and the executor's published prediction was WRONG ON ONE OF FIVE POINTS. The prediction was written and given to the owner BEFORE the row ran, which is the only reason the error is visible at all, and it is recorded here in full rather than quietly corrected. Correct: zero `RESYNC entered` and zero `RESYNC exited` lines; zero TRANSITION lines; zero ALERT lines; no state file write, `state_1200252169.dat` still at mtime 13:46:44 and md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19` throughout; the numbers field absent on every line, since `AgPnlNumbersString` returns empty whenever the state is not ACTIVE; and the server clock frozen while local advanced. WRONG: the executor predicted ZERO DEGRADED markers and there are ELEVEN, on LOCKED LIFE lines from 14:59:42 to 15:04:42.

WHY THE PREDICTION FAILED, stated as a reading error rather than as a surprise. The executor traced `g_ag_degraded` and `g_ag_resyncing`, found both set in exactly one place, the `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` guard at the head of `AgEvaluateActive`, correctly concluded that neither can be set while LOCKED, and stopped there. It missed `AgObservabilityNote()` at `AccountGuardian.mq5:500`, a Stage 6 addition that returns `"DEGRADED: disconnected"` from a SEPARATE global, `g_ag_obs_connected`, which the timer samples every tick independently of the state machine. The comment at lines 102 to 110 states that these globals are kept deliberately separate from `g_ag_degraded` "precisely so the two cannot be confused"; the executor read that comment and drew the opposite conclusion from it.

THE ERROR PROPAGATED INTO A WRONG FINDING BEFORE IT WAS CAUGHT, which is the part worth recording. On the strength of the wrong prediction the executor told the owner that a disconnect while locked would be "almost invisible" and that this WIDENED the LOCKED observability issue opened earlier this session. That was wrong and is withdrawn: Stage 6 covers connection state under a lock perfectly well. The observability issue stands for the NUMBERS and only the numbers, which is what it says, so no entry needs correcting. It is recorded because the wrong conclusion was one sentence away from being written into that issue.

WHAT THE ROW ACTUALLY PROVES, and it is a design claim holding up rather than a null result. The two degraded channels are distinguishable in the artifact, exactly as the Stage 6 comment claims. ACTIVE emits `waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions`, from the Q10 gate at line 532, as seen at 12:09. LOCKED emits `waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until` plus a separate `DEGRADED: disconnected` field, from the Stage 6 note at line 502, as seen now. Different strings from different sites, so a reader can always tell which fired. The absence of RESYNC under a lock is likewise correct rather than missing: the gate exists to hold the first post-disconnect BREACH DECISION until history is stable, and a locked guardian takes no breach decisions. Window measured from the frozen clock: 14:58:54 to 15:05:12, six minutes eighteen seconds. THE ONE PASS LAG APPEARS FOR THE THIRD TIME: at 14:59:12 the server stamp had already frozen at 14:58:54 while no marker was present yet, the same shape as 12:09:03 and for the same ruled reason.

A GAP DERIVED FROM THIS ROW AND EXPLICITLY NOT OBSERVED, recorded as a reading rather than as a finding, and deliberately NOT opened as an ISSUES row without the owner. Because `g_ag_resyncing` is set only inside `AgEvaluateActive`, a disconnect that begins AND ends inside a locked window arms no resync gate at all. Lock expiry then runs `AgTransition(AG_STATE_ACTIVE, "lock expired", "")`, entering ACTIVE directly rather than through SYNCING, and the coherence gate is `if(g_ag_resyncing)`, so with the flag false the first post expiry pass performs no stability check. For this to matter the disconnect must STRADDLE the expiry boundary: reconnect after `locked_until`, the clock jumps past it, expiry fires within a tick or two, and ACTIVE is entered while history may still be synchronising. That is the nightly break and weekend freeze shape this ledger already documents at length. It did not happen tonight and could not have: the reconnect was 15:05 and expiry is 01:00, about ten hours apart.

P2-H DID NOT CLOSE, AND THE REASON WAS PUBLISHED BEFORE THE ROW RAN. Owner raised `DailyLossPercent` from 5 to 10 while locked. There are ZERO `input changed while LOCKED` lines in the journal. The row's claim, that a limit raised while locked produces a loud WARN while the snapshot holds, is therefore UNTESTED rather than passed or failed. The cause is the one the executor put in writing before the owner touched the input: MT5 reloads the EA on an input change, `INFO|deinit|reason=5`, and `AgEnterLockFromBoot` reseeds the Q6 input witness from the LIVE inputs at `AccountGuardian.mq5:386` to `:388`, so the witness captured percent 10 as the locked-in value and now agrees with the input. Nothing remains to warn about. The executor's other published prediction for this row, that the WARN would read `limit=0.00 base=0.00` and expose the empty snapshot in operator-facing text, is likewise untested since the WARN never fired. WHAT THE ROW DID PROVE, and it is not nothing: the lock held across the input change without a flicker, `locked_until` unchanged at 2026-08-19 01:00:00, the state file rewritten byte identical at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19`, no transition out of LOCKED, and LIFE lines resuming at `state=LOCKED` with `seconds_in_state` restarting from 15, which is the reload and not a state change. HOW TO ACTUALLY TEST IT, named and not performed: the WARN needs a LOCKED evaluation pass that observes an input differing from the captured witness, which an input change through the dialog can never produce because the reload always reseeds first. That is a row that may not be reachable through the terminal UI at all, and it is left for the owner rather than worked around. OWNER INSTRUCTED 2026-08-18: leave the row UNTESTED and do not manufacture a substitute test, since a substitute would prove something else while carrying this row's name.

THE INPUT CHANGE WAS ERASED A SECOND TIME, BY THE CRASH, AND NO ARTIFACT ANYWHERE NOW RECORDS THAT IT HAPPENED. Owner observed after the forced kills that `DailyLossPercent` reads 5 on the chart, having set it to 10 at 15:36 and never restored it, and asked what the EA actually booted with. ANSWERED FROM ARTIFACTS, with the negative results stated first. B1, the 16:00:46 boot, left ZERO journal lines, so nothing whatever can be said about its inputs. B2, the 16:01:24 boot, is no better served directly: the EA logs no input value at any point, verified by grep over `AccountGuardian.mq5`, where `DailyLossPercent` appears only as the input declaration at line 19 and inside arithmetic and comparisons. The current LIFE lines imply nothing either, since LOCKED lines carry no numbers, which is the second time in one session that the observability gap has blocked a direct answer. AND THE OBVIOUS CHECK FAILS: the `tier=floor` label cannot discriminate, because with base above 2133.13 both five percent, about 106.7, and ten percent, about 213, exceed the 106.6565 floor, so both print `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor` identically.
THE DECISIVE ARTIFACT IS THE CHART STATE FILE, read only: `MQL5\Profiles\Charts\Default\chart01.chr`, mtime 2026-08-18 13:46:03, containing under its `<expert>` block `DailyLossPercent=5.0` and `DailyLossCurrency=0.0`. That mtime PREDATES the 15:36 input change by nearly two hours. MT5 persists chart state, EA inputs included, on clean shutdown, and `taskkill /F` denied it that chance on both kills, so the file still holds the pre change value and the relaunch restored 5 from it. Corroborated independently by the Q6 witness itself: `AgEnterLockFromBoot` captures the live input at boot and `AgEvaluateLocked` warns on any divergence, and with ZERO `input changed while LOCKED` lines all day the live input has equalled the boot captured value continuously since 16:01:24, which with a chart reading 5 means it booted at 5. Restored from the PROFILE's chart state file, not from a template; `Default.tpl` is dated 2026-08-03 and is not implicated.
SO THE OPERATOR ACTION WAS ERASED TWICE OVER, by two independent mechanisms, and this is the finding rather than the input value. Once from the guardian's own witness, by the reseed on reload at 15:36. Once from the terminal's persisted chart state, by a process kill that prevented the save. There is now NO artifact anywhere, in the journal, in the state file, or in the chart, recording that `DailyLossPercent` was ever set to 10 on a locked account. It survives only in the owner's memory and in this ledger. Enforcement never moved and was never at risk; what was lost is the record.

THE CONSEQUENCE THE OWNER ASKED TO BE RECORDED ON THIS ROW, and it is larger than the row itself. THE RESEED ON RELOAD ERASES THE RECORD OF AN INPUT CHANGE MADE WHILE LOCKED. `AgEnterLockFromBoot` sets `g_ag_locked_in_percent` and `g_ag_locked_in_currency` from the LIVE inputs on every boot, so after any reload the witness matches whatever the inputs now say, and the guardian can no longer tell that they ever differed. An operator who raises a limit during a locked window leaves NO trace of having done so in the journal: no WARN at the time, because the reload preempts the first comparing pass, and none afterwards, because the witness has been re-seeded to agree. Today's run is the demonstration: `DailyLossPercent` went 5 to 10 at 15:36 on a locked account and the journal contains zero `input changed while LOCKED` lines. Enforcement did not move and that is not the point; the audit trail did not record an operator action on a locked account. OWNER INSTRUCTED 2026-08-18: the reseed question JOINS THE NEXT BUILD AS DESIGN ITEM 5, classed as a DESIGN ITEM AND NOT AS A DEFECT. That classification is the owner's and is recorded as given: the four items before it are defects with a wrong behaviour to correct, while this one is a deliberate behaviour whose correctness is open. WHETHER THE RESEED IS CORRECT BEHAVIOUR IS A QUESTION FOR THE NEXT BUILD AND IS NOT RULED HERE. The argument for it is on the record already, in the Stage 3 note the reseed was written to answer: without it the first LOCKED pass compares live inputs against a default-constructed 0.0 and warns about a change nobody made. The argument against it is this paragraph. Both are recorded and neither is decided.

ALL THREE BOOT WITNESSES HAVE NOW FIRED, AND THE STAGE 4 ENTRY THAT WAITED FIVE STAGES FOR THIS IS ANSWERED. On the 15:36:27 boot, in one block: `boot witness FILE fired|reason=DAILY_BREACH|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, `boot witness GV fired|raw=2026.08.19 01:00:00|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`, and `boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor|bounded=2026.08.19 01:00:00`. The GV path had never fired before today. Counting the whole day: DERIVED fired at 13:02:42 with `replay=0` and again at 13:46:44 and 15:36:27 with `replay=1`, FILE fired at 13:46:44 and 15:36:27, GV fired ONCE, at 15:36:27 only. THE SINGLE GV FIRING IS NOT A COINCIDENCE AND IS THE SUBJECT OF ITS OWN ISSUES ROW opened this session: the mirror is overwritten with zero by `OnTimer` on every cold boot before the witness reads it, so it can only fire on a reload that preserved memory, which is what 15:36:26 was and what 13:46:41 was not.

THE CRASH PATH CLOSES ON TWO FORCED KILLS, and the executor's published table was right on every row it named and incomplete on one it did not. Owner ran `taskkill /F /IM terminal64.exe` twice, relaunching after each, with the two inits deliberately placed inside the 300 second window so the crash-loop chain could advance. Measured init timestamps from the halt file rather than from the owner's report, which said 15:59 and 16:01: B1 is `1787068846`, 16:00:46, and B2 is `1787068884`, 16:01:24, a gap of THIRTY EIGHT seconds. THE HALT FILE IS THE PROOF THE KILLS WERE HARD and it is quoted whole at the tail: `S|1787060801|1`, the 13:46:41 session that Task Manager's End task shut down gracefully, then `S|1787067386|0`, `S|1787068846|0`, `S|1787068884|0`, three consecutive UNCLEAN entries. The contrast between the `|1` and the three `|0` in one file is the discriminator the P2-B entry said was needed and could not supply.

B2 VERIFIED AGAINST THE PREDICTION, LINE BY LINE: `DEBUG|crash-loop check|chain=2/3 consecutive unclean, gap bound 300s`, the chain advancing for the first time in the project's history; `TRANSITION|BOOT->SYNCING`, a true cold start; `boot witness FILE fired`; `boot witness DERIVED fired|live=1|replay=1|realized=-1893.50|floating=0.00|running_min=-1893.50|limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor`; `TRANSITION|SYNCING->LOCKED`; one ALERT; and `state_1200252169.dat` rewritten byte identical at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19` for the fourth boot running. `limit_cmp=106.66|tier=floor` was predicted specifically and holds even though `DailyLossPercent` is now 10: the live limit is roughly 213 and the ratchet floor at 106.6565 is still the stricter value, so the floor governs.

THE GV WITNESS DID NOT FIRE ON THIS COLD BOOT EITHER, WHICH IS THE SECOND INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION AND TURNS THE DEFECT FROM AN OBSERVATION INTO A PATTERN. Count for the whole day: `boot witness GV fired` appears exactly ONCE, at 15:36:27, the parameter reload that preserved memory. It is absent from the 13:46:41 cold boot and absent from the 16:01:24 cold boot, both of which reached LOCKED on FILE and DERIVED alone. Two cold boots, two non firings, one warm reload, one firing: the self erase mechanism recorded in its own ISSUES row predicts exactly that split and nothing else explains it.

A RESULT NEITHER THE OWNER NOR THE EXECUTOR PUT ON THE LIST, and it is a clean pass on a path that had never run live. `AG|2026.08.18 16:01:24|WARN|stale mutex heartbeat (11s), taking over crashed-instance mutex`. The killed instance died holding the mutex and never released it, and the F8 stale heartbeat takeover did precisely what it exists for, measured at an 11 second staleness against the 10 second threshold. One occurrence in the whole day's log, and it took a genuine process kill to produce it; every earlier restart this session was graceful enough to release the mutex cleanly.

B1 LEFT NO JOURNAL LINES AT ALL, AND THAT CORRECTS SOMETHING THE EXECUTOR WROTE EARLIER THIS SESSION. There are ZERO EA lines between 16:00:00 and 16:01:20; the log runs from `15:59:42` straight to the B2 init. B1 lived 38 seconds and lost its entire boot block, `chain` line included. Before the flush the executor told the owner that a hard kill "loses only seconds of journal, not minutes" and that this SOFTENED the P2-B finding about the halt file outlasting the journal. THAT WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN: it generalised from the 15:36:26 session, which had lived 23 minutes and had therefore already flushed, to a case with no flush at all. The P2-B finding stands undiminished. B1 IS THEREFORE ATTESTED BY TWO ARTIFACTS AND ZERO LOG LINES: its halt file entry `S|1787068846|0`, and B2's own `chain=2/3`, which could only reach 2 by walking back onto an unclean B1 entry inside the 300 second window. The chain arithmetic is itself evidence of the session that left no trace. B1's own `chain=1/3` line is UNVERIFIABLE and is recorded as such rather than assumed from the arithmetic that says it must have read that way.

THE 2133 DEPOSIT IS INVISIBLE IN EVERY ARTIFACT THE EXECUTOR CAN READ, AND THAT IS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AT ONCE, ONE A GAP AND ONE THE DESIGN WORKING. Owner deposited 2133 on the demo at about 16:52 while the account was LOCKED, having asked in advance to be told what would be visible. THE LIFE LINES DID NOT MOVE, as predicted before the deposit: sixteen lines from 16:44:25 to 16:51:55 are identical in every field except `seconds_in_state` and the two clocks, `state=LOCKED|waiting_on=expiry: TimeCurrent >= locked_until`, with no numbers block at all. The state file stayed at md5 `1CF59DF8CFA804E636576DAB4ECB2E19`, the halt file at `18A82E7E498B038D7A80295B62D4985F`, the floor file untouched since 02:00:00. NO CORROBORATION EXISTS: the terminal journal ends at 16:01:24 and records no balance deal, so the deposit rests on the owner's report alone and the executor states that rather than implying otherwise.
THE TWO CAUSES MUST NOT BE CONFLATED AND ARE SEPARATED HERE. That the LIFE lines carry no numbers under a lock is the OPEN observability gap, and this is the fourth time in one session it has blocked a direct reading. That the deposit will never appear in the guardian's numbers, not even after expiry returns it to ACTIVE, is the DESIGN WORKING AS RULED and is not a gap at all: the F12 whitelist excludes balance deals from realized, and the Q2 day base identity makes them neutral, verified on tonight's own figures, `base = B + 1893.50` before and `base = (B + 2133) - 239.50 = B + 1893.50` after. The only quantity a deposit moves is `ACCOUNT_BALANCE`, which the guardian logs in no state. So the guardian is BUILT blind to deposits WITHIN THE DAY THEY LAND, deliberately.
CORRECTED 2026-08-19 AT THE EXPIRY, AND THE ORIGINAL WORDING WAS WRONG. This paragraph first said that `base` and `limit` after 01:00 "should read as though nothing was deposited". THAT IS FALSE AND IS WITHDRAWN. The Q2 neutrality holds only inside the day the deposit lands, because `all_deals` counts it there and cancels it out of the identity. AFTER THE ROLLOVER THE NEW ANCHOR EXCLUDES YESTERDAY'S DEALS ENTIRELY, so `all_deals` returns to about zero and `base` collapses to `ACCOUNT_BALANCE` itself, deposit included. Measured at 01:00:31: `base=2133.00`, which IS the account balance and not a coincidence. So a deposit is invisible for the remainder of its own day and fully visible from the next anchor onward. The executor wrote the wrong half of that before the event and is recording the correction here rather than editing the claim away.

STAGE 5, THE RATCHET, exactly the five ratified elements and no more. Single scalar `floor_currency` persisted as `floor_<login>.dat` through `AgAtomicWrite`, carrying the same never loaded guard and the same free name quarantine the state file family uses. It SEEDS on the first completed ACTIVE computation, LOWERS on a decrease as a running minimum, HOLDS on an increase with a cadence capped WARN naming both the live value and the floor, RESEEDS AT ROLLOVER, and does NOT RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP. The ratcheted quantity is the DERIVED `limit_currency` and never the raw inputs, which is the correction the 2026-07-30 addendum made and the ruling ratified: under Q8's min of enabled a leg that is not binding can be disabled with zero effect on the enforced limit, and an input level ratchet blocks that as a false positive on the owner's own worked case.

`min(limit, floor)` IS PRE BREACH ONLY and the code makes that structural rather than careful. It is applied in the live comparison, called from the breach tail and from nowhere else, and as TIER 2 of the boot cascade in the order the instruction named, snapshot then floor then live. Q6's snapshot governs the locked window unconditionally and the floor never touches it, the two being disjoint by construction since the ratchet governs pre breach ACTIVE while the snapshot governs post breach LOCKED. ONE CONSEQUENCE WORTH STATING because getting it wrong would be silent: the snapshot taken at breach is the ENFORCED limit, not the raw live one, so it is automatically the floor at that instant exactly as the 2026-07-30 addendum predicted, and snapshotting the live value instead would have loosened the locked window by precisely the amount the ratchet had been holding back.

TWO DESIGN CALLS INSIDE STAGE 5 THAT THE RULING DID NOT SPELL OUT, both recorded rather than left in the code to be discovered. FIRST, a backward step HOLDS the floor and KEEPS ENFORCING IT. The alternative, treating a floor whose anchor is later than the window as stale, would let one clock rewind drop the floor in a single act, which is the same one step disarm the negative gap ruling of 2026-08-04 refused in the crash loop chain; holding is also the strict direction. SECOND, a corrupt floor file is quarantined and reset but LOCKS NOTHING, unlike a corrupt state file which locks via CORRUPT_STATE. The floor is a pre breach tightening and not lock state, so losing it errs toward the live limit, and the residual that follows, that destroying the floor file resets it on the next seed, is the one the 2026-07-30 ruling already accepted on the record when it ordered the ratchet built.

`AgRatchetUpdate` was placed in `Persist.mqh` beside the floor family rather than in the EA. That is the owner's ruling C of this date applied to a second case rather than a new decision: a script cannot include an EA, so while the function sat there the six vectors question SEVEN calls for could not be written at all. The WARN cadence arrives as a parameter rather than by relocating the EA's `AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, which keeps Phase 1's structure untouched.

DEGRADED MARKER ON SYNCING AND LOCKED LIFE LINES while `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` is false, closing the measured 2026-08-14 gap where seven SYNCING lines emitted entirely offline were indistinguishable from healthy ones to any reader; ACTIVE already carries Q10's own marker and is deliberately not double marked. THE RULINGS APPLIED. ITEM A, `AG_QUOTE_FROZEN_SECONDS = 120` ratified: the UNRULED marker is gone from the source and the grounds now sit at the constant, FINAL in DECISIONS of this date, ISSUES entry closed. STAGE 4, THE BOOT DERIVATION. `AgBootDerivation` runs at the SYNCING exit and ORs three witnesses with STRICTEST WINNING on duration. FILE witness fires on `locked_until > now` rather than on the reason field, so an expired lock's stale record reads as not-locked and needs no clear-on-unlock rewrite. GV witness carries a bare timestamp and therefore defaults to DAILY_BREACH, a GV having no CORRUPT_STATE concept to express. DERIVED witness replays broker history and is the AUTHORITY, precisely because a local machine attacker cannot forge or delete server side history while the file and the GV are both locally writable and are therefore accelerators rather than authority. CORRUPT_STATE takes the reason on a tie, because a DAILY_BREACH label would hide the one thing that finding says, that the record cannot be trusted. THE OR ONLY ADDS A LOCK: no witness unlocks, unlock remains exclusively the `TimeCurrent >= locked_until` comparison, and an unevaluable replay returns a third code that holds the EA in SYNCING rather than licensing an unlock, which is the F6 obligation made structural rather than advisory.

ONE SHARED REPLAY, which is what the instruction asked for and is worth stating precisely. `AgRealizedFold` folds the whitelisted deals once and returns BOTH the realized sum and the running minimum, so the F12 whitelist and the Q3 per deal formula exist in exactly one place and cannot drift between the live path and the replay. `AgRealized` is now that same fold with the minimum discarded. The refactor is behaviour preserving BY CONSTRUCTION and the argument is stated rather than assumed: sorting cannot change a sum, so every Phase 1 figure this function has produced is reproduced exactly. The fold sorts `(DEAL_TIME, DEAL_TICKET)` ascending per design item 1's tiebreak, and the reason it must is that a running minimum is order sensitive where a sum is not, MQL5 does not document `HistoryDealGetTicket`'s order, and an intra second dip is exactly the case where ordering decides whether the dip is visible at all. The insertion sort is deliberate and bounded: this runs at boot only, never per tick, which is the design's own scope note about not replaying the whole history every second.

THE GV MIRROR is now written from memory on every tick beside the mutex heartbeat, unconditionally, which is what makes it self healing: a GV cleared through the terminal's own Global Variables window while the EA is alive is restored within one tick. The state FILE is deliberately NOT rewritten per tick and stays event triggered, at breach, at expiry and at corruption handling, matching the halt file's pattern.

VECTORS EXTENDED BY 14 TO 56, covering exactly what ruling C unblocked and nothing more: Q1's base, ruling THREE's extra day including the measured 00:58 pre anchor case where the unfrozen path locks for 120 seconds and the frozen path locks for more than a day, ruling FOUR's floor and its no-op when the computed value already clears it, the witness path's clamp, and the rewound clock geometry where the latch floor sits ABOVE the clamp ceiling and must win. That last case is constructed by moving the latch rather than the clock, which reproduces the exact geometry deterministically without needing a machine whose clock has been stepped backward. Vector `i14` asserts the domain split head on by proving the computed path is never clamped, which is the one way the two domains could silently collapse into each other.
