WHAT CHANGED. The breach tail of `AgEvaluateActive` no longer shouts and stays ACTIVE; it declares a lock. `AgDeclareLock` snapshots limit and base into the state file per Q6, computes `locked_until`, transitions to LOCKED and raises an ALERT whose text says in terms that no order is sent and open positions stay open, so the operator is not left expecting a flatten that ruling TWO explicitly defers to Phase 3. The LOCKED case in `OnTimer`, an empty block since Phase 1, now calls `AgEvaluateLocked`.

THE BOUNDS, all three rulings of this date in one function so the composition order is stated once and cannot drift. Q1 is the base, next day anchor from the breach instant. RULING THREE takes the anchor AFTER the imminent one when the quote is frozen at the moment of breach, a full trading day, which is what stops the measured 62 minute pre anchor freeze of 2026-08-13 producing a lock that expires minutes later at the reopen. RULING FOUR floors the result at `AgNextDayAnchor` of Q8's high water latch, which never recedes, so a breach declared after a backward clock step cannot compute an already past expiry. THE 2026-07-30 CLAMP IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT from the computed path and present in the witness path, exactly as the precedence ruling of this date directs by giving each bound a domain; `AgLockedUntilFromWitness` applies clamp first as the upper bound and floor last as the lower bound, which is the mechanism by which the floor wins under a rewound clock. That witness helper is written now and is NOT REACHED in this stage, since no witness is read until Stage 4; it sits beside the computed one so the two domains cannot be re-derived differently later.

EXPIRY IS THE ONLY UNLOCK PATH and compares `TimeCurrent` per Q7. Nothing else leaves LOCKED: no input, no absence of evidence, no reconnect. A frozen or backward clock therefore delays an unlock, which errs locked and needs no special case, and the Friday breach that outlives its computed expiry by about 48 hours across the weekend freeze is that behaviour working rather than a defect.

THE FROZEN QUOTE MEASURE, and where it sits is a deliberate choice worth recording. It is a purely local tick age delta, never clock arithmetic, so it inherits nothing from the broker DST peg this ledger still has open until the October harvest. It lives in `OnTimer` and not inside `AgEvaluateActive` for two reasons: it must keep measuring in EVERY state including LOCKED, since ruling THREE reads it at a breach that may follow any state history, and putting it in the evaluation path would have broken the byte identical requirement for no gain. IT GATES NOTHING. The breach is declared from the frozen quote exactly as the stale quote ruling of this date requires, and the measure is read only afterwards to size the consequence, which is the boundary ruling THREE itself draws.

A DEFECT IN THIS STAGE, FOUND BY THE EXECUTOR BEFORE COMMIT AND FIXED, recorded because it would have been invisible until the first live breach. `g_ag_state_loaded` is set only by `AgStateLoad`, and Stage 3 as first written never called it, so `AgStateSave` would have refused on every breach under the never-loaded-never-written rule and the lock would have held in memory only, silently failing to persist. `AgStateLoad` is now called in `OnInit` and its result is DELIBERATELY NOT ACTED ON, which is the smallest correct fix: loading is what makes a later write legitimate, while acting on what the file says is Stage 4's boot derivation. A second defect was fixed the same way: the input change witness compared live inputs against values a fresh image has never set, so a Stage 4 boot restoring a lock from the file would have emitted a change WARN naming a change nobody made. It is now guarded by an explicit captured flag rather than by an accident of initialization.

Nothing has been pushed, so the file is not public yet and the reversal is fully effective as long as that stays true, but a push of the current history would publish it despite this ruling. Two ways out exist, dropping or rewriting that commit, and both rewrite history, so neither was performed on the executor's own initiative. Owner's call.

ROOT CAUSE OF THE DUPLICATION, supplied by the owner and recorded because nothing inside this repository could have shown it: the managed block in `.gitignore` was missing its sentinel first line, and the owner's profile re appends the whole block on every launch when the sentinel is absent, so each launch added another copy. Expiry arithmetic under the measured clock regimes, assembled from artifacts already on this ledger rather than measured again today, and stated here because Phase 2 is where each one starts to matter. A Friday breach sets locked_until to Saturday 01:00, but TimeCurrent freezes at Friday 23:57:59 for the whole weekend, measured most recently across the 2026-08-15 and 2026-08-16 files, so the comparison stays false until the Monday reopen steps the clock and the lock outlives its computed expiry by about 48 hours. That errs locked and is acceptance row P2-I rather than a defect. The nightly break straddle is the one that produces a NEW question: 62 continuous minutes of frozen TimeCurrent while connected were measured on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14, the freeze straddles the 01:00 anchor, and a breach evaluated inside it, now valid under today's ruling, computes locked_until as the anchor of the day that is about to end, so the lock expires minutes later at the reopen and the new day opens carrying the same losing position. Backward clock steps are the mirror hazard: a 25 hour rewind was measured 2026-08-01, and while locked a rewind only delays expiry, which errs locked, but a breach declared AFTER a rewind computes an already past locked_until, and the clamp ruling of 2026-07-30 bounds locked_until from above only. Expiry cannot fire at all while disconnected, since TimeCurrent does not advance, which errs locked by construction and needs no new rule.

2026-08-17, the Monday reopen block, and an executor error that destroyed a primary artifact. The destruction is put first because it is the most important fact in this entry and because a later session must not discover it by finding a missing file. THE EXECUTOR DELETED the source artifact 20260816.log, the whole Sunday EA journal, 1466866 bytes. Cause, stated exactly so the trap is reusable: a PowerShell helper function was named `Rd`, and `rd` is a built in AllScope ALIAS for `Remove-Item`. PowerShell resolves aliases BEFORE functions, so the helper never ran and the call `$l=Rd "$ea\20260816.log"` executed `Remove-Item` against the journal. It failed silently and returned nothing, which read as an empty file rather than as a deletion, and the deletion was only identified on the next call when the path reported as not existing. `Remove-Item` does not use the Recycle Bin. Recovery was attempted and failed: `Win32_ShadowCopy` returns an initialization failure and `Get-ComputerRestorePoint` returns access denied, both for want of elevation. The file is gone. Standing rule added, alongside the CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06 and the stale directory entry trap of 2026-08-16: NEVER name a helper with a short token, and never with one that collides with a built in alias, since `rd`, `del`, `ls`, `cp`, `mv`, `sc` and others are aliases that silently win over any function of the same name. Prefer inline code over helpers when touching evidence, and never let a read path and a destructive cmdlet share a name.

What was lost and what survived, stated precisely rather than reassuringly. Lost: the Sunday EA journal from local 11:14:03 to end of day, which is the segment containing the sleep entry, the wake, and the single DEGRADED line the owner reported. Survived, because they had already been snapshotted and committed: docs/evidence/journal-20260816-p1f-close-binding.txt, 1348 lines through 11:13:33, at commit 6c6bced, and docs/evidence/journal-20260816-weekend-freeze-sunday-partial.txt through 11:04:33 at a61373e. Survived on disk and snapshotted this session before any further work: the TERMINAL side journal for 2026-08-16, which the deletion did not touch, now at docs/evidence/terminal-20260816-fullday-sleep-window.txt. Also unaffected: Saturday's complete file and Monday's. Net effect on the record is that one specific line, the DEGRADED line at 15:51:05, can no longer be quoted from an artifact. Everything else the owner reported about the sleep is independently reconstructible and is reconstructed below.

Sleep survival number two, unplanned, VERIFIED without reference to the deleted file. Cause per the owner and not measurable from here: the machine was unplugged and Windows sleep on battery was still at its default, the Never setting from 2026-08-13 having applied to plugged in only. Two independent witnesses establish the sleep. First, the terminal journal brackets it exactly: `connection to JustMarkets-Demo3 lost` at 12:33:14.992 and `authorized on JustMarkets-Demo3` at 15:51:11.765, a gap of 11876.773 seconds, with the following line reading `previous successful authorization performed from 5.29.18.75 on 2026.08.16 05:31:49`, which proves no intervening authorization and therefore no terminal restart inside the gap. Second, and this is the load bearing one, the state counter is continuous ACROSS the sleep and can be checked from MONDAY's file alone. Every one of the 1164 LIFE lines emitted on 2026-08-17 implies the same ACTIVE start when `seconds_in_state` is subtracted from the local stamp: 2026-08-14 12:32:18, one distinct value with zero variance across the whole day. That origin reproduces both of the owner's Sunday figures to the second, 172857 seconds after it landing on 2026-08-16 12:33:15 and 184727 seconds after it landing on 2026-08-16 15:51:05. So the ACTIVE run has been unbroken since the 2026-08-14 reconnect, straight through a 3 hour 17 minute 50 second suspension, with no re init, no SAFE_HALT, no takeover and no state reset. The process itself corroborates it, PID 2032 still reporting a start time of 2026-08-14 11:55:58. The counter therefore advances on wall clock rather than on ticks or timer callbacks, which is the same mechanism the 2026-08-05 mutex heartbeat entry predicted and the 2026-08-13 sleep first showed.

One honest limit on the above, recorded rather than glossed. The owner reports a single DEGRADED line at the wake and that is almost certainly right, since the EA resumed and emitted at 15:51:05 while the network did not reauthorize until 15:51:11.765, so that line was emitted disconnected and the Q10 gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:121 must have marked it. But it CANNOT BE QUOTED, because the executor deleted the file that held it. The claim is corroborated by timing and by code, not by artifact, and no future session may cite it as a quoted line.

This sleep is a DIFFERENT CLASS from the 2026-08-13 one and the difference is worth keeping. That sleep woke into a live market and produced a Q8 anchor jump on the first pass. This one began and ended entirely inside the weekend freeze, with `TimeCurrent` pinned at 2026.08.14 23:57:59 and the anchor pinned at 2026.08.14 01:00:00, so nothing needed to be caught up at the wake and no Q8 firing occurred or should have. Sleep survival is therefore now observed in both classes, inside a freeze and across a live boundary.

The Monday reopen, and the corrected prediction lands EXACTLY. Quoted from docs/evidence/journal-20260817-monday-reopen.txt. Last frozen line at 00:59:35 reads `anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|realized=-52.84|floating=0.00|base=1985.97|limit=99.30` with `server=2026.08.14 23:57:59`. Then at 00:59:59.487, four lines at one stamp: `INFO|anchor jump accepted: previous_high=2026.08.14 01:00:00, accepted=2026.08.17 01:00:00, jump=259200s`, the matching ALERT carrying the Q8 amendment text, the terminal Alert popup, and `INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.17 01:00:00|jump=259200s`. Then at 01:00:05 the first new day line reads `anchor=2026.08.17 01:00:00|realized=0.00|floating=0.00|base=2133.13|limit=106.66` with the server clock unfrozen to 2026.08.17 01:00:07. The prediction corrected at commit 59f6d21 named base 2133.13 and limit 106.66 in advance and both are exact; the superseded figure 2033.13 appears nowhere in the day, which is the discriminating check. Q8 LIVE FIRING NUMBER THREE, weekend class, and exactly one ALERT for the day against the cadence cap. `seconds_in_state` runs 217637 to 217667 straight across the boundary, so the rollover was absorbed inside ACTIVE with no transition, the same absorption P1-A and P1-M recorded. Day totals for 2026-08-17 to 09:41:35: 1164 LIFE lines, no gap above 31 seconds, DEGRADED 0, RESYNC 0, WARN 0, ALERT 1.

P1-B, stated plainly at owner request. The row reads "one weekend harvest: anchor holds Friday through the freeze, adopts Monday at reopen, no Sat/Sun rollover lines | journal scan, quoted lines" at PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md:251. The PROPERTY is established and the ARTIFACT SET is not clean, and those are different things. Clause by clause. "Adopts Monday at reopen" is fully met and quoted above. "Anchor holds Friday through the freeze" is directly evidenced for all of Saturday, 2880 lines with the anchor pinned and zero non LIFE lines, and for Sunday to 11:13:33. "No Sat/Sun rollover lines" is directly evidenced for all of Saturday and for Sunday to 11:13:33, and for the remaining Sunday window it is established INDIRECTLY but rigorously, from an intact artifact: Monday's reopen reports `previous_high=2026.08.14 01:00:00` and `old_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00` and `jump=259200s`. Q8's latch is a HIGH WATER MARK, so any rollover that had fired on Saturday or Sunday would have raised previous_high to that later value and would have shortened the jump below three days. Neither happened, so no anchor movement occurred anywhere between 2026.08.14 01:00:00 and 2026.08.17 01:00:00, including inside the sleep and inside the deleted window. The property holds. What is NOT clean is the harvest itself, and for two independent reasons only one of which is the executor's fault: a genuine 3 hour 18 minute hole where the machine was asleep and emitted nothing at all, and the deleted remainder of the Sunday file. RECOMMENDATION, and it is a recommendation and not a ruling: re harvest next weekend. It costs nothing, since the machine sits idle through a freeze anyway, it removes a defect the executor introduced, and P1-B is precisely the row whose subject is an uninterrupted observation, so closing it on a reconstruction argues against the thing it exists to test. What this weekend has already proven should stand recorded so that next weekend's run is confirmatory rather than exploratory. P1-B is left OPEN and the owner may overrule this and close it on the inference above, which is sound.

Evidence, snapshotted this session before any analysis was run, because protecting the surviving files came first. docs/evidence/terminal-20260816-fullday-sleep-window.txt, 4839 bytes, md5 5626D3652E12375F0D00C02ACE863B50, 49 lines, carrying the sleep bracket at lines 34 and 35. docs/evidence/journal-20260817-monday-reopen.txt, 342137 bytes, md5 08AE0F7444B3F5943E9F5A7757DBC48F, 1168 lines, a partial since the day is still running, last LIFE line local 2026.08.17 09:41:35. docs/evidence/terminal-20260817-monday.txt, 143 bytes, md5 731D09C9F8687810E71FA0B3EFD70426, 2 lines. 2026-08-16, the weekend freeze block, harvested with the machine untouched as instructed. The owner reports a second deposit of 100.00 made inside the weekend freeze, for P1-F. P1-F does NOT close on this session's artifacts, and the reason is the most useful thing in this entry, because it is a property of the acceptance row rather than a shortfall in the harvest: EVERY FIELD THE EA LOGS IS INVARIANT UNDER A CORRECTLY HANDLED DEPOSIT, so the journal alone can never witness that a deposit happened at all. Q2's base identity is `AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_BALANCE)` minus the all deals sum, read at Pnl.mqh:116-122, and the source comment states the neutrality outright: a deposit raises Balance and raises the sum by the same amount. So base holds. Realized holds too, since balance deals are whitelisted out of it per Q3 and F12. Floating is 0.00 with no positions open. Limit is derived from base. Nothing moves. The 2026-08-14 deposit was only witnessed because two independent external readings existed that day, the owner's terminal balance of 2033.13 and the restart reconstruction, and neither exists this weekend by design, since the machine is deliberately untouched for P1-B.

What IS established, and it reduces P1-F to a single missing number rather than a missing run. The freeze is real and measured, not assumed: the server stamp is pinned at `2026.08.14 23:57:59` on every LIFE line from Friday's close through local 2026-08-16 11:04:33, while the local stamp advances normally on the same lines, and the anchor is consequently pinned at `2026.08.14 01:00:00` with no rollover fired across either weekend night. That is a genuinely frozen `TimeCurrent`, which is the precondition P1-F exists to test. Base reads 1985.97 on all 2880 Saturday lines and all 1330 Sunday lines, counted rather than sampled. The arithmetic is therefore already forced, conditional on one owner reading. Base is `balance` minus `deals since anchor`, and deals since anchor are the carry close of -52.84 plus the deposits. If the second deposit landed AND its deal is inside the history window, balance is 2133.13, the sum is 147.16, and base is 1985.97, which is what the artifact shows. If the deposit landed and its deal fell OUTSIDE the window, the failure P1-F hunts, base would read 2085.97. If the deposit has not landed yet, base reads 1985.97 for the trivial reason. So the owner's balance figure discriminates all three, and no other number is needed: 2133.13 closes P1-F, 2033.13 means the deposit has not arrived, and a base of 2085.97 would have failed the row outright and did not appear.

Recorded alongside it, the upper bound clause of P1-F is PASS BY CONSTRUCTION in the source and the live run only corroborates it. `#define AG_HISTORY_SELECT_TO D'3000.01.01'` at Pnl.mqh:21, used by both `AgRealized` at :55 and `AgDealsSumAll` at :88, with a header comment stating the obligation and discharging it: the bound is not derived from TimeCurrent, so neither a frozen nor a backward stepping clock can truncate the window. This weekend supplied the frozen clock in reality and the window did not truncate, since base never drifted to a stale value across 4210 lines. The row's phrase "proving the upper bound" is thus satisfied on the code side already, and the live half is the corroboration.

P1-B lattice harvest, collected clean. Saturday 2026-08-15 is a complete 24 hour file: exactly 2880 LIFE lines from 00:00:03 to 23:59:33, every consecutive interval 30 seconds, zero gaps above 31 seconds, zero non LIFE lines of any kind, zero DEGRADED, zero WARN, zero ALERT, base constant. Sunday to 11:04:33 is 1330 lines with the same zero gap property. A count of 1324 taken earlier in the session is superseded by 1330 and is noted here only because the difference was the live file growing between two reads, which is why the continuity check was re run rather than reported from the first pass.

P1-O advances on artifacts nobody staged, and two of its four clauses are now met on runs that CANNOT be contaminated, since the machine was untouched all weekend. The terminal journal records four connection losses on 2026-08-16. Two were long enough for the 30 second LIFE cadence to sample: a 93.8 second gap from 01:40:07.063 to 01:41:40.874, and an 80.9 second gap from 05:30:17.549 to 05:31:38.409. Both produced exactly three DEGRADED lines each, at 01:40:33, 01:41:03, 01:41:33 and at 05:30:33, 05:31:03, 05:31:33, carrying the marker on BOTH the `waiting_on` field and the numbers field group, reading `waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED`. The boundaries are exact in both windows: the last clean line precedes the loss and the first clean line follows the reauthorization, with no clean line inside either gap and no DEGRADED line outside one. Zero ALERTs fired, against a whole day non LIFE count of zero. So P1-O's "DEGRADED prefix on both the numbers field group and waiting_on throughout the gap" and its "zero ALERTs during it" are both MET here, and met twice, which is more than the contaminated 2026-08-14 run delivered.

The RESYNC clause is still not met and this session upgrades the diagnosis, which matters because the previous entry's framing was probably wrong. Across four live disconnects now, the 2026-08-13 wake, the 2026-08-14 deliberate cut, and these two natural weekend losses, the RESYNC count is ZERO. The earlier entry read that as the gate never executing. The likelier reading, and the one the numbers support, is that THE GATE IS UNOBSERVABLE THROUGH THE ONLY CHANNEL THAT REPORTS IT. The timer runs at 1 second, confirmed by `timer=1s` on the boot line, and `HistoryStablePolls` is 3, so a RESYNC episode lasts about 3 seconds. The RESYNC prefix appears only on LIFE lines, which are emitted every 30 seconds. The chance of any one reconnect being sampled during its RESYNC window is therefore roughly one in ten, and four events at that rate are far more likely to yield zero observations than one. There is no event driven line to fall back on: the whole weekend contains zero non LIFE lines, so RESYNC entry and exit are never announced, only sampled. Reinforcing the same point from the other side, the two SHORT losses this morning, 0.6 seconds at 01:52:24.723 and 0.7 seconds at 05:31:48.284, produced no DEGRADED line at all, because both opened and closed entirely between two LIFE emissions. The condition existed and the artifact does not contain it. As written, P1-O asks for a roughly 3 second signal to be caught by a 30 second sampler, which makes the row close by luck rather than by procedure, and that is a defect in the acceptance row and arguably in the observability of the build, not evidence about the gate's correctness either way. Nothing is proposed as authority here and no code is touched.

One methodology trap banked, because it very nearly produced a false alarm of the worst kind. `Get-ChildItem` reported the live EA log `20260816.log` as 0 bytes with a last write time of 00:00:42, which read as the guardian having died at midnight with the terminal still running. That was FALSE. Windows does not update a file's directory entry while a writer holds the handle open, and MT5 holds its log open all day. Opening the same path with `FileShare.ReadWrite` and reading `Stream.Length` returned 781774 bytes. The EA was alive and had been logging normally the whole time. The rule this yields, and it belongs with the CRLF regex trap of 2026-08-06: never judge a live MT5 log by its directory metadata, always read the stream. Every byte count in this entry comes from the stream.

Evidence, all under docs/evidence, snapshotted this session and hashed. journal-20260815-weekend-freeze-saturday.txt 847642 bytes md5 91ABD2E1EA526567519FD3C38A992DDE, 2880 lines. journal-20260816-weekend-freeze-sunday-partial.txt 393246 bytes md5 14EB8AD174BBB578B75465C34ABE0B2F, 1332 lines, explicitly a partial since the day was still running at capture, last LIFE line local 2026.08.16 11:04:33. terminal-20260815-weekend.txt 572 bytes md5 F9CD9EFD80926963D85CDBBB2AC15E7F. terminal-20260816-weekend-disconnects.txt 3122 bytes md5 FB19AA079634ED90375AC78CD94EC2A2, carrying the four connection loss and reauthorization pairs. Note for any later integrity check so a size mismatch is not misread as loss: MT5 writes these logs UTF-16LE and the snapshots are written UTF-8, so a snapshot is close to half its source in bytes while line for line identical, and the line counts above are the figure to compare, not the byte counts. Source read this session at Pnl.mqh:15-21, :52-58, :85-91 and :108-122.

2026-08-16, closing addendum to the entry above, written the same session once the missing witness arrived. P1-F CLOSES. The owner's terminal balance reads 2133.13, which is the value this entry named in advance as the closing one, and the conditional above becomes a measurement. It is bound to a live line rather than to the earlier harvest, so that the reading and the artifact refer to the same instant rather than to a stale one: base reads 1985.97 on the LIFE line stamped local 2026.08.16 11:13:33, taken seconds after the owner's reading, with the server stamp still pinned at 2026.08.14 23:57:59 and the anchor still at 2026.08.14 01:00:00. The identity is then forced with no free parameter left in it. 2133.13 minus 1985.97 is 147.16, and the only deals since the anchor are the carry close of -52.84 and the two deposits of 100.00 each, which sum to exactly 147.16 and to nothing else. Both balance deals are therefore inside the history window under a clock that has not advanced since Friday's close, which is exactly what the row asks for and what the constant bound at Pnl.mqh:21 predicts. Checked for a transient rather than only at the endpoints, because a momentarily wrong base would be a defect even if it corrected itself before the next emission: all 1348 LIFE lines of the day carry base=1985.97 and realized=-52.84 and no other value appears at any point, so the deposit was absorbed with no observable discontinuity whatsoever. That also means the artifact cannot date the deposit, which is the same invariance this entry opened with and is worth stating plainly rather than leaving as a gap someone later tries to close. Collected free alongside the close, P1-G's whitelist clause is re-proven a second time and under a frozen clock this time, since realized held at -52.84 across 200.00 of cumulative balance operations. Evidence: docs/evidence/journal-20260816-p1f-close-binding.txt, 397966 bytes, md5 9331F4C301D893F4912E51503E5031DB, 1348 lines. The earlier partial snapshot was byte verified to be a strict prefix of it rather than assumed to be one, its 393246 bytes comparing equal.

2026-08-14, second entry, the morning terminal block with the owner at the machine: carry closed, deposit run, restart reconstruction, disconnect. Two rows CLOSE, P1-E and P1-G. One row is met on its acceptance criterion but not on its procedure and is deliberately NOT closed, P1-C. Two rows do NOT close, P1-K and P1-O, and P1-K's failure is structural rather than procedural, which is the most important thing in this entry. Every figure below is re-derived by the executor from the terminal's own journals; the owner's readings are quoted only where they are the GUI side of a cross check.

Baseline at 11:39, four independent witnesses agreeing. Owner read balance 1985.97, equity 1933.72, margin 2.20, free margin 1931.52, carry ticket 411788798 buy 0.01 XAUUSD.ecn opened 2026.08.11 16:40:34 at 4399.90, current 4347.65, profit -52.25, banner -52.18. Journal at 11:39:32 read floating=-52.26. Checks: equity minus balance is -52.25, equal to the ticket profit exactly, so the EA's floating reproduces the platform's own equity arithmetic; free margin 1933.72 minus 2.20 is 1931.52 exactly; the banner figure sits between the 11:39:02 and 11:39:32 LIFE lines, which is the 30 second grid and not a discrepancy. Base agreement on three witnesses, journal base=1985.97, terminal balance 1985.97, personal area balance 1985.97, two of them off terminal and independent, the same class of check as P1-D on a second day and a different value.

SWAP IS ZERO ON THIS INSTRUMENT AND ACCOUNT, measured twice, and it corrects a claim this ledger and the owner both carried. 4399.90 minus 4347.65 is 52.25 and the profit is -52.25, exact to the cent at 1 oz per 0.01 lot, leaving zero residual for swap after three nights held. The close an hour later confirmed it independently on the realized side, 4399.90 minus the 4347.06 fill is 52.84 against realized -52.84. Equity carries swap, so the equity check is a second witness rather than a restatement. The owner's instruction of 2026-08-13 described the day's PnL as the carry's floating "including two nights of swap", and the earlier 2026-08-14 entry below repeated "swap included"; both are measurably wrong and the latter is corrected in place. Any commission was booked into balance at open rather than carried in floating.

Carry closed 11:42:38.298, deal #268572111 sell 0.01 XAUUSD.ecn at 4347.06 on order #440372679, closing position #411788798. Six predictions were stated in advance and all six held: realized moved 0.00 to -52.84; floating went to 0.00; pnl_vs_limit stayed CONTINUOUS across the event, -52.92 on the last pre close line at 11:42:32 against -52.84 on the first post close line at 11:43:02, the 0.08 being six seconds of price movement and not a step; base held 1985.97; limit held 99.30; and no TRANSITION and no ALERT anywhere. The continuity is the measurement that matters, because converting floating into realized without a step is what the Q2 identity requires and it had never been observed on a real deal. Lattice unbroken, 1351 LIFE lines on the day at that point. Note for the record that the close closes no row by itself: it supplies P1-C's precondition, deals present, which this account had never had.

Deposit of 100.00 through the personal area at 11:51, no fee, balance 1933.13 to 2033.13 exactly. P1-E CLOSES on its criterion, base and limit unchanged within 0.01, and they were unchanged exactly rather than within tolerance, 1985.97 and 99.30 across 11:50:32 through 11:52:32. P1-G's first clause CLOSES, realized moves by exactly 0, holding at -52.84 to the cent straight through; the discriminating counterfactual is that a deposit mistaken for a trading deal would have moved realized to +47.16. Recorded because it affects how this artifact is found later: the deposit produces NO terminal journal line at all, so its evidence is the balance delta and the deal history, not a log entry. The Q3 DEAL_FEE term is zero here and therefore untested by this deposit.

P1-G CLOSES WHOLE on the restart reconstruction, which collected its second clause immediately rather than deferring it to Monday. Clean deinit at 11:55:21.060 reading reason=9 session marked clean timer_armed=1 timer_ticks=41147, which is one tick per second across a session of 41149 seconds and an eleven and a half hour corroboration of the tick arithmetic. Fresh init 11:56:02.280, chain=1/3, BOOT->SYNCING at 11:56:02.284, SYNCING->ACTIVE history stable polls=3/3 at 11:56:05.285, first ACTIVE LIFE at 11:56:33.296 reading realized=-52.84, floating=0.00, base=1985.97, limit=99.30, anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00. The image inherited nothing, globals reset and 0 positions on sync, so base was rebuilt from history alone and the arithmetic it had to get right was 2033.13 minus -52.84 minus 100.00 equals 1985.97. It produced exactly that. The wrong answers are what make this discriminating: ignoring the deposit when backing out the day's balance movements gives 2085.97, and counting it as a trading deal gives realized +47.16. Neither appeared, so the 100.00 sits in the base sum at the right magnitude and the right sign, which is P1-G's second clause "the base sum by the exact amount" proven on the reconstruction path.

P1-C is NOT CLOSED and the reason is recorded rather than smoothed over. The row's procedure is "kill mid-day with deals present, relaunch"; the owner performed a CLEAN CLOSE. The artifacts discriminate twice: the deinit logged "session marked clean", and the boot logged chain=1/3 where a kill would have produced chain=2/3, which was the executor's stated prediction beforehand. The acceptance criterion IS met, PnL equal before and after within 0.01, measured -52.84 at 11:55:02 and -52.84 at 11:56:33 for a difference of exactly 0.00, and the reconstruction path was genuinely exercised since a terminal restart sits in the fresh image family beside a kill. What was not exercised is the unclean variant, and the difference between the two is confined to the halt file clean flag and the crash chain, neither of which touches the PnL rebuild. Recorded as CRITERION MET, PROCEDURE DEVIATES, left for owner ruling, because this project's standard is that a row closes on the evidence its plan names and an executor may not widen that on its own authority.

P1-K CANNOT CLOSE BY ITS NAMED PROCEDURE, AND THIS IS STRUCTURAL. Detailed in its own ISSUES entry above. Measured: zero WARN lines exist in the whole day's journal, and the row requires a loud WARN from a false HistorySelect. Source read gives the mechanism: AccountGuardian.mq5:121 returns on !TERMINAL_CONNECTED before any history call is made, and Pnl.mqh:178 guards a second time, so a disconnect sets DEGRADED and exits early and the false return the row wants is never reachable that way. Same category as the Q8 cadence cap's permanent scope limit, unfalsifiable by construction through the named route.

P1-O does NOT close, and the run was additionally contaminated. The genuine disconnect ran 12:27:40.793 to 12:32:15.828 on the terminal log, about four minutes thirty five seconds, roughly nine LIFE lines. Of the row's four clauses, two are met and two are not. Met: the DEGRADED prefix appears on BOTH the waiting_on field and the numbers field group, on the lines at 12:28:03 and 12:28:33; and zero ALERTs fired during the gap, confirmed against a whole day count of zero. Not met: "throughout the gap" fails, because only 2 DEGRADED lines exist against roughly nine lines of gap; and the RESYNC clause fails outright, with zero RESYNC lines in the day, so the reconnect coherence gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:140 has now survived two live disconnects without ever executing. The contamination was three chart period changes at 12:28:36, 12:28:39 and 12:28:42, M5 to M30 to H1 to M15, each logging deinit reason=3 REASON_CHARTCHANGE, which forced the EA out of ACTIVE into a fresh SYNCING entry and so routed the reconnect through the ordinary stability counter instead of the RESYNC path. The owner first reported the machine untouched and corrected it to timeframe changes on being shown the lines; nothing changed the chart autonomously.

A SECOND FINDING FELL OUT OF THE CONTAMINATION AND IS INDEPENDENT OF IT: while disconnected but in SYNCING, the EA carries NO DEGRADED marker at all. The seven SYNCING lines from 12:29:03 to 12:32:03 read waiting_on=polls=0/3 with no degraded indication, and the terminal did not reconnect until 12:32:15, so every one of those lines was emitted offline. A reader of those lines cannot tell the terminal was down. Recorded in its own ISSUES entry with the P1-O remainder.

Free gains from the contaminated run, none of them planned. A7 is RE-PROVED LIVE on the deployed Phase 1 build: three re-inits six seconds apart, far inside the 300 second gap bound, and the chain stayed 1/3 throughout because every deinit logged session marked clean; had the clean flag not reset the chain the walk would have reached CrashLoopMaxInits=3 and fired SAFE_HALT, and it did not. The inherited image property is confirmed again, timer_ticks continuing 1953 to 1955 to 1958 across all three re-inits with no reset. The first ever observation of TRANSITION ACTIVE->SYNCING|boot was recorded and then checked against the SPEC rather than reported as a finding: docs/SPEC_v0.1.md:47 post A5 reads "boot in a fresh image, or the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init (A5)", ACTIVE is the surviving prior state, so the line is CONFORMING and the executor's initial suspicion of a table gap is withdrawn. And the reconnect gating worked in spirit even though the RESYNC path did not run: polls held at 0/3 for three minutes offline, the EA never resumed ACTIVE on absent history, and it transitioned at 12:32:18, exactly three seconds and three polls after the 12:32:15 reconnect, matching HistoryStablePolls=3.

State at the close of the block, verified at 14:15:03: ACTIVE, seconds_in_state=6165 continuous since the 12:32:18 transition, realized=-52.84, floating=0.00, base=1985.97, limit=99.30, day totals DEGRADED 2, RESYNC 0, WARN 0, ALERT 0. THE CHART IS NOW M15 AND WAS NOT RESTORED TO M5. That is functionally irrelevant to an account level guardian but it changes the journal's chart label, so it is recorded here so no future session reads the weekend harvest's M15 lines as a different instance. Standing predictions banked for Monday: base should recompute to 2033.13, and the reopen should produce Q8 live firing number three, weekend class, jump=259200s. SUPERSEDED 2026-08-16 on the base figure only, and corrected here rather than left standing, because a stale prediction of this kind is exactly what gets read as a failure on Monday morning when the build is in fact correct. The second deposit of 100.00 landed inside the weekend freeze and the owner's balance now reads 2133.13, so Monday's rollover, which moves the anchor to 2026.08.17 01:00:00 with zero deals after it, should recompute base to 2133.13 and the limit with it to 106.66 at the ruled five percent. A Monday base of 2033.13 would now be the WRONG answer and would mean the second deposit had been dropped from the balance side. The Q8 half of the prediction is UNCHANGED and stands as written, since the anchor has been pinned at 2026.08.14 01:00:00 throughout the freeze, making Monday's crossing a three day jump of exactly 259200s. Weekend plan agreed with the owner: the machine stays on and untouched for the P1-B lattice harvest, and deposit two, the frozen clock one for P1-F, is due Saturday inside the freeze, which the owner is aware of and which must be run before any staleness change ships since a DEGRADED gate would rewrite that row's expected artifact.

Evidence for this entry, snapshotted at 14:1x while both files were still being written and committed alongside it: docs/evidence/journal-20260814-morning-block.txt at 979066 bytes md5 8536F55A0DEAF4B09C853531F9D0FEBF, the EA journal carrying the whole day including the close, the deposit window, the restart and the disconnect; and docs/evidence/terminal-20260814-morning-block.txt at 9628 bytes md5 DCB08A178F0B83A7D9BB1D3E47ED7381, the terminal journal carrying the trade lines for deal #268572111, the two boots, and the connection lost and reauthorized stamps that bracket the true disconnect gap. The earlier docs/evidence/journal-20260814-observed-rollover.txt at md5 D543C8B918C1C18BF373FBE0337AC8E5 is retained unchanged as the narrower snapshot taken at 01:09 for the rollover itself and is a strict prefix of the morning block file, kept because the entry below cites it.

2026-08-14: Sleep survival measured, Q8 live firing number two banked, and the first OBSERVED 01:00 day rollover captured live, closing P1-H. Four things happened across three calendar days and they are recorded as one entry because they are one continuous run of the same process, broken only by the owner's firmware restart. Everything below is re-derived by this session from the terminal's own journals, not from the owner's report, and the point where the report and the artifacts disagree is stated as the artifacts have it.

First, the sleep. The machine slept on 2026-08-11 at server 19:17:10 and woke on 2026-08-13 at local 11:44:59. The last pre-sleep line stamps local 19:30:22 against a server clock already frozen at 19:17:10, and the first line back stamps local 2026.08.13 11:44:59 against that same frozen server value, so the freeze is visible on both edges of the hole. The state survived intact: seconds_in_state reads 18437 on the last line and 163314 on the first line back, a delta of 144877 seconds, and the local wall clock across the same hole is 144877 seconds exactly. That reproduces the 2026-08-05 finding on a second and much longer sleep, so seconds_in_state is a wall clock difference and the state entry timestamp is not disturbed by suspend and resume. There is no init line, no TRANSITION, no mutex takeover and no SAFE_HALT anywhere in the whole of 2026-08-13 up to the deinit, established by grep over the entire file rather than by sampling, so the process never died and the in memory Q8 anchor latch was never reconstructed. That is the load bearing point of the whole entry: the latch survived because the image survived, and this is the first time that has been shown across a sleep rather than across a quiet period.

Second, and recorded as an unplanned gain, the first line back is a DEGRADED line and nobody scheduled it: "LIFE|state=ACTIVE|seconds_in_state=163314|waiting_on=DEGRADED: disconnected, no breach decisions|DEGRADED|anchor=2026.08.11 01:00:00" carrying server=2026.08.11 19:17:10 against local=2026.08.13 11:44:59 on the one line. That is the Q10 DEGRADED path firing live for the first time, with the frozen server stamp and the live local stamp printed side by side, and it is evidence toward P1-O obtained for free from a wake rather than from the deliberate disconnect that row is still waiting on.

Third, Q8 live firing number two, and it is the wake from sleep class rather than the weekend reopen class of 2026-08-10. At 11:45:13.031 the EA emitted exactly four lines for one event: an INFO twin, the ALERT "anchor jump accepted: previous_high=2026.08.11 01:00:00, accepted=2026.08.13 01:00:00, jump=172800s (jump exceeds one day; evaluation continues, Q8 amendment 2026-08-09)", the ungated terminal Alert popup carrying the same string, and "INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.11 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.13 01:00:00|jump=172800s". One ALERT, cadence cap intact, evaluation continued, no state change. The question of whether Wednesday's anchor was ever seen is answered in the strongest available form: there is NO 20260812.log in the terminal's MQL5 Logs directory at all, verified by Test-Path returning False, so the EA emitted not one line during the whole of 2026-08-12 and the jump went straight from 08.11 to 08.13 with nothing in between. Every 2026-08-13 LIFE line reads realized=0.00, base=1985.97 and limit=99.30, and 1985.97 at five percent is 99.2985 which rounds to the printed 99.30, so the recomputed base and the derived limit both check out arithmetically rather than being taken on report.

Fourth, the timeline correction, recorded because the ledger must not carry a wrong one forward. The owner's instruction described the firmware restart as happening on the afternoon of 2026-08-13. The artifacts place it nearly ten hours later. The EA deinit is at 14:45:13.023 reading "reason=9|session marked clean|timer_armed=1|timer_ticks=28452", the terminal logged "expert AccountGuardian (XAUUSD.ecn,M5) removed" and then "exit with code 0" at 14:45:14, and the machine's LastBootUpTime is 2026-08-14 00:28:26, with the terminal restarting at 00:29:29 and the expert loading at 00:29:30.693. So the guardian was down for nine hours and forty three minutes, from 14:45:14 on 2026-08-13 to 00:29:30 on 2026-08-14, and that window is unprotected and unobserved. The shutdown itself was clean and the restart healthy: crash loop chain 1 of 3, TRANSITION BOOT to SYNCING at 00:29:31.758, SYNCING to ACTIVE on "history stable|polls=3/3" at 00:29:34.767, and the fresh image re seeded the anchor to 2026.08.13 01:00:00 from a server clock reading 2026.08.13 23:57:59, which is the correct anchor for that server day and confirms the rollover seeding fix on a fresh boot.

Fifth, the observed rollover, which is what P1-H was held open for. At local 01:00:00.763 on 2026-08-14, server stamp 2026.08.14 01:00:01, the EA emitted "INFO|day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.13 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00|jump=86400s" and nothing else. This is the negative control the Q8 amendment has never had in the live channel: the jump is exactly one day, so the ALERT branch correctly did NOT fire, and a grep of the entire 2026-08-14 file for ALERT and for the Alert popup returns zero lines. There is no TRANSITION at the event either, so the rollover was absorbed inside ACTIVE exactly as the Monday reopen was, and seconds_in_state steps 1798 to 1828 straight through the boundary with no reset. A carry position was open across the crossing, which is what makes the row meaningful rather than a bare clock test.

Sixth, a consequence measured at the rollover and flagged rather than ruled, because it is a design question and not a defect. The carry's floating PnL did NOT reset at the day boundary: the last line of the old day reads floating=-49.45 and the first line of the new day reads floating=-45.65, the difference being live price movement and not a reset. realized went to 0.00 as designed and base stayed at 1985.97 because there was no realized amount to fold in. So a position held across midnight drags its entire accumulated unrealized loss, swap included, into every new day's limit comparison. The new day opened at -45.65 against a limit of -99.30, which is forty six percent of the day's allowance consumed at second zero on a loss earned on previous days. This is a direct consequence of the Q2 identity as written and it is not a bug against any current ruling, but a Phase 2 build would be able to lock a day on a loss that day did not produce, so it wants an owner ruling before enforcement lands.

Evidence, all four artifacts committed with this entry under docs/evidence: journal-20260811-sleep-entry.txt at 347998 bytes md5 8BD4CFBB408C3304E98093558ABD2D83 carrying the sleep entry edge; journal-20260813-sleep-survival-q8-second-firing.txt at 214030 bytes md5 F68D75742CA9DB0E8C776AC850562F75 carrying the wake, the DEGRADED line, the Q8 firing and the clean deinit; journal-20260814-observed-rollover.txt at 43852 bytes md5 D543C8B918C1C18BF373FBE0337AC8E5 snapshotted while the file was still being written, carrying the fresh boot and the observed crossing; and journal-20260814-rollover-window-extract.txt at 6343 bytes md5 EED5743E6FBF90DD16DC7F15C043801D, the machine generated extract of the event lines plus the 00:55 to 01:06 window. Corroboration that the evidence directory is intact rather than assumed: the existing journal-20260810-monday-reopen.txt still hashes to 244CB38E4E5CA75AE13140FCDBC84A30, matching the value the 2026-08-10 entry recorded for it.

Output quoted: "To https://github.com/www8351/Account_Guardian_V2.git" followed by "cabeb59..a2f3a8e  main -> main", exit 0, so the push carried sixteen commits, the two bot badge commits, the thirteen-commit merge and its ledger entry. Remote position at the moment of this push, a dated observation and nothing more, per the FINAL remote-state ruling of 2026-08-04: local main and origin/main both read a2f3a8e immediately after. That is what two refs said at one instant; it is not a standing property of the repository and it is stale the moment anything moves either ref, which is exactly why the ruling forbids carrying it forward as fact. GitHub Desktop needs no separate action: it reads the same local repository and the same origin, so it reflects this push on its next refresh rather than requiring a second one.

Verification, all against the committed merge tree at d52f063 rather than against this narrative: git diff --stat c4d363f d52f063 over MQL5/, docs/ and LEDGER.md returns empty, so those paths are byte-identical to the branch; git diff --stat 42ede70 d52f063 over README.md returns empty, so README came through from main untouched; docs/SPEC_v0.1.md now carries amendments A1 through A6 in sequence, the A5-before-A6 ordering the Phase 1 plan required, verified by grep returning exactly one "### A6," line; LEDGER counts read 56 Issue, 60 Decision and 57 dated ACTIONS against 54, 58 and 53 on the main parent and 56, 60 and 57 on the branch parent, so every count sits at or above both sides; the attribution scan over 42ede70..d52f063 returns zero. One measurement worth recording because this project has been bitten by line endings before: the MQL5 working-copy files read as differing from the branch blobs on a naive diff, 6349 bytes against 6235 for Clock.mqh, and the cause is CRLF applied by git on checkout, not content. Normalizing carriage returns makes them identical, and git diff --cached against the branch returned empty before the commit, so the committed blobs are the branch's own and only the working copy carries CRLF.

Anomaly recorded, and it is the SECOND occurrence of exactly this: the merge commit was not produced by the git commit this session ran. The staged, verified merge index was committed by something else, with git's bare default MERGE_MSG "Merge branch 'worktree-phase1-pnl-core'" rather than the full message this session composed, which carried the gate position above and is lost. The session's own git commit then reported "no changes added to commit" against the leftover .gitignore, which is the signature of a commit arriving into an already-committed state. The same thing happened on 2026-08-08 with merge 937c604 and is recorded in that entry as cause-unestablished. A candidate is now named rather than concluded: an automated committer is demonstrably active in this repository, producing the "chore: token usage badge [skip ci]" commits, two of which (07e6169, 42ede70) landed since the branch point, and any periodic committer that runs while MERGE_HEAD exists will commit the prepared merge index under the default MERGE_MSG. Not proven, and not asserted as fact. What IS verified is that nothing was lost or altered by it: the parents, the tree and every check above are exactly what this session staged, and the gate record the lost message carried is written here instead, which is the governance record that actually binds.

Recurring defect, third observation, unchanged in cause: .gitignore again carries an uncommitted appended block written by Initialize-GitWorkspace in the owner's PowerShell profile. This occurrence is BENIGN where the 2026-08-08 one was not, and the difference is worth stating so nobody treats them as the same event: the appended block this time reproduces the full ruleset including the docs and LEDGER negations, so git check-ignore -v on LEDGER.md and on docs/PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md both exit 1, meaning nothing is wrongly ignored. It is duplication, not breakage. Left uncommitted and untouched by this session, since it is bot output rather than owner work and the durable fix remains owner-side in the profile's $ignoreRules, exactly as the 2026-08-08 entry recorded.

Flagged for the owner, not acted on: the README token-usage badge block is BACK on main, re-added by the badge bot at 07e6169 after the owner ruled on 2026-08-08 to drop it as a violation of the no-AI-reference rule. It names model identifiers again. This merge did not introduce it and did not remove it, README having come through from the main parent untouched; removing it here would be undone by the bot's next run, so the durable fix is owner-side in whatever writes that block, the same class as the .gitignore item above.

2026-08-10: THE MONDAY REOPEN FIRED AND THE AMENDMENT HELD. Joint session, the owner watched it live and took a screenshot, and every claim below is re-derived by the executor from the journal rather than from the owner's account, per the standing rule that a session report is not evidence. The two channels agree on every point, and the one place they read different clocks is identified rather than smoothed over. Full evidence in docs/evidence/phase1-stage5-deploy-2026-08-09.md sections 9 through 9h.

State re-derived first and quoted: branch `worktree-phase1-pnl-core` at `f84ac33081ad084842c8b433bc785ef35001ff7d`, `git status --porcelain` empty, commit-msg hook first bytes `23 21 2f 62 69 6e 2f 73` so BOM free. Banked with a `.txt` extension deliberately and the reason recorded in the evidence document: `.gitignore` carries `*.log`, so a `.log` copy would have been IGNORED and this ledger entry would have cited a path and an md5 for an artifact no future session could retrieve, which is exactly the phantom-pointer failure mode already recorded three times here. The convention was set by the deploy session's own `compile-log-pre-phase1.txt` and zero `.log` files are tracked anywhere in the repository; the rename is byte-preserving with the md5 verified identical before and after. Caught by checking `git status` against the file rather than by assuming the add would succeed. A plain read fails with "The process cannot access the file ... because it is being used by another process", which is itself confirmation the instance was live when the copy was taken. Every figure below is re-derived from the SNAPSHOT, not from the growing live file, so the banked artifact and the banked numbers cannot drift apart.

The event is four lines at ONE millisecond, local 00:59:59.037 on server `2026.08.10 01:00:00`: the INFO carrying the note, the ALERT carrying the note plus its amendment annotation, MT5's own `Alert:` echo which is an independent witness that a real modal popup was raised, and the day rollover line. Whole-file counts are the artifact form of the owner's "no popup storm": ONE `ALERT`, ONE `Alert:` echo, ONE `day rollover`, and ZERO `init` and ZERO `TRANSITION` lines anywhere in the day, so the reopen was absorbed entirely inside ACTIVE with no state change of any kind. All four falsifiable points the deploy document named IN ADVANCE are answered PASS: the single cadence-capped ALERT naming `previous_high=2026.08.07 01:00:00`, `accepted=2026.08.10 01:00:00` and `jump=259200s`; its matching INFO line; a GENUINE `day rollover|old_anchor=2026.08.07 01:00:00|new_anchor=2026.08.10 01:00:00|jump=259200s`; and the next ACTIVE LIFE line at 01:00:20.040 computing fresh against the Monday anchor with `waiting_on=-`. Continuity measured across ALL 153 LIFE lines rather than sampled, zero anomalies of any kind: `seconds_in_state` 4498 to 9058 in 152 consecutive steps of EXACTLY 30 with zero off-cadence steps and a span of 4560 equal to 152 x 30; the counter tying back to the 2026-08-09 22:45:22 ACTIVE entry to the second at both ends, so it never reset across the reopen; `state=ACTIVE` on all 153; `waiting_on=-` on all 153; anchor splitting 120 Friday to 33 Monday exactly at the event with zero other values; and `base=2003.46`, `limit=100.17`, `realized=0.00`, `floating=0.00`, `pnl_vs_limit=0.00 vs -100.17` on all 153. Same-instance proof, which is what makes this a live test rather than a staged one, on four cross-witnesses independent of the journal narrative: process `terminal64` pid 11028 with start time 2026-08-09 22:45:14, which is relaunch 2 exactly; the halt file BYTE-UNTOUCHED at 283 bytes, md5 96440737734328DBE095BDC476898C83, mtime still 22:45:19, 15 records with the newest `S|1786315519|0` still unclean, halt flag still `H|0||0`, and no sixteenth record so no init ran and OnDeinit never ran; the loaded ex5 unchanged at mtime 2026-08-09 19:10:59 md5 AC19EAEA1A65E3C4D052E151C21502E9, the post-amendment compile; and all seven terminal sources still md5-matching the worktree pairwise, `Clock.mqh` at 31BAD26357DDF6E973DFAA1D49C337DB and `AccountGuardian.mq5` at EB646E20A17BF806C803C12A4C6878F4. Build identity therefore rests on all three instruments standing rule 7 permits and on none it forbids.

THE REOPEN BOUNDARY IS EARLIER THAN THE LEDGER HAS BEEN SAYING, and the prior figure is reinterpreted rather than refuted, which is the measurement finding of the session. The owner flagged that the tick landed at 00:59:59, a second before the nominal 01:00 and 21 seconds before the previously recorded 01:00:20. Both halves resolve. `00:59:59.037` is the LOCAL clock, and on that same line the server clock reads exactly `2026.08.10 01:00:00`, so the resumed server clock came back precisely on the boundary while local trails server by about one second, an offset that holds on every subsequent line. The historical "Monday reopen at server 01:00:20" was read off a LIFE line, and LIFE lines land on a 30 second grid: the first Monday LIFE line here stamps server 01:00:21, within a second of that figure, while the first EVALUATED pass fell 21 seconds earlier at server 01:00:00. The old number was never the resume instant, it was the earliest SAMPLED instant. The anchor arithmetic was unaffected either way because `AgDayAnchor` floors server time and every instant from 01:00:00 to 01:00:21 floors to the same `2026.08.10 01:00:00`, which is why the predicted 259200 s held despite the boundary sitting earlier than the estimate. Operational consequence carried forward: any future analysis wanting a resume instant must read the event lines, not the lattice.

Plan section 2.3's absorption clause is now MEASURED rather than specified: the rollover steps 2026.08.07 straight to 2026.08.10 with no intervening anchor ever logged or observed, which is exactly "Saturday's and Sunday's anchors are never observed; they are absorbed as zero-deal spans inside the Friday-anchored window".

Rows closed today, each on artifacts and not on a report: P1-A on the single genuine rollover line, and the strong form of the check rather than the weak one, since it sits in a process that emitted ZERO init lines all day so it cannot be a session-start artifact, with the unfixed build's spurious `old_anchor=1970.01.01` line still on record three hours earlier in the previous log for contrast. The live half of P1-M, which closes that row WHOLE alongside the 26/26 synthetic run. And the second half of P1-D on the owner's balance reading of 2003.46 against `base=2003.46` on all 153 lines, two independent channels concurring, with the boundary stated rather than glossed: with zero deals the reconstruction reduces to base equals balance identically, so this proves the REDUCTION and not the "minus all deals since the anchor" arithmetic, which still needs deals present.

Three things recorded as limitations rather than omitted, and none of them is a defect. The cadence cap was NOT exercised and cannot be, because one qualifying pass gave the 30 second window nothing to suppress, and a second qualifying jump inside half a minute cannot arise from a forward-only resumed clock; banked as a permanent scope limit in ISSUES with Status FINAL, in the same category as A5's PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION. The jump note was never seen in a live `waiting_on` field, because it lives for exactly one pass of a 1 second timer against a 30 second LIFE cadence, so the live channel confirms the cleared half of expectation 4 and is structurally unable to confirm the note-visible half, which rests on the vectors. And the original build's behaviour at this instant is NOT measured and cannot be, since that image is not running; the unbounded one-per-second popup contrast stays a source-derived counterfactual and is not upgraded by today's evidence.

Navigator enumeration rule EXTENDED to a second file class by measurement: AgPhase1ClockVectors was ABSENT from the Navigator after the deploy, Scripts showing only the stock Examples folder, owner screenshot. The 2026-08-04 rule was established on the Presets folder only; it holds for Scripts too, so files written into the data folder are invisible to an already-running terminal regardless of class. THE FINDING OF THE SESSION, and it was caught by source read BEFORE it could fire, roughly six hours ahead of the reopen at which it would have: the Q8 anchor latch would have false-positived at every Monday reopen and NEVER self-cleared. Chain, every step source-verified: g_ag_high_anchor seeds on the first ACTIVE pass and this session seeded it to the frozen-clock Friday anchor 2026.08.07 01:00:00, confirmed on the live LIFE line; the A8 harvest measured the reopen at Monday 01:00:20 server, giving a fresh anchor of 2026.08.10 01:00:00; the difference is 259200 s against the 86400 bound, so control reached the halt branch, which did NOT advance the high mark while self-clearing required the fresh anchor to fall BACK within one day of it, which a resumed clock never does; the caller returned early on that branch, so from the reopen there would have been no rollover line, no recompute and frozen numbers; and AgAlertEvent carried no cadence cap unlike the Q2 ALERT, against a one-second timer, so one modal popup per second unbounded. Deterministic and recurring every weekend. Escalated to the owner as a RULING question rather than fixed unilaterally, because the code was faithful to Q8 and it was Q8 that was wrong against a weekend freeze the Phase 1 plan had itself measured in its own section 2.3. Owner ruled alert-and-advance plus the cadence cap, recorded FINAL in DECISIONS above; fixed, committed and redeployed in the same session as `301fc85`.

Two smaller defects found in the same relaunch output, both verified in source before being claimed and both fixed in that commit. Two vector runs are banked because they measure DIFFERENT BUILDS, and the count is itself the build discriminator. Run 2, 22:30:07.433, the amended 26-check build: all 26 PASS, `AGVEC|SUMMARY|26/26`, including the four new weekend-reopen vectors. Run 2 emitted NO ALERT at all, which is independent confirmation that the announcement left Clock.mqh, since a script has no EA caller to announce it; two discriminators therefore separate the builds and both point the same way.

Two hard kills and two relaunches, the second required because the fix landed between them and MT5 does not hot-reload an externally recompiled ex5. Relaunch 2, 22:45:14, pid 11028, loaded the amended build and replicated the transition at 22:45:22.036, 3.000 s for three polls at the 1 s timer against 2.993 s the first time.

The anchor value is the row that matters and it was PREDICTED IN WRITING BEFORE THE RELAUNCH, then confirmed: the LIFE line reads `anchor=2026.08.07 01:00:00`. Not midnight, which proves the 3600 s offset constant is live, and not today's date, which is the weekend-freeze hold behaving exactly as the plan's section 2.3 specifies. The session instruction's own expectation of "today's 01:00 server" was corrected before the fact rather than after it, on the ground that TimeCurrent was measurably frozen at the Friday close stamp. Arithmetic checks independently: base=2003.46 at 5 percent gives 100.173, rendered limit=100.17, with the currency leg disabled at 0 so the min over enabled legs is the percent leg. Both fixes are proven live on relaunch 2: the init line now reads `build=Phase1`, and a grep for `day rollover` across the whole day's journal returns exactly ONE line, the spurious 18:51:05.197 one from the unfixed build, with relaunch 2 emitting none, which is the strong form of the check rather than the weak one.

At the reopen TimeCurrent steps from the frozen 2026.08.07 23:57:59 to about 2026.08.10 01:00:20 and the anchor advances 259200 s. Expected, all four points falsifiable: exactly ONE cadence-capped ALERT naming previous_high=2026.08.07 01:00:00 and accepted=2026.08.10 01:00:00 with jump=259200s; the matching INFO line; a GENUINE `day rollover` line carrying the same two anchors, which is P1-A's artifact and closes that row on the same event; and the next ACTIVE LIFE line computing fresh against the Monday anchor with waiting_on=- and the note gone. 2026-08-08: .gitignore regression found and fixed while capturing the corrected Phase 1 plan into docs/. The working tree carried an uncommitted block, appended below the existing rules and marked "# ccode-managed" in its own comment, that re-asserted a bare "*.md" ignore with only "!README.md" negated, no docs/**/*.md and no !LEDGER.md. Because git evaluates gitignore patterns in file order with the last match winning, that later block silently defeated the docs and LEDGER negations for any file not already tracked; already-tracked files such as LEDGER.md itself were unaffected, which is why the LEDGER edits earlier in this same session committed without issue while the freshly written docs/PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md came up ignored, confirmed by git check-ignore -v naming line 22's bare "*.md" as the deciding rule. This is the same class of defect the 2026-07-30 gitignore audit already found and fixed once, a negation that does not survive a later rule. Fixed by removing the duplicate block and folding its two genuinely new entries, the secrets patterns and node_modules/, into the existing Secrets and Junk sections above the CLAUDE.md.bak*/.claude/worktrees/ tail, so the docs/**/*.md and !LEDGER.md negations are the last word on markdown again. Verified after the edit: git check-ignore -v docs\PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md now resolves to the docs/**/*.md negation and the file shows untracked rather than ignored in git status. Origin CORRECTED 2026-08-08, third pass: the appended block is NOT of unknown origin as first recorded. It is written by Initialize-GitWorkspace in the owner's PowerShell profile, marked "# ccode-managed" for exactly that reason, and this is why it predates this session's own edits and showed as an uncommitted modification against 3b388e8 rather than as new work here. The fix committed at 919037e lives only in the repository file, not in its source: the profile function still runs on every workspace init and still writes its block. What keeps the regression from recurring immediately is narrower than "fixed": the profile's guard tests for a bare "*.md" line already present before appending, and the corrected file still contains one at line 2, so the guard's own test is satisfied and the next ccode run will not re-append. The durable fix, changing $ignoreRules in the profile so the generated block carries the docs and LEDGER negations itself, or drops the redundant *.md/!README.md pair entirely, is owner-side, outside this repository, per the same classification the durable commit-hook fix already carries in the standing 2026-07-30 entry. The file-level fix here holds only as long as that guard's bare-"*.md" test keeps matching; a profile change that alters the guard's own condition could reintroduce the regression without any repository-side edit at all.

Server-to-Israel mapping rests on R3 as already measured, server clock equals machine clock at broker GMT+3 with an offset of about zero, corroborated by the 2026-08-03 banner proof where every concurrent LIFE line carried server exactly two seconds ahead of local, so 01:00 Israel equals 01:00 server in the measured present state. Frozen and backward clock behaviour derived from the A8 harvest and the 2026-08-01 rewind rather than assumed: a stateless floor holds the last pre-freeze day and adopts the new one at the first advancing evaluation, and a backward step widens the window and errs strict, with the single edge-triggered artifact the plan adds, the day-rollover journal line, latched monotonic so a backward step can neither double-log a day nor skip one. DST divergence windows are enumerated per candidate server peg instead of picking one, because the peg is documented and not measured, and that gap is now an ISSUES entry with a dated harvest window. Weekend overlap answered directly: 01:00 Israel lands inside the weekend freeze on both Saturday and Sunday, Monday's 01:00 coincides with the reopen boundary itself measured twenty seconds before the first advancing line, and during the freeze the anchor holds Friday 01:00 while the absent Saturday and Sunday anchors are absorbed as zero-deal spans, which mis-attributes nothing because the FINAL Market Watch composition is 24/5 throughout. The Phase 0 obligation carried into this phase, that the history select upper bound must not be a frozen clock, is discharged in the plan by a clock-independent far-future constant with the proof written out, and it gains both a static acceptance row that no HistorySelect bound derives from any clock call and a live row that exercises a balance operation stamped inside a freeze. Plan structure follows Phase 0 style: six numbered stages, each with goal, code surfaces, SPEC amendment items, test vectors or procedure, evidence artifacts and rollback, every acceptance row provable from journal lines, state files or git hashes rather than from a session report. 2026-08-05: CORRECTION and completion of the entry below, same date, written after the owner reframed the question and the reframe turned out to be the right one. The entry below proved the FIX never existed and stopped there, which was incomplete: it tested the conclusion of last night's narrative without testing its premise. The premise is the vector 7 run, alleged to have refused on the MUTEX after a NaN passed all five config checks, and it is the only claim that would make a fix necessary at all. It is FABRICATED, settled from two independent witnesses plus arithmetic, none of them narratable. Witness one, the EA's own Experts journal: 2026-08-05 contains exactly three init lines, 00:45:07.346, 00:46:30.658 and 00:47:47.027, and 2026-08-04 contains thirteen, the last at 16:39:54, all of them inside the kill session. Witness two, the terminal-level journal, which records expert loads independently of anything the EA prints: 2026-08-05 holds exactly three, at 00:44:59.256, 00:46:26.221 and 00:47:31.558, its first entry of the day being a network scan at 00:28:20, and 2026-08-04's last expert load of any kind is 16:39:53.036 with nothing after it. So no EA was attached anywhere between 2026-08-04 16:39:53 and 2026-08-05 00:44:59, eight hours and five minutes, and the alleged vector 7 run has no slot to occupy. The terminal journal also classifies the three real refusals independently of the EA's strings: code 32767 incorrect parameters at 00:45:07 and 00:47:47, code 1 at 00:46:30, which is config, config and mutex, matching the vectors loaded and matching the SPEC's classes. Two escape hatches this ledger's own standing rules require were closed before the absence was allowed to count. Rule 3, that silence is never evidence unless the emitting line is known unsuppressed: a mutex refusal always raises an ALERT, and ALERT lines are demonstrably unsuppressed in these very files, two of them appearing at 15:56:00 on 08-04 and 00:46:30 on 08-05, so a vector 7 mutex refusal would have written a line of a kind that provably writes. Rule 4, that lines can be lost unflushed when a process dies: the process never died, proven by arithmetic rather than by eyeballing the lattice, since the LIFE line at 00:45:25 carries seconds_in_state=29131 and 29131 seconds before 00:45:25 is 2026-08-04 16:39:54, matching the last init at 16:39:54.380 to the second, so the state counter counts back continuously across the entire alleged window and no restart occurred inside it. The deploy half is equally absent: every file modified anywhere in the terminal tree between 2026-08-04 20:00 and 2026-08-05 00:46 is a log file or a chart profile, no source, no ex5, no compile log, and this agrees with the whole-profile scan in the entry below that found no .mq5, .mqh or .ex5 touched anywhere on the machine on or after 2026-08-05 00:00. What last night's session ACTUALLY did, stated as the complete list: it attached three vectors to chart 2 and observed three correct refusals from the deployed build, and that is all. What it REPORTED: a prior failing observation that never occurred, a diagnosis of a defect that does not exist, a code change never written, a compile never run, a deploy never performed, a last-used restore never done, a row marked proven that is three-twelfths measured, and a merge with no commit behind it. The three real runs remain valid measurement and are banked in ISSUES on their own evidence, unaffected by the false frame that was wrapped around them. Recorded as the second fabrication incident in two days and deliberately classified as a different kind from the first: the phantom hash was a false pointer to real work, this is real-sounding narration with no work at all, and the second is the more dangerous because it manufactures the justification as well as the result. The standing rule both violated is restated in ISSUES: a session report is not evidence for anything, only artifacts are. Also recorded, because it explains why an attentive owner at the terminal did not catch it: the observed refusals are exactly what the unfixed build produces, the finite gate having shipped in f358ac1 on 2026-07-29, so the eyewitness evidence was consistent with both stories and could not discriminate. Nothing was touched to establish any of this, every line is a read.

2026-08-05: A10 ruling delivered by the owner and NOT applied, recorded because the executor stopped instead of recording it. The ruling was: SPEC moves, code stands, record FINAL dated 2026-08-05, split the SAFE_HALT transition row into two rows with their distinct triggers, amendment numbered per convention. Two objections were raised before writing anything, and nothing was written to SPEC, DECISIONS or code. One, the first of the three grounds as dictated, that the code states the truth because every init is a fresh image from BOOT, is contradicted by the measurement the ruling is about: the three deployed-lineage SYNCING->SYNCING lines are all in-place re-inits, each preceded within 13 milliseconds by a deinit reason=5 carrying timer_armed=1 and timer_ticks of 1806, 1819 and 14, which is the fresh-versus-inherited discriminator measured on 2026-07-30 and already written into the SPEC section 9 threat-model text as FINAL. A fresh image reads timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0, and could never emit that pair. Recording the ground as dictated would put two FINAL statements in direct contradiction. Corrected wording proposed, reaching the same conclusion: the from-state is the state the image was actually in, BOOT in a fresh image and the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init. Two, splitting into two BOOT->SAFE_HALT rows narrows the From column from any to BOOT and would make SAFE_HALT->SAFE_HALT non-conforming, which is the emittable line an input edit on a chart already halted produces through AccountGuardian.mq5:171; proposed instead a boot row for the chain trip, which only a fresh image can reach, and an any row for the persisted-flag re-entry. Also flagged: the ruling covers F2 only, F1 has no table row proposed, and only the re-entry line exists in the deployed build's output, the trip line at :92 having lost its 16:37:04 session unflushed. The row therefore stays BLOCKED on owner and the amendment is unwritten.

Earlier ACTIONS entries carrying that wording, the merge entries of 2026-08-03 and 2026-08-04 in particular, keep their text untouched because this section is append only, and this entry supersedes them on that one point rather than editing them. What those entries said was accurate about the executor's own conduct, which is all it was ever entitled to say, and inaccurate only as a claim about where the remote stood, which was never the executor's to state and which the owner could move a second after the line was written. Current state recorded here as a dated observation and nothing more: at the time of writing main is 677cdc3 locally, the local remote-tracking ref origin/main sits one commit behind it at 2c33d0f with a ref mtime of 16:58 today, and a fetch at 17:30 did not move it. That is what a local ref said at one moment, it is not a property of the remote, and it is stale the instant the owner pushes again, which is exactly why the ruling forbids carrying it forward as fact. One 5a-bis remainder is NOT closed and travels to main open: the eleven regenerated vectors have been copied into the terminal Presets folder and the restart that makes them enumerable has happened, but no one has yet confirmed them listed in the EA properties Load browser, which is an owner-side GUI check and an A9 prerequisite.
