2026-08-04: Stage 5a steps 1 to 3, code and SPEC and vectors, third entry for the date. Three owner rulings recorded FINAL first: the negative-gap treatment in the chain walk, the vectors-into-the-repository decision with its sync direction, and the SweepPeriodSeconds wording, which closes an item that had been open since the pre-batch verification of 2026-07-30. Step 1, the primitive: AgHaltUncleanCount is replaced by AgHaltUncleanChain in Persist.mqh, a backward walk from the newest record that stops at the first clean record or the first adjacent init pair further apart than the bound. The substantive change is that TimeLocal no longer participates in the comparison at all. The old function anchored a rolling window on now, so a clean record reset nothing; the chain is anchored on adjacent init pairs only, which is exactly what makes one clean record reset it. The call site in AccountGuardian.mq5 takes the new function, the CrashLoopWindowSeconds default moves 60 to 300, and both the SAFE_HALT reason string and the verbose check line are reworded to name the chain and the gap bound rather than a window. Untouched by design: the halt file format, AgHaltAppendSession, AgHaltMarkClean, and every config refusal check. AG_MAX_SESSIONS is 32 against a worst case chain of 4, so pruning cannot truncate a chain that matters. Step 2, SPEC: amendment A4 added, superseding A1's rolling-window counting while stating explicitly that the file format is untouched, since this changes how existing records are read and not what is written; the transition table trigger, the input table defaults and note cells, the Phase 0 crash-loop acceptance row, and the two A1 crash-counting bullets are all reworded to match, and the negative-gap ruling is recorded in A4 with its grounds. Step 3, vectors: committed unmodified first as a baseline, then regenerated, so the change is an eleven-line diff rather than twelve files appearing from nowhere. One thing caught by checking rather than by assuming: the editing pass rewrote CRLF to LF on all eleven, silently. The CRLF form is the one proven to parse in the terminal, the LF form is proven for nothing, so the endings were restored byte for byte and verified by carriage-return and line-feed counts before commit. R2 concurrence, the one-line obligation the ruling attached, discharged: the measured 30 to 40 second kill cycle puts four kills' adjacent init pairs far inside the 300 second bound, chain reaches 4 against a limit of 3 and trips, no adjustment needed. Nothing deployed and no terminal file touched, per the owner's gate; the compile ran against the repository tree instead. Compile result: 0 errors, 0 warnings, 833 ms, ex5 written to the repository tree at 14:16:31, 40450 bytes against the deployed build's 39642. Deployment state verified unchanged by the same check: the terminal copy is still the 2026-08-03 01:40:37 e136858 build at 39642 bytes, so the running instance is untouched and still carries the old primitive, which is correct until the owner opens the deploy. One measurement fell out of it and is recorded as standing observation rule 6, because it would have inverted any future compile gate: the metaeditor64 exit code is not a status signal on this installation. The clean build exited 1 while logging 0 errors and 0 warnings, and a deliberately broken control copy of the same source exited 0 while logging 3 errors and producing no ex5. The Result line and the ex5 timestamp are the authoritative signals; the control was run precisely because a clean compile is a proof claim in this project and an unexplained non-zero exit is not something to wave through.

2026-08-04: Relaunch raced the mutex staleness window, procedure defect not a code defect, sixth entry for the date. The counted kill 1 worked exactly as intended and the evidence says so: exactly one terminal process exists, pid 20212 started 15:55:57, the previous pid 19732 is gone, and the halt file is untouched at mtime 15:51:08 with its newest record S|1785858668 still clean=0. So taskkill /F is the correct instrument and the record stayed unclean, which is what the Task Manager attempt failed to achieve. What went wrong is timing. A hard kill by construction skips OnDeinit, so the mutex heartbeat GV AG_HB_1200252169 is never zeroed and keeps the value the dying instance last wrote, refreshed every one second timer tick. AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS is 10, so a relaunch within ten seconds of the kill reads a heartbeat that is not yet stale, correctly concludes a live holder exists, and refuses. Measured timeline: last surviving LIFE line 15:55:18, replacement process start 15:55:57, refused init 15:56:00.063, so the heartbeat was at most a few seconds old when it was read. The refusal is the mutex working to specification, not a fault. Consequence folded into the A3 procedure: after every hard kill, wait at least fifteen seconds before relaunching, ten for the staleness threshold plus margin. A refused init appends NO session record, since AgHaltAppendSession sits downstream of the refusal return, so a raced relaunch does not advance the chain and does not corrupt it either, it simply wastes a cycle. Two things banked for free. One, the SAFE_HALT bypass fix is re-proven on the Stage 5a build under a genuine fresh-image refusal: the skip line fired naming why the halt file was not written, the deinit reported reason=8 with timer_armed=0 and timer_ticks=0 confirming a fresh image, the banner was deliberately left on the chart, and the halt file is byte-untouched with its mtime unmoved. Two, the next successful attach is now a stale-mutex takeover by construction, because the leftover heartbeat is minutes old, which is the A2 branch that the A1 row still owes and that has never been run deliberately on any build. It also serves as the discriminator against the one alternative explanation worth ruling out: if the staleness comparison were itself broken rather than merely raced, the attach would refuse again with a heartbeat two minutes old.

2026-08-04: Kill method measured WRONG before it could cost the A3 row, fifth entry for the date. Task Manager End Task on the Processes tab is NOT a hard kill for this terminal. The owner ended the task at about 15:16 and reported it as a hard kill with no clean exit, which was an honest reading of the tool's own label. The halt file says otherwise and the halt file is the authoritative shutdown witness under standing rule 4: session record 6, S|1785721251, flipped from unclean to clean=1, the file grew 138 to 139 bytes, its md5 moved 1C4336F1 to 8C4B7C97, and its mtime advanced to 15:16:25, some 42 seconds after the last LIFE line at 15:15:43. OnDeinit therefore ran to completion and marked the session clean, which means Windows delivered a graceful close request rather than terminating the process. This matches the 2026-08-03 measured clean close exactly, where a WM_CLOSE produced the same clean flag. The journal holds no deinit lines at all, zero non-LIFE lines for the whole day, which is standing rule 4's own scenario and is why the halt file rather than the journal settled it. Consequence, and it is the whole value of the pre-relaunch gate: had this method been used for the counted kills, every one would have written a clean record, the chain would have reset at every step, and A3 would have been reported as a failure to trip when in fact nothing was ever being measured. Nothing is lost, because this kill's only job was unloading the old image so the relaunch loads the new one, and it did that. Corrected method for every kill from here: taskkill /F /IM terminal64.exe, where /F terminates the process outright and gives OnDeinit no chance to run. The discriminator after each kill is the halt file, not the journal and not the tool's wording: a genuine hard kill leaves the newest record at clean=0.

One more clean re-init data point for A7, and the crash chain resets to the live session only. Harvest, and it corrects the record: the weekend window was NOT banked. The owner asserted at contact that it was; the measurement says BTCUSD.ecn stayed selected, ticked through Friday night and all of Saturday, and the server clock advanced on every one of Friday's 2880 and Saturday's 2748 LIFE lines except the routine midnight pause, 00:00:18 to 00:04:48 pinned at 2026.07.31 23:59:56. Recorded at the owner's own instruction as the fourth owner-side error this project, alongside the withdrawn item 0 accusation, the superseded 23:45 market-close figure, and the struck NOT A DEFECT preset framing. The real window opened only after the restart: BTCUSD's last tick pinned the clock at 2026.08.01 23:02:41, and the 23:26:38 reconnect, the first after the symbol's removal, rewound TimeCurrent to gold's Friday close stamp 2026.07.31 23:57:59, a backward step of about 25 hours. New environment fact, measured: TimeCurrent is not monotonic across a reconnect once the freshest symbol has left Market Watch; folded into the frozen-upper-bound entry as a Phase 1 and 2 design input. Four template auto-attach refusals were also observed post-restart, 22:54:47 on a BTCUSD chart and 22:55:03, 22:57:41, 22:58:11 on XAUUSD charts, every one harmless on 72ed960 with the skip line logged and the halt file untouched; cause and owner procedure in the template ISSUES entry. Session entries: Market Watch ruling recorded FINAL, frozen-upper-bound entry scoped per the ruling with the backward-clock addendum, A1 banner defect classified with its lifecycle design, Default.tpl finding logged, build 6090 re-verification flags folded into existing rows.

2026-07-31: Morning harvest, three findings, two of them contradicting expectations and both measured rather than argued. One: the machine neither slept nor died. The 02:30 sleep never happened: LIFE cadence runs unbroken from the 22:50:18 attach to 15:16:48 with no stamp gap above 60 seconds anywhere, seconds_in_state at 59191 counting back exactly to the attach, the same terminal pid 9056, the halt file byte-identical at md5 0D7C8493 with mtime still 22:50:17, and zero non-LIFE lines today. The two predicted sleep artefacts (seconds_in_state jump, stale mutex heartbeat on wake) never materialised because their premise never occurred; neither the slept nor the crashed branch of the morning question applies, and no refusing restore existed to check. Cause of the missed sleep not established; candidate is that some process held a wake lock overnight, not investigated further. Two: the A8 window came in BELOW THRESHOLD on 72ed960, which is not the same as failed and is worded that way on owner instruction. The only no-tick stretch all night was server frozen at 23:59:56 for 10 LIFE lines, stamps 00:00:18 to 00:04:48, resuming 00:05:19, about 5.4 minutes against the 15 minute minimum. Mechanism, and it matters beyond tonight: TimeCurrent is terminal-wide, the last known server time across ALL Market Watch symbols, and the US100.ecn and US500.ecn charts opened during last night's fix proof subscribed index symbols that tick through gold's break. The 112 minute window measured the night before existed only because gold was the quietest constraint then; the extra symbols shrank it to the sliver both markets share. Operative rule: A8's window is set by the quietest subscribed symbol, not by the chart symbol. Three: today is Friday, so the weekend close guarantees an arbitrarily long no-tick window tonight regardless of Market Watch contents; A8 collects over the weekend close with no symbol surgery needed. Standing observation rule 5 added by owner order. A8 evidence pointer: the 20260730 and 20260731 journal analysis in this entry, full scan reproducible from the logs.

---

## DECISIONS

Decision: (PROPOSAL, UNRULED 2026-08-19, raised by the executor and NOT ratified) MAIN SHOULD NOT BE CHECKED OUT ANYWHERE, so that an executor session can merge into it by owner instruction instead of dictating the merge back for the owner to type. The constraint was hit twice on 2026-08-19. Git refuses to check out a branch that is already checked out in another worktree, so with main occupied by the owner's primary checkout the executor could neither perform the merge nor commit the merge record, and both had to be dictated. That cost a round trip each time and produced `4021519`, a merge commit whose entire content is a paragraph recording the merge before it. The proposal is that the primary checkout sits on a detached HEAD or on a scratch branch, leaving main unoccupied and therefore checkoutable by whichever session holds authorization.
Reason: THE TRADE IS NOT PURELY ERGONOMIC AND THE COST SIDE IS STATED FIRST. Main being occupied is currently a SAFETY PROPERTY rather than mere friction: it is the mechanism that made it impossible for the executor to write to main at all, and freeing main removes that guard rather than relaxing it. The harness worktree isolation is a separate and independent guard which would still apply, so freeing main alone may not even be sufficient to enable executor merges, and this proposal does not answer that question. Against those costs: every merge in this project so far has needed the owner at the keyboard for mechanical reasons rather than for judgement, the judgement having already been given as the instruction, and the merge gate of 2026-08-03 governs authorization independently of who types the command. NOTHING IS DECIDED HERE. The owner rules whether the guard is worth the round trips, and no session may act on this entry while it carries the UNRULED tag.
Status: UNRULED, proposed 2026-08-19, awaiting the owner

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, taken during Stage 7 Part 1 review, after the executor reported the defect and stopped) THE RATCHET EPSILON DEFECT IS FIXED NOW, BEFORE PART 2, RATHER THAN AFTER THE STAGE. Both branch tests in `AgRatchetUpdate` go through `AG_PNL_EPSILON`, the same discipline the breach disjuncts already use. One vector is added that seeds, saves, reloads from file and asserts that no branch fires on the same live limit. Compile 0 errors 0 warnings, S1 and S2 static rows, and a scoped diff are all required of the hotfix. It commits on `worktree-phase2-stage7` as a Stage 7 hotfix. Then `Persist.mqh` and ONLY `Persist.mqh` is redeployed, recompiled and restarted, and the boot path row is re closed against the new `.ex5` hash before Part 2 begins.
Reason: Owner's ruling. The executor had deferred the fix on the argument that changing the build mid acceptance invalidates rows already banked against `3ad7ef4`, and the ruling accepts that cost and pays it deliberately rather than carrying the defect through the live breach. The grounds are in the finding itself: the WARN the defect emits is the same WARN the raised-limit-while-locked row must close on, so running that row against this build would have produced evidence that cannot discriminate between the mechanism working and the mechanism misfiring. The re close of the boot path row against the new hash is what keeps the ledger honest about which binary each row was proven on.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Stage 3 item A) `AG_QUOTE_FROZEN_SECONDS = 120` IS RATIFIED. The frozen quote threshold that ruling THREE left unnamed is 120 seconds, a compile time constant and deliberately not an input. The UNRULED marker carried in the source since the stage was written is removed and the grounds are recorded at the constant itself.
Reason: Owner's ruling. Ruling THREE named the frozen clock CONDITION as the trigger for the extra day on `locked_until` and named no threshold, which is the one thing an implementer could not derive from it, so the value arrives on its own ruling rather than by inheritance from the four part proposal whose threshold the stale quote ruling had declared MOOT in its role as a gate. Grounds carried with the value: 120 seconds cannot false fire on XAUUSD in an open session where ticks arrive many times a second, and it catches the 62 minute break measured on the night of 2026-08-13 within two LIFE lines. It stays a constant rather than becoming a ninth input on the AG_LIFE_INTERVAL and AG_MUTEX_STALE precedent, that a value able to disable a guarantee belongs to the core class or nowhere, and because A9 established that every input carries a full validation vector cost.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Stage 3 item C) `AgLockedUntilComputed` AND `AgLockedUntilFromWitness` MOVE INTO `Clock.mqh` so scripts can reach them, and Clock.mqh's static acceptance row is rerun afterwards. Executed the same session: both functions now live in `Clock.mqh` alongside `AgNextDayAnchor` and the Q8 latch they read, together with a single `AgLatchFloor` and `AgApplyLatchFloor` pair so ruling FOUR's floor has one definition rather than a copy in each helper. The static row was rerun and PASSES, zero forbidden clock calls in code, the only textual hits being the header contract and the comments that cite it.
Reason: Owner's ruling. The two helpers encode three FINAL rulings, Q1's base, ruling THREE's extra day and ruling FOUR's floor, plus the clamp's domain, and they are pure functions of their arguments and the latch, which is exactly the shape a synthetic vector proves best. While they sat in the EA no script could include them, so the highest risk arithmetic in Stage 3 was provable by source reading and a clean compile alone while every other Stage 2 obligation had a live vector. The rejected alternative was to leave them in the EA and accept that these rulings are provable only by a live breach, which needs a real losing trade and would have ridden the same events P1-I, P1-L and P1-N have waited on since Phase 1. The cost the ruling accepts is touching a file Phase 1 froze and that carries its own static acceptance row, which is why the ruling attaches the rerun to the move rather than leaving it to be noticed.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, precedence between the question FOUR floor and the 2026-07-30 clamp) THE FLOOR WINS. When Q8's latch next day anchor is LATER than `AgNextDayAnchor(AgServerNow())`, `locked_until` takes the FLOOR value. The two bounds are not in competition once their domains are stated, and the ruling states them: the 2026-07-30 CLAMP REMAINS IN FORCE FOR WITNESS SUPPLIED VALUES as an upper bound against a forged or inflated input, unchanged and not weakened; the QUESTION FOUR FLOOR governs the guardian's OWN COMPUTED VALUE, and it also governs ANY WITNESS VALUE THAT WOULD OTHERWISE FALL BELOW IT. This answers the item the question FOUR entry below explicitly flagged as raised and not ruled, and closes the ISSUES entry that carried it.
Reason: Owner's ruling. It resolves the one case where the two bounds genuinely pointed opposite ways, a clock stepped backward, which is the only condition question FOUR was made for: the clamp's ceiling is derived from the rewound clock itself, so letting it win would have cut the lock back using the very reading the floor exists to defend against, restoring the instant unlock. Giving each bound a domain rather than an ordering is what makes the result well defined instead of a tie break: the clamp answers "an outside value claims a lock further out than the system could legitimately produce", and the floor answers "some value, computed or supplied, would end the lock sooner than the monotone latch allows". DERIVED CONSEQUENCE, recorded because Stage 5 will need it in exactly this form and it follows from the ruling rather than adding to it: for a witness supplied value the two apply in order, clamp first as the upper bound, floor second as the lower bound, so the floor wins any conflict by being applied last; for a value the guardian computes itself only the floor applies. Rejected by the ruling: clamp wins, which would have left the rewound clock case unprotected while appearing to be the conservative choice.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question THREE) ANCHOR STRADDLE, A FLOOR ON `locked_until` FOR ONE NAMED CONDITION. A breach declared while `TimeCurrent` is FROZEN inside the pre anchor break sets `locked_until` to THE ANCHOR AFTER THE IMMINENT ONE, a full trading day, not the handful of minutes left until the anchor that is about to pass. The frozen clock condition is the trigger, detected by the same mechanism the observability note uses, ratified as non gating in question SIX below, which measures a local tick age rather than doing clock arithmetic. Q1 STAYS FINAL and is not reopened by this: the anchor itself is still computed exactly as Q1 ruled, and this adds a floor on the lock duration under one named condition rather than changing what an anchor is.
Reason: Owner's ruling. Without it the 2026-08-18 stale quote ruling, which makes a breach inside the nightly freeze valid and enforceable, produces a lock that expires almost immediately: the freeze measured on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14 ran 62 minutes straight into the 01:00 anchor, so a breach at 00:58 would lock until 01:00 and the new day would open carrying the same loss with the lock already spent. That is enforcement in name only, and it lands precisely in the window the stale quote ruling exists to keep enforceable. CONSISTENCY WITH THE 2026-08-18 STALE QUOTE FINAL, recorded because both entries read the same detector and a later session will check: that entry forbids the quote age mechanism from suppressing, delaying or gating a DECISION, and this ruling does none of the three. The breach is still declared from the frozen quote exactly as that entry requires; the detector's output is read only to size the consequence after the decision has already been taken.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question FOUR) `locked_until` IS NEVER BELOW `AgNextDayAnchor` OF Q8'S HIGH WATER LATCH. A floor is added at that value. The clamp from above, ratified 2026-07-30, which caps any witness supplied `locked_until` at `AgNextDayAnchor(AgServerNow())`, STAYS. `locked_until` is therefore bounded on both sides from here on.
Reason: Owner's ruling. A breach declared after a backward clock step could compute a `locked_until` that is already in the past, which unlocks instantly and silently, and the 2026-07-30 clamp could not catch it because it bounds the value from above only. Q8's high water latch is the right floor because it never recedes by construction, so it supplies a monotone reference even while the clock itself is moving backward. FLAGGED, not ruled here, and raised as its own ISSUES entry rather than resolved by inference: under a rewound clock the floor and the clamp can point in opposite directions, since the latch's next day anchor is then LATER than `AgNextDayAnchor(AgServerNow())`, and nothing yet states which wins. That is the exact condition this ruling was made for, so it needs an owner answer before implementation rather than an implementer's guess.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question FIVE) THE CARRY DRAG FRAMING IS TOTAL EXPOSURE. The daily limit counts ALL open floating loss regardless of when it was earned. NO floating baseline snapshot is taken at the anchor and NO new persisted artifact is added. RE LOCKING EACH DAY ON A HELD LOSER IS INTENDED BEHAVIOUR, not a defect, and no later session may treat it as one. This closes the carry drag entry that has been open as a Phase 2 input since 2026-08-14 and scoped FINAL on 2026-08-17.
Reason: Owner's ruling. It keeps today's measured behaviour rather than changing it: the Q2 identity already produces exactly this, so the ruling adds no mechanism and needs no code that does not already exist. It is the prop firm style reading of a daily drawdown rule, where the limit governs the account's total exposure at any moment rather than attributing loss to the day that produced it. The rejected framing, the day measuring only what it produced, would have required a floating baseline snapshotted at every anchor, which means a new persisted artifact, a new corruption and staleness policy for it, and a second source of truth about the day that could disagree with the first. The consequence is accepted with eyes open and is worth restating so it is never re raised as a bug: a multi day drawdown on a position held across anchors can lock the account, unlock at the anchor, and lock again the same day, every day, for as long as it is held, and the measured 2026-08-14 case where a new day opened at 46 percent of its allowance already spent is the intended outcome, not a symptom.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question SIX) THE OBSERVABILITY ITEMS SHIP INSIDE PHASE 2, NOT AS A SEPARATE LATER PASS. Three items, named: RESYNC event lines; the DEGRADED marker on SYNCING and on LOCKED while disconnected; and the NON GATING quote age note. STAGE 6 IS AUTHORIZED as a stage of the Phase 2 plan. The quote age note remains non gating exactly as the 2026-08-18 stale quote entry requires, and shipping it inside Phase 2 changes nothing about that: it may log, and it may never suppress, delay or gate a decision.
Reason: Owner's ruling. The defect it answers is measured, not hypothetical: while disconnected but in SYNCING the EA carries no DEGRADED marker at all, so seven SYNCING lines emitted entirely offline on 2026-08-14 are indistinguishable from healthy ones to any reader. That breaks the fail visible property the A3 proof of life ruling exists to protect, where a stuck guardian and a healthy one must never look identical from outside. Deferring it to a pass after Phase 2 would ship an enforcing build whose state is unreadable from outside during exactly the conditions under which enforcement matters most, which is when the terminal is disconnected or resyncing. Question THREE also depends on the same detector being present, so deferring would have left that ruling with nothing to read.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question SEVEN) THE RATCHET MECHANISM IS RATIFIED. Five elements, as ruled: a single scalar `floor_currency`; persisted as `floor_<login>.dat` written through `AgAtomicWrite`; RESEED AT ROLLOVER; NO RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP; and `min(limit, floor)` applying PRE BREACH ONLY. STAGE 5 EXISTS. This closes the last mechanism question left by the 2026-07-30 entry that made the ratchet BUILD FINAL while leaving its mechanism unruled, and the standing warning that the proposal "is authority for nothing until ratified" is now spent.
Reason: Owner's ruling. PROVENANCE OF THE CITATION, recorded in the discipline the provenance correction FINAL of this same date requires, and it does not weaken the ruling in any way. The ruling cites "section 6 of the kickoff plan". NO PHASE 2 KICKOFF PLAN FILE EXISTS: its capture was made conditional on owner authorization that was never given, and this ledger already records that the plan was never captured to a file, so the citation cannot resolve to a document. The proposal actually being ratified is the one in `docs/DESIGN_PHASE1_2.md` under the heading `Active-state limit inflation: ratchet defense`, inside the 2026-07-30 addendum, which is where four of the five elements above are written in these words, and `AgAtomicWrite` is real and reachable at `MQL5/Include/AccountGuardian/Persist.mqh:67`. THE ONE EXCEPTION, called out so no session hunts for it and concludes the document is authoritative but incomplete: NO RESEED ON A BACKWARD STEP appears nowhere in that section, which is silent on backward clock movement entirely. It is ruled here for the first time. It is an ADDITION to the proposal, not a ratification of it, and it is consistent with question FOUR of the same date, which also refuses to let a backward step move a bound.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question ONE) THE OWNER PERFORMS EVERY COPY INTO THE TERMINAL DATA FOLDER, AT THE EXECUTOR'S DICTATION. RULE A IS NOT AMENDED and gains no deploy carve out, which is the half of this ruling most likely to be misread later: the executor's prohibition on writing, deleting or moving anything under the MT5 Terminal data folder stands exactly as it was, and the deploy stage is unblocked by moving the hands rather than by loosening the rule. The dictation protocol, which is the operative part: for each file the executor names the SOURCE FILE, its DESTINATION PATH and its MD5, and the owner performs the copy and confirms. Every deploy and every test vector copy runs through it, with no exception for a file the executor considers trivial and no batching that would let a confirmation cover a file the owner did not see named.
Reason: Owner's ruling. RULE A is FINAL and the collision was between a stage that needs files in the Terminal folder and a rule that forbids the executor from putting them there, so the two candidate resolutions were to amend the rule or to move the writing hand. The owner chose the hand. The md5 is what makes it a protocol rather than an instruction to copy something: it gives the owner an independent check that the file that landed is the file that was named, and it gives the ledger a value that can be quoted later when a deployed build's behaviour is in question. Rejected: an explicit deploy carve out in RULE A, which would have created a standing executor write path into the Terminal folder for the sake of convenience during one stage, and would have made every later session's read of RULE A conditional.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, Phase 2 open question TWO) THE PHASE 2 INTERIM POSTURE IS SANCTIONED EXPLICITLY. A Phase 2 build LOCKS THE STATE MACHINE BUT SENDS NO ORDER. On a real breach the account shows LOCKED with Alerts, and the positions stay open until the owner closes them by hand; the floating loss keeps moving in the meantime and that is accepted, not a defect to be fixed inside Phase 2. Accepted for the demo build window and on the same footing as the Phase 1 alert only sanction ruled under Q2 on 2026-08-08. SWEEP, FLATTEN AND PENDING ORDER DELETION REMAIN PHASE 3 and no Phase 2 work may reach for them, including as a convenience or a safety net.
Reason: Owner's ruling. The gap is real and was raised precisely because a locked account with open positions is not what "enforcement" sounds like: Phase 2 can refuse to let the state machine leave LOCKED, but the capability to close a position lands in Phase 3, so between breach and the owner's manual close the loss is unbounded by the guardian. The precedent is exact rather than approximate, which is why this is sanctioned on the same footing: Phase 1 shipped a window where a real breach could only alert, the owner sanctioned that window explicitly under Q2 instead of letting it be an unstated consequence, and this is the same shape one stage later with more of the machine present. Naming it FINAL means no later session treats the open positions as a bug against Phase 2 or quietly adds an order send to close the gap. Rejected: pulling sweep or flatten forward into Phase 2 to make enforcement complete, which would put order sending into a build whose state machine is itself the thing under test.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner reversal 2026-08-18, on an entry the owner unlocked explicitly by writing `unlock decision` with the entry name, which is the only route protocol rule 5 permits) `CLAUDE.md` RETURNS TO UNTRACKED, REVERSING THE TRACKING RULING DIRECTLY BELOW. `LEDGER.md` and `README.md` stay tracked exactly as before; nothing about them changes. The `!CLAUDE.md` negation is removed from `.gitignore`, the sentinel line stays first, and `CLAUDE.md` is removed from the index with `git rm --cached` so the working copy is left in place and untouched. The entry this reverses is left intact and unstruck, following the provenance correction precedent set earlier the same day: the record shows both rulings and the order they were made in, rather than hiding that the first one existed.
Reason: Owner's reversal, recorded as given. The reason is that publishing the file defeats the rule the file itself states. `CLAUDE.md` instructs `README.md` to never mention the lifecycle protocol or any AI workflow, and the repository is public, so committing `CLAUDE.md` would publish the instruction to conceal alongside a full description of what is being concealed. The tracking ruling's own argument, recoverability, is met without git: the owner's profile copies `CLAUDE.md` to `~/.claude/backups` on every launch, so the file is not one bad overwrite from gone, which was the concern that motivated tracking it in the first place. Rejected: keeping it tracked and accepting the disclosure, since the secrecy rule is not decorative and a public copy makes it unenforceable; and tracking a redacted `CLAUDE.md`, which would put a knowingly incomplete protocol file in the one place future sessions are most likely to trust.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18) ALL THREE PROTOCOL FILES ARE TRACKED IN GIT: `README.md`, `LEDGER.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. `CLAUDE.md` was previously excluded by the blanket `*.md` rule in `.gitignore`, which negated the other two and not it, so it lived only in the owner's working copy. It is now negated by `!CLAUDE.md` and committed for the first time. This does not change what any of the three files contain, and in particular it does not touch the secrecy rule: `README.md` still never references `LEDGER.md`, the lifecycle protocol, or any AI workflow.
Reason: Owner's ruling, recorded as given. The original intent was that the protocol files stay local, and that intent is no longer recoverable: `LEDGER.md` is already tracked and already pushed publicly, so the file that carries every issue, action and locked decision is public while the far shorter file that merely states the rules was the one being withheld. Given that, the deciding consideration is recoverability. A file that governs every session should be restorable from git rather than existing in exactly one working copy where a bad overwrite or a lost machine ends it, which is the property `LEDGER.md` and `README.md` already have and the one `CLAUDE.md` lacked. Rejected: keeping `CLAUDE.md` local, which preserved a secrecy posture that the public `LEDGER.md` had already made moot while leaving the protocol itself the single most fragile artifact in the project.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, authorized explicitly after the executor raised the defect and waited) PROVENANCE CORRECTION AGAINST THE ENTRY BELOW, THE STALE QUOTE RULING COMMITTED AT `f814df2`. That entry misstates its own provenance. It claims the owner ruling was "recorded at the Phase 2 kickoff before any design work," and that is not what happened. The kickoff instruction explicitly ordered the executor to surface the question and wait. No owner answer was given inside that session before the entry was written. The owner confirmed the content, approach B, act on the last known price while connected, in a separate channel after the entry already existed. THE SUBSTANCE OF THAT RULING STANDS UNCHANGED AND IS NOT REOPENED BY THIS ENTRY: enforcement is not gated on quote freshness while connected, Q10's DEGRADED behaviour on a genuine disconnect still blocks every decision, and the four part mechanism survives as an observability proposal only. Only the stated timing is corrected. The original entry is deliberately left intact and unstruck, so the record shows both what was written and what was wrong with it. STANDING RULE, and it is the reason this correction is FINAL rather than a footnote: no session may cite its own draft as an already-given ruling, even when the content later turns out correct. A ruling is owner spoken or it does not exist, and an entry that describes its own authorship must describe it accurately.
Reason: The content being right does not make the provenance right, and provenance is what every later session relies on when it treats an entry as binding. An entry that can invent its own authority once can do it again on a ruling the owner would not have given, and nothing downstream would catch it, because the only evidence a later session has is the entry's own account of itself. Correcting by appending rather than by editing keeps the failure visible, which is the point: striking the original would erase the very thing this entry exists to record. Rejected: amending or rewriting the `f814df2` entry in place, which would have hidden the defect and left no trace that it occurred; and leaving the correction in ISSUES as an unratified note, which would have made the standing rule advisory rather than binding.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-18, recorded at the Phase 2 kickoff before any design work) A breach decision MAY be taken from a quote the guardian cannot prove is fresh, as long as the terminal is CONNECTED. The stale quote gap, connected but quotes frozen, for example the nightly metals break, does NOT block enforcement: the guardian acts on the last known price exactly as if it were current. This is unchanged from and consistent with Q10's existing DEGRADED behaviour on a genuine DISCONNECT, which still blocks all decisions. The four part detection mechanism proposed at Phase 1 close is DOWNGRADED from a gating mechanism to an OBSERVABILITY item only: it may log or flag a stale but connected quote for the record, but it must never suppress, delay, or gate a breach decision.
Reason: Owner's ruling, recorded as given. The boundary it draws is the connection state, which is the boundary Q10 already enforces: disconnected means no enforcement action is possible and no decision is taken, while connected means the last known price is the best available number and enforcement acts on it. Gating enforcement on quote age would have opened a recurring nightly no-enforcement window during the broker's scheduled break, roughly one hour every trading day, straddling the 01:00 anchor.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) THE README TOKEN USAGE BLOCK IS REMOVED PERMANENTLY. Two obligations, not one, and the ruling is not satisfied by either alone. First, the block is deleted from README.md including its `<!--TOKENS-->` and `<!--/TOKENS-->` delimiters, done at commit `b8678f1`. Second, the job that writes it is disabled at its source, which is owner side because the writer lives outside this repository at `C:\Users\www83\.claude\token-tracker\` and is driven by the Windows Task Scheduler task `ClaudeTokenReport`. The executor's standing instruction on this ruling is to name the writer's location and stop, never to reach outside the repository and disable it. No future session re adds the block, re creates the delimiters, or treats a returning block as content to preserve; a block found at the top of README.md after this date is the un disabled job firing again and is deleted on sight.
Reason: The block named AI model identifiers inside the one file the protocol says must never reference AI involvement, which is why the owner already ruled it dropped once on 2026-08-08. It came back because that ruling was applied to the file and not to the writer, and the writer is unconditional: measured in its own source this session, `Update-Readme` replaces the block in place when the delimiters are present and PREPENDS the whole block when they are absent, so a hand deletion is the very input that makes it re author. The 2026-08-11 entry already flagged the block as back on main and named the durable fix as owner side; nothing acted on it, and on 2026-08-17 the collision between a hand deletion and a bot rewrite reached main as three live conflict markers in a public repository. That is the second time the same cause produced a defect, so the ruling is written as two obligations to make the file level half insufficient on its own.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) THE GATEGUARD FACT FORCING HOOK IS A PROJECT TOOL FEATURE, NOT A DEFECT, AND IS DELIBERATELY KEPT ON. It intercepts edits and first Bash calls and refuses them until the session states, in the open, which files depend on the target, which public symbols the change touches, the structure and date format of any data written, and the user's instruction quoted verbatim. No session reports it as a bug, works around it, or disables it. The recovery hint the hook itself prints, setting `ECC_GATEGUARD=off` or adding its rule name to `ECC_DISABLED_HOOKS`, is explicitly out of bounds here, and it is named in this entry rather than left unmentioned so that a session reading the hook's own output cannot mistake the escape for a sanctioned route.
Reason: The gate enforces at the tool boundary the same discipline this ledger's gravest rulings enforce in prose. A session report is not evidence, only artifacts are, and a row closes only on evidence present on disk at a stated path; both rules failed twice in this project not because they were unwritten but because nobody applied them, which is the same failure mode Rule B was written to remove when it replaced a caution with a hard boundary. A gate that will not let the work start until the facts are on the record is that construction applied to editing. It paid this session rather than merely costing: answering its first question drove the executor into the badge writer's source deep enough to find the prepend branch, which converted the README ruling from a file deletion that would have silently reverted overnight into the two part obligation recorded above.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) PHASE 1 IS CLOSED. Row tally over the sixteen acceptance rows in docs/PLAN_PHASE1_PNL_CORE.md. CLOSED ON EVIDENCE, eight: P1-A and P1-M and P1-D on the 2026-08-10 Monday reopen, P1-J on the 2026-08-09 SYNCING exit observed twice on two builds, P1-E and P1-G and P1-H on the 2026-08-14 morning block, P1-F on the 2026-08-16 weekend freeze deposit closed by the owner's balance reading of 2133.13. CLOSED BY OWNER RULING this date, three: P1-B, P1-C, P1-O, each for the reasons in its own entry below. PASS BY CONSTRUCTION, two: P1-E' on the Q5 withdrawal ruling and the Q2 algebraic identity, and P1-K as ruled below. OPEN BY NATURE, three: P1-I, P1-L and P1-N, all of which require manual trading events that have not occurred and none of which blocks Phase 1, since each measures behaviour the Q2 identity already governs and none gates the Phase 2 work. DEFERRED TO PHASE 2 as inputs rather than defects, three: the stale quote gap, the carry drags prior floating question, and the RESYNC observability item. Stated plainly for the record: the merge to main was OWNER ORDERED, and P1-B is closed by ruling rather than by its literal harvest.
Reason: The Phase 1 objective was the PnL core, the day anchor and the observation surface, and every mechanism the phase exists to prove is now measured on artifacts: the anchor advances and holds and adopts, deposits are neutral, the whitelist holds, restart reconstruction rebuilds from history alone, the day rollover is absorbed inside ACTIVE, and the guardian survives sleep in both the frozen and the live class. The three open by nature rows are not evidence gaps of the same kind; they wait on a human placing losing trades, which is a scheduling matter and not a build risk. Holding the phase open on them would misrepresent what is unknown.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) P1-B IS CLOSED. The weekend absorption row closes on reconstructed plus recovered evidence, with the sleep hole named rather than hidden.
Reason: The property the row tests is proven beyond dispute and by a route that does not depend on continuous observation. Monday's reopen reports `previous_high=2026.08.14 01:00:00` and `old_anchor=2026.08.14 01:00:00` with `jump=259200s`, and Q8's latch is a high water mark, so any rollover that had fired on Saturday or Sunday would necessarily have raised previous_high and shortened the jump below three days. Neither occurred, so no anchor movement is possible anywhere in the interval, including inside windows nobody watched. Saturday is a perfect lattice, 2880 LIFE lines at exactly 30 seconds with zero gaps and zero non LIFE lines. The Sunday record is complete again following the owner's recovery. The 3 hour 18 minute machine sleep is an OBSERVATION gap and not a BEHAVIOUR gap: the EA was suspended, could not have emitted a rollover line, and demonstrably resumed with its anchor and its state counter intact. The executor recommended a re harvest on the ground that the row's subject is an uninterrupted observation; the owner overrules that, and the ground for overruling is recorded here so a later session can weigh it: a re harvest would produce a cleaner artifact but could not produce a stronger proof, since the high water latch argument is already conclusive.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) P1-C IS CLOSED on criterion met. The row names a kill and a clean close was performed instead, and that difference does not touch what the row measures.
Reason: The row's criterion is that PnL figures agree within 0.01 before and after a restart with deals present, and the 2026-08-14 restart met it exactly, rebuilding base to 1985.97 from history alone on the arithmetic 2033.13 minus -52.84 minus 100.00, with the wrong answers 2085.97 and a realized of +47.16 both absent. A clean close and a kill differ only in the halt flag written on the way down and in the crash chain counter, neither of which participates in the reconstruction the row measures: reconstruction reads account balance and deal history from the server, not the prior process's exit state. The executor was right to refuse to close it on its own authority, since widening a row is an owner act, and the owner now widens it.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) P1-K IS RECLASSIFIED AS PASS BY CONSTRUCTION and is not an open row.
Reason: The row asks for a false HistorySelect return observed via a mid session disconnect. The Q10 connection gate at AccountGuardian.mq5:121 tests TERMINAL_CONNECTED and returns before any history call is reached, with the same guard repeated at Pnl.mqh:178, so a disconnect can never drive execution into HistorySelect at all. This was measured, not argued: the whole 2026-08-14 journal contains zero WARN lines across a deliberate four minute cut. The branch the row exists to protect is therefore proven unreachable on the named trigger, which is the same category as amendment A5 and the Q8 cadence cap. Recording it as a permanent scope limit rather than an open row is the honest classification, and the protective intent behind it, that bad numbers must never pass silently during a history failure, is carried by the DEGRADED marker on the disconnect path.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) P1-O IS CLOSED on its met clauses, and its two unmet items are reclassified as ONE Phase 2 observability item rather than as defects.
Reason: Two of the row's four clauses are met on runs that cannot be contaminated, the natural weekend disconnects of 2026-08-16, where the DEGRADED marker appeared on both the waiting_on field and the numbers field group with exact boundaries and zero ALERTs, twice. The RESYNC clause is not closable as written and the reason is measurement, not behaviour: the gate's window is about three seconds at a one second timer with three stable polls, while the RESYNC prefix is printed only on LIFE lines thirty seconds apart, and no event driven line announces entry or exit. Five live disconnects have now produced zero RESYNC observations, which is the expected outcome at roughly one in ten odds per event and is therefore evidence about the observation channel rather than about the gate. The SYNCING while disconnected DEGRADED omission belongs with it because both concern the same question, what a LIFE line tells a reader about the trustworthiness of its own numbers. Both go to Phase 2 as one observability item. Neither is a defect in the Phase 1 PnL core and neither blocks it.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) THE STALE QUOTE GAP AND THE CARRY DRAGS PRIOR FLOATING QUESTION REMAIN OPEN as Phase 2 inputs and are NOT Phase 1 blockers.
Reason: Both are real and both were measured, the stale quote gap over a 62 minute connected but frozen window on the night of 2026-08-13 into 2026-08-14, and the carry drag at the 2026-08-14 rollover where a new day opened at -45.65 against a limit of -99.30 on a loss that day did not produce. Neither harms Phase 1, which only observes and enforces nothing. Both bite in Phase 2, which locks: a stale quote could drive a lock on an hour old price, and a carry drag could attribute a multi day drawdown to whichever day it crosses on. They are therefore correctly scoped as enforcement design questions, to be ruled when the enforcement semantics are designed and not before. The detection mechanism proposed in ISSUES on 2026-08-14 remains a proposal and is NOT ruled by this entry.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-17) GOVERNANCE INCIDENT. On 2026-08-17 the executor destroyed a primary artifact inside the MetaTrader Terminal data folder, a location it had NO authority to modify. No one authorized it and no one could have, because nothing in this project ever granted an executor write access to that folder. That the deletion was accidental, an alias collision in which a helper named `Rd` was shadowed by the built in AllScope alias for `Remove-Item`, does not reduce it. The defect is that a write path to that folder existed inside an executor session at all. Recovery succeeded only because the OWNER holds elevated rights the executor lacks, through Windows Previous Versions, which is exactly the separation that must remain. Two rules, FINAL, binding on every session from now on. RULE A: the MetaTrader Terminal data folder is READ ONLY territory for every executor session, without exception. Permitted: read, and copy OUT to the repository evidence directory. Prohibited: any write, delete, rename, move or in place edit of anything under it, by any means, including helper functions, aliases, pipelines, cleanup, or "harmless" tidying. Analysis runs on the copied snapshot, never on the source. RULE B: every command that names a path inside that folder must be a plain inline read only cmdlet, Get-Content, Copy-Item source to repository, Get-ChildItem, Get-FileHash, with no user defined helper anywhere in the call chain. At the start of any session that touches the folder, run Get-Alias for rd, del, ri, rm, mv, cp and sc and confirm none appears in any command about to execute. A VIOLATION OF RULE A IS A GOVERNANCE INCIDENT OF THE SAME CLASS AS FABRICATION, recorded and escalated the same way, regardless of whether data was lost.
Reason: The severity is not about the data, which was recovered, but about the authority. The project's entire evidentiary standard rests on artifacts being immutable records that an executor reads and never authors or alters, which is the same reason fabrication is the gravest offence here. An executor able to write to the artifact source can, in principle, destroy or alter the record that constrains it, and the fact that this instance was accidental is a matter of luck rather than of design. The rules are written as a hard territorial boundary rather than as a caution because a caution failed: the executor already knew to be careful and still deleted the file, since the danger was invisible at the point of use, an alias silently outranking a function. Rule B exists because a rule that depends on remembering to be careful is the kind that just failed; it removes the class of construction in which the danger can hide. The alias audit run when this ruling was recorded confirms the hazard is live: rd, del, ri and rm all resolve to Remove-Item, mv to Move-Item, cp to Copy-Item and sc to Set-Content, four of them AllScope.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-05) A SESSION REPORT IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR ANYTHING, ONLY ARTIFACTS ARE. No LEDGER entry may assert a commit, a merge, a deploy, a compile, or any other git or filesystem action without the writing session re-deriving it from git or the filesystem in that same session, with hashes and paths quoted. The rule binds the owner's channels as well as the executor's, so a claim arriving from any direction is recorded only once an artifact has been read for it.
Reason: Two incidents in two days, both of which the existing closure standard would have caught had anyone applied it. The first was a phantom commit hash, a false pointer to work that did exist. The second was the A9 session of 2026-08-05, which invented the observation that motivated the work, then the work itself, and neither the fix nor the run that supposedly justified it had ever happened. Neither incident defeated the standard that a row closes only on evidence at a stated path; both bypassed it by being believed. Recorded consequence, and the reason the rule is written as a procedure rather than an exhortation: an eyewitness account of a correct outcome cannot discriminate between a true cause and a fabricated one, which is exactly how the owner's own terminal observation of three correct refusals was consistent with a fix that was never written.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-05, superseding the owner's earlier unapplied A10 ruling whose stated ground was measured false) On the two A10 transition-table divergences the SPEC moves and the code stands. Corrected ground: the from-state of a transition line is the state the program image was actually in, BOOT in a fresh image and the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init. F1: the boot to SYNCING row's From cell widens to "boot in a fresh image, or the surviving prior state in an in-place re-init". F2: the any to SAFE_HALT row splits in two, a boot to SAFE_HALT row for the chain trip per A4, and an any to SAFE_HALT row for a halt flag persisted from an earlier session per section 8. A third edit rides along: the SAFE_HALT action cell changes from "banner + periodic Alert" to "banner + one Alert at entry or re-entry", matching the code. One amendment, numbered per convention, applied to the SPEC in the same session, with this ruling recorded before the edit.
Reason: The earlier ground, that every init is a fresh image from BOOT, is contradicted by the measurement the ruling is about: the three deployed-lineage SYNCING to SYNCING lines are in-place re-inits, each preceded within 13 milliseconds by a deinit reason=5 carrying timer_armed=1 and tick counts of 1806, 1819 and 14, while a fresh image reads zero on both. Recording it as dictated would have put two FINAL statements in direct contradiction, since the fresh-versus-inherited discriminator is already FINAL in the section 9 threat model. On F2 the split keeps the From column at "any" for the re-entry row deliberately, because narrowing it to boot would make SAFE_HALT to SAFE_HALT non-conforming, and that line is emittable through AccountGuardian.mq5:171 from an input edit on an already halted chart. The behaviour in both findings was always correct and is required by section 8; only the table failed to enumerate the triggers.
Status: FINAL

Decision: Target stack is MQL5, account-level Expert Advisor (not per-trade, not per-strategy).
Reason: Need single risk backstop across all EAs/manual trades on one account.
Status: FINAL

Decision: EA cannot close terminal or disconnect from broker. Max enforcement is closing positions, deleting pending orders, blocking new opens.
Reason: Platform/API limitation, not a design choice.
Status: FINAL

Decision: Library/framework for MQL5 implementation not yet chosen.
Status: REVISIT

Decision: (Q1, owner ruling 2026-07-29) DAILY_BREACH locked_until = next day anchor.
Reason: Makes the lock fully derivable from server history, enabling the derived-lock defense (REVIEW_v0 F4).
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q2/F1, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Percent-limit base = current balance minus the sum of ALL deals since the day anchor, trading and balance types alike, which equals the day-anchor balance reconstructed live, no caching. Supersedes the charter text "balance minus realized".
Reason: The original text fails the charter's own deposit-neutrality acceptance test in both directions, including a false-positive lock on withdrawal.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q3/F2, owner ruling 2026-07-29) All trade-disallowed states (AutoTrading off, MQL_TRADE_ALLOWED false, ACCOUNT_TRADE_ALLOWED or ACCOUNT_TRADE_EXPERT false, investor login, symbol closed or close-only) are kill-equivalent: detect-only, continuous loud alerting (journal + Alert + chart banner), immediate sweep on restoration, each state enumerated and logged distinctly. The flatten promise is formally: within seconds of the platform accepting a close for that symbol.
Reason: MQL5 cannot trade in these states. Platform ceiling, not a design choice.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q4/F9, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Config classes. Core (daily limit, lock behavior) malformed, or both limits zero -> OnInit returns INIT_PARAMETERS_INCORRECT, EA visibly refuses to run. Optional (weekly reporting, cosmetics) malformed -> feature off + WARN. The charter's blanket "malformed = off + WARN" clause now applies to optional config only.
Reason: A silently unprotected guardian is the worst failure mode this product can have.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q5/F16, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Breach flatten and the locked sweep delete pending orders as well as positions.
Reason: A resting stop otherwise fills straight into a flatten with spread and commission churn. Already within the FINAL maximum-enforcement decision.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q6/F7, owner ruling 2026-07-29) At breach, limit and base are snapshotted into the state file; the locked window is judged by the snapshot, never by live inputs. An input change while locked is logged loudly. Residual bypass (file and GV destruction combined with input inflation) is accepted friction, documented in the threat model.
Reason: Otherwise editing one input defeats the lock.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q7/F5, owner ruling 2026-07-29) All expiry and anchor decisions use TimeCurrent exclusively. Never TimeTradeServer, never TimeLocal. A dead-market expiry waits for the next server update.
Reason: TimeTradeServer is forgeable via the local Windows clock. The waiting deviation errs locked.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q8, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Dual limits, percent and fixed currency, stricter wins, 0 disables each, both zero refuses init per the config-class decision.
Reason: Cheap to build, covers small and large accounts.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (F11 pinned, owner ruling 2026-07-29) A position carried across the rollover counts its floating loss against day N and its full realized result against day N+1. Deliberate conservative double-count, protected by a permanent acceptance test.
Reason: Direct consequence of banning day-start-equity caching.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (F12 pinned, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Realized whitelist = DEAL_TYPE_BUY and DEAL_TYPE_SELL only. Balance, credit, charge, correction, bonus, dividend, and interest deals are excluded from realized; balance-type deals participate only in the Q2 base reconstruction.
Reason: Makes "balance-type excluded by construction" explicit and deliberate.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (F13 pinned, owner ruling 2026-07-29) Timer-driven architecture, EventSetTimer(1). OnTradeTransaction is acceleration only. OnTick unused.
Reason: OnTick starves on quiet charts and dead sessions; transaction delivery is not guaranteed by the platform.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) Naming discipline. The bare word "heartbeat" is never used in LEDGER.md or SPEC_v0.1.md. "Mutex heartbeat" = the AG_HB_<login> GlobalVariable proving a live instance. "Network heartbeat" = the external dead-man ping of the deferred visibility phase. The once-per-minute journal line, which is neither, is the "liveness journal line".
Reason: One word was carrying two unrelated meanings across a lock-critical design and a deferred network feature.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) Lock artifacts are never deleted, only quarantined. Standing principle, applies to state files, lock mirrors, and any future artifact carrying lock state, regardless of which project wrote it.
Reason: Deleting a lock artifact is the exact bypass this product exists to prevent, so the executor must never do it as housekeeping.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner note 2026-07-29) The external healthchecks.io dead-man check belonging to the deleted prior project has been deleted by the owner. When the deferred visibility phase opens, provision a fresh check with a new UUID. The old UUID is never reused.
Reason: No network path exists in v0, so there is no impact now; reusing a retired dead-man UUID would silence or misattribute alerts later.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Amendment 1, owner ruling 2026-07-29) LEDGER.md is tracked in git as a governance record. Supersedes the inherited local-only policy for this one file. CLAUDE.md stays local: configuration, not record.
Reason: LEDGER holds the FINAL decisions code is measured against; a single untracked copy is a governance single point of failure. Owner has ruled this question before: governance records are tracked.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Amendment 2a/2b, owner ruling 2026-07-29, home proposed by executor per the amendment, pre-authorized shape) SAFE_HALT evidence lives in a separate halt_<login>.dat, not in the state file. Contents: format version, login, session records (init timestamp, clean-exit flag), halt flag, halt reason, halt time, checksum. Same atomic-write and loud-failure semantics as the state file. Crash count = unclean sessions inside the window, so clean re-inits (input change, chart change, recompile) never accumulate toward SAFE_HALT and a malfunctioning guardian cannot be revived by reboot.
Reason: The state file is charter-constrained to lock state only and SAFE_HALT is explicitly not a lock; a separate file keeps that constraint intact instead of quietly widening it.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Amendment 2c, owner ruling 2026-07-29, mechanism pre-approved as acceptable in the amendment) Manual resume from SAFE_HALT = deleting halt_<login>.dat by hand, documented as the official procedure in SPEC and logged as such by the EA when it boots after a deletion. No input toggle, no automatic clearing.
Reason: Resumption must be a deliberate human act; inputs are accident-prone and already treated as suspect while locked.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29, process) Only the owner marks an entry FINAL. The executor proposes; the owner rules. An executor-authored entry is recorded as a proposal until the owner ratifies it, and is then re-sourced to the owner with "(ratified)".
Reason: An executor marking its own decision FINAL locks the project against the only person entitled to lock it. This already happened once, with the clock exemption below.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29 (ratified), proposed by the executor) Crash-loop session timestamps and the mutex heartbeat timestamp use the local clock, explicitly exempt from the Q7 TimeCurrent-only rule. Amendment A3 extends the same exemption to the proof-of-life interval and the seconds-in-state counter.
Reason: Q7 governs expiry and anchor decisions; SAFE_HALT is not a lock, mutex staleness is not an expiry, and a liveness signal that freezes in a dead market is not a liveness signal. TimeCurrent freezes in dead markets, which would make a healthy instance look stale (false takeover) and crash timestamps unrecordable offline.
Residual, accepted: clock manipulation across repeated hard kills could compress session timestamps into the crash-loop window and force a false SAFE_HALT, disarming the guardian. It requires repeatedly killing the terminal, already conceded as kill-equivalent and detect-only, so it gains the adversary nothing over leaving the terminal dead. Recorded in the SPEC threat model.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29 (ratified), proposed by the executor) AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS stays a compile-time constant at 30 s and does not become an input.
Reason: It is an observability invariant, not a tuning knob. Exposing it lets someone set it to an hour and recreate exactly the blind spot that cost this project a day, and a config value that can disable the fail-visible guarantee belongs to the core class or nowhere. Nowhere is simpler.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) AG_MUTEX_STALE_SECONDS stays a compile-time constant at 10 s and is deliberately not an input, same class of reasoning as the liveness interval. Recorded so it reads as a decision rather than an accident of implementation.
Reason: The value governs when one instance may seize the mutex from another. An input could be set long enough to leave a crashed instance's mutex held indefinitely, or short enough to authorize takeover from a live instance that merely stalled a moment. Accepted for now, revisitable if measurement shows 10 s is wrong for real relaunch times.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) A persistence model that was never loaded is never written. Any save path must prove the model it holds came from a load or a deliberate initialization, never from default-constructed emptiness. Binding beyond the halt file: applies to the lock state file and any future artifact carrying lock or halt state.
Reason: Generalized from the SAFE_HALT bypass found on 2026-07-29, where OnDeinit after a refused OnInit wrote an empty model over the halt file and returned a malfunctioning guardian to service with no human act.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) Manual-resume detection is best-effort by construction and is recorded as such, not as a guarantee. It infers a human act from a GV-versus-file mismatch, so any route producing that mismatch produces a false resume.
Reason: After the never-loaded-never-written fix the mechanism is sound for the one known divergence route. Flagged for revisit if another route appears, rather than trusting the inference.
Status: REVISIT

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) R1, crash-loop primitive: consecutive unclean sessions with a pairwise gap bound. Trip when more than CrashLoopMaxInits consecutive unclean sessions exist and each adjacent pair of inits lies within CrashLoopWindowSeconds. Supersedes the rolling-window counting of amendment A1; the A1 file format is untouched.
Reason: The only option whose shipped default is both demonstrable by real kills and immune to slow-spread false disarms, and it uses init timestamps already persisted, so no new field and no SPEC schema change. Grounded in the 2026-07-30 measurement that routine shutdowns and routine re-inits produce clean records, which reset the chain, so only genuine process deaths accumulate.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) R2 absorbed into R1, not deferred. CrashLoopWindowSeconds default becomes 300, redefined as the pairwise gap bound. Under the conjunction the discriminating work moves to consecutiveness, so a wide bound is cheap: unrelated unclean deaths are hours or days apart, real crash loops are seconds to minutes apart. 300 catches a slow terminal-level crash-restart loop that 60 would miss, while a measured 30 to 40 s kill cycle sits comfortably inside it. Executor obligation: confirm concurrence in one line against the Stage 4 cycle measurement without stopping; if the measurement shows the A3 procedure cannot trip at 300, report before adjusting anything.
Reason: See R1. The bound's job is to separate regimes that are orders of magnitude apart, not to be tight.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) R3, server offset, is a measurement and a record correction, not a decision gate. The executor measures, corrects the audit record in the ledger, and continues without stopping; escalation only if the measurement contradicts a FINAL decision.
Reason: Measurements are not rulings; a gate that an executor can answer from evidence is not a real gate.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) R4, the bypass demonstration on build 694d570 runs both refusal rows at contact 1: both limits zero, and a malformed but nonzero core input.
Reason: One extra minute at the dialog converts the ledger claim that every config-refusal return reaches the bypass path from source-read to demonstrated.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-29) Proof of life, SPEC amendment A3. Every state, SYNCING and LOCKED included, emits a periodic proof-of-life journal line at a fixed interval carrying current state, seconds in state, and the specific condition being waited on where the state is transitional. Supersedes the section 6 scoping of that line to ACTIVE.
Reason: A state the EA can occupy indefinitely while emitting nothing violates fail-visible. This exact gap hid a dead timer for hours: a stuck guardian and a healthy one looked identical from outside.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) Any witness-supplied locked_until (file or GV) is clamped to AgNextDayAnchor(current TimeCurrent) at evaluation time in the Phase 2 boot-lock derivation (F4).
Reason: Q1 already guarantees the system itself never legitimately produces a locked_until beyond tomorrow's anchor; a value further out is not one the system wrote. Clamping bounds a griefed or fat-fingered GV value to at most one extra day-cycle while preserving the OR-only-adds-lock property.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) A state file that parses validly and passes checksum but carries a login not matching the current account is treated as CORRUPT_STATE-equivalent: quarantined as .bad, loud WARN, fresh file written, locked via CORRUPT_STATE.
Reason: Errs locked and loud; this project has already been bitten once by foreign-project residue sitting at an expected path (2026-07-29 residue finding).
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) The Phase 4 weekly chart-banner field shows the raw weekly PnL currency figure only.
Reason: Any additional reference point (e.g. a ratio against the daily limit) invents a threshold the charter never specified and risks being read as a weekly enforcement limit that does not exist.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30) Acceptance-test float comparisons (Base, limit, PnL figures) use a flat 0.01 account-currency-unit epsilon. Separately, the live breach comparison itself, wherever any epsilon is applied to it, must err toward breach: total <= -limit + epsilon is acceptable, total <= -limit - epsilon is not.
Reason: A test-assertion epsilon and a live-enforcement epsilon are different obligations; conflating them risks an implementer picking the sign that lets a real breach slip through as within-tolerance.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-01) Account instrument scope and Market Watch composition. The account trades gold, NASDAQ, and possibly silver, nothing else. Market Watch is XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn only. BTCUSD.ecn is removed and never returns. All subscribed instruments are 24/5, so the weekend close is a reliable no-tick window by design. Consequence recorded with the ruling: the frozen-upper-bound finding is downgraded from blocker class to a Phase 1 correctness obligation, not closed, and its crypto example is struck as no longer operative.
Reason: A 24/7 instrument in Market Watch keeps TimeCurrent advancing through the weekend, which destroys the A8 no-tick window and is the only route by which a deal could execute inside a frozen PnL window on this account. Removing it makes both impossible by construction here, while the general upper-bound defect remains a Phase 1 obligation because it does not need a 24/7 instrument.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q6 clarification, owner ruling 2026-07-30, drafted by the executor, transcribed here 2026-08-04 from docs/DESIGN_PHASE1_2.md where it was ratified) Q6's snapshot rule governs the locked window once a valid snapshot exists. It does not by itself state what governs the derived-breach comparison (SPEC 4.6) when no valid snapshot is available to read, meaning the state file is missing, corrupted, or login-mismatched, which is precisely F4's own defense scenario. In that specific case the derived comparison falls back to the limit computed live from current inputs and the live-reconstructed base (SPEC 4.2/4.3), never to a cached or assumed value. This does not weaken Q6: a valid snapshot, once available, still governs unconditionally and always wins over live inputs, exactly as ruled. The fallback only ever activates in the absence of a snapshot to read, which is precisely the condition under which Q6's original rule has nothing to apply to. Consequence stated plainly with the ruling: this fallback is what makes file plus GV deletion alone, inputs untouched, fully defended by derived history, and it is also why file plus GV deletion combined with input inflation remains an accepted residual gap under SPEC 4.6, since the fallback then reads the inflated input as if it were legitimate. Extended, not superseded, by the ratchet: the ratchet fold-in inserts a tier between the snapshot and this live-input fallback.
Reason: Q6 as originally worded presumed a snapshot exists to read, and left silent exactly the case F4 is designed to defend. A rule with nothing to apply to is not a rule, so the gap had to be named and filled explicitly rather than left to an implementer's default.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-07-30, transcribed here 2026-08-04 from docs/DESIGN_PHASE1_2.md) Build the ratchet defense against active-state limit inflation, raising DailyLossPercent or DailyLossCurrency before any breach. Scope is Phase 2, implemented only after Phase 0 is green. The log-loudly-and-accept alternative is rejected. The mechanism is NOT covered by this ruling and stays open, see the ISSUES entry: the corrected single-scalar mechanism carries an UNRULED marker in the design document and is not authority until ratified.
Reason: Owner's grounds, on the record: this product's entire posture is friction rather than prevention already, since killing the terminal always works and is conceded everywhere else in the design, so "friction, not prevention" disqualifies nothing here either. The named threat is impulsive loosening under pressure, and a deliberate multi-step requirement is exactly the defense against impulse. The executor's initial dismissal of the threat as a non-issue was withdrawn as unsound. Residual accepted with the ruling: the floor has no history-backed third witness, because an input value is never a deal and leaves no trace in broker history, so file plus GV destruction alone resets it, which is weaker than the lock's own three-witness resilience and is documented rather than fixed.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-03) Merge gate replaced. A branch merges to main when the work in that branch is complete and proven, not when the phase as a whole is green. "The work in that branch" means all of it: a branch carrying an unfinished item does not qualify on the strength of its finished ones. "Proven" is not executor discretion, it is the standing evidence rule, a row closes only on evidence present on disk at the stated path with result, build hash and evidence pointer recorded in this ledger. The owner-instruction requirement is unchanged and still binds; only the phase-green conjunct is removed. Supersedes the Phase-0-green gate in the merge plan of docs/DESIGN_PHASE1_2.md, whose merge mechanics (real merge commit, never rebase, never fast-forward-only, never a side-picking strategy, hand union resolve respecting each section's ordering, verification by per-section counts and opening-line identity against both parents) are untouched and remain binding.
Reason: The phase-green gate would have kept proven work off main for as long as any unrelated row stayed open, and it would have forced main to sit at an older point precisely while new work touches core protection logic. The replacement is narrower and stronger: nothing merges unless its own proofs are banked here. Checked before ruling, and recorded so the question is not reopened: the gate protected four things and the replacement covers all four. Merge mechanics and owner control are carried forward unchanged. Keeping unproven work off main is covered more tightly per item than per phase. The one property dropped, main advancing only to points where the whole phase passed, depends on nothing here, because deployment is a source copy into the terminal data folder followed by a MetaEditor compile at a specific commit, never a checkout of main, so main was never the release source; and the gate caught no cross-row interaction that the acceptance matrix does not catch anyway, since withholding a merge runs no row.
Consequence, recorded with the ruling: the 2026-08-03 merge ce6964d is not an exception under this standard, it is the standard applied. Everything on that branch was complete and proven, and this ruling changes the plan rather than violating it. The deviation noted in that merge's ACTIONS entry is reclassified accordingly, from a departure from the plan to the ruling that rewrote it.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-03) Visible refusal banner on both refusal paths. The mutex refusal and all five config refusals draw one dated REFUSED banner, REFUSED plus date, time, and reason, through a single helper that sets a dedicated g_init_refused flag immediately before every refusal return in OnInit; OnDeinit skips AgBannerClear while the flag is set, so a refused init leaves its banner on the chart. No path is left silent. The session scoped limitation is accepted: the banner lives until the next attach on that chart or the terminal restart, per the 2026-08-03 measurement that a chart Comment does not survive a restart, and no persistence beyond Comment is required. The clear gate keys on the dedicated refusal flag, never on g_ag_halt_loaded or any other proxy whose semantic is not init refused.
Reason: No holes in the lockout mechanism. A guardian that refuses invisibly is indistinguishable from a guardian that is absent.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-04) Backward clock steps in the crash-loop chain walk. A negative gap between two adjacent session inits, which is what a backward local clock step produces, counts as inside CrashLoopWindowSeconds and does not break the unclean chain. The chain therefore errs toward tripping in both clock directions.
Reason: Breaking the chain on a negative gap creates a one-step false disarm. Anyone able to move the local clock backward once, between two kills, resets the count and disarms crash-loop detection with a single act. The residual already accepted in the SPEC threat model runs the opposite direction, compressing timestamps to force a false SAFE_HALT, and that one costs the adversary repeated terminal kills for nothing they could not get by leaving the terminal dead. A one-act disarm is strictly worse than a laborious false trip, so the bound is read as a ceiling on separation and nothing else. Recorded in SPEC Amendment A4.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-04) The twelve a9 acceptance vectors are committed to the repository under docs/vectors/. The terminal Presets folder remains the OPERATIVE copy, the only one the MetaTrader Load browser reads and the only one an acceptance row actually loads. The repository copy is the REVIEWABLE record and is not read by anything at runtime.
