Sync direction is repository to terminal, never back: edit and commit here, copy the file into the terminal Presets folder, restart the terminal, then confirm the file is listed in the Load browser before running any row that depends on it. The restart is mandatory, not a precaution, because a running terminal never enumerates files added to its data folder after it started. If the two copies ever disagree, the terminal copy is what ran and the repository copy is wrong; correct the repository copy to match what ran and record why, never the reverse. Procedure written into docs/vectors/README.md.
Reason: A vector that exists only inside the terminal data folder is invisible to review, so a change to one appears from nowhere and no diff can be inspected. Committing the twelve unmodified first, then changing them, makes the 60 to 300 change a reviewable eleven-line diff against a baseline. The operative copy stays in the terminal because that is a platform fact, not a choice, and naming which copy is authoritative prevents a future session from proving a row against a file the terminal never loaded.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-04) SweepPeriodSeconds wording, closing the open item. Both the SPEC section 5 input table cell and the source comment at AccountGuardian.mq5:21 change from "clamped 1..5" to language stating the value is refused outside 1..5. Applied in the Stage 5a pass, which is the next code change the earlier entry attached the comment fix to.
Reason: The old wording is wrong about behavior, not merely imprecise. It implies a value of 9 is corrected to 5, when the EA in fact returns INIT_PARAMETERS_INCORRECT and refuses to initialize, which is what the Q4 config-class rule and the section 8 malformed definition both require. A reader trusting the table would expect a running guardian where there is none, which is the exact failure mode this project treats as worst-case.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-04) The repository does NOT live inside the MT5 terminal data folder. The two stay separate, and deployment stays what it already is, an explicit source copy into the terminal data folder followed by a MetaEditor compile at a specific commit. The question raised was whether to host the working tree inside the data folder so the terminal reads it directly and the copy-plus-compile step disappears. Answer is no, permanently.
Reason: What the merged layout would have bought, stated fairly because it is real: no source copying at all, git tracking exactly the bytes the terminal loads, and the deployed-versus-committed verification step becoming unnecessary, which was genuine work in this session. It loses anyway on three grounds. First, the data folder is continuously rewritten by the terminal itself, journals, history, chart profiles, gvariables.dat, so the working tree would never be clean and git status would carry no signal without a large and fragile ignore set that has to be maintained against a folder layout this project does not control. Second, and worse, the halt file and every state artifact live in that folder, so a careless git operation, a checkout, a clean, or a branch switch, would delete or alter live guardian state; the present separation makes that class of accident impossible rather than merely unlikely. Third, it erases the boundary between what has been tested and what is running: every save would touch the live environment immediately, destroying the staged deploy this project depends on and which the merge-gate ruling of 2026-08-03 already relies on when it records that main is never the release source.
Alternative considered and not adopted, recorded so it is not re-proposed as new: symlink only MQL5\Experts and MQL5\Include from the terminal tree into the repository, a common arrangement in MQL5 work, which would keep journals, history and state artifacts outside the repository and so answers the first two grounds above. Rejected on the third ground alone: a terminal-side compile would immediately change what loads on the next init, removing the controlled deploy step. Available to revisit if deploy overhead ever becomes the actual bottleneck, but not while this software guards real money.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-04) Remote state is owner-managed. The pushes to origin were the owner's own manual actions through GitHub Desktop, made whenever pending commits appeared there, and they are neither an executor action nor a violation. Two things follow and both bind permanently. One, the executor never pushes: it commits locally and stops there, and a push happens only when the owner performs it or explicitly instructs it in the session. Two, no ledger entry may assert "not pushed", "nothing pushed", "local only", an ahead-by count, or any other remote position as a durable fact. Remote position may be recorded only as a dated observation of what a named local ref said at the moment of writing, marked as such, and never as a standing property of the repository. Existing ACTIONS entries carrying the old wording are not edited, ACTIONS being append only; they are superseded by the ninth ACTIONS entry of 2026-08-04, which states this ruling and records the position as of that moment.
Reason: The owner pushes out of band at will, so any claim about the remote is stale the moment it is written and the ledger has no way to know when it went stale. The executor can only speak to its own conduct, which is that it does not push, and a local remote-tracking ref proves only what the last fetch or push saw, not where the remote stands now. Recording an unfalsifiable claim as fact is the failure this ledger exists to prevent, and here it also risked reading as an accusation against the owner for operating their own repository.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (owner ruling 2026-08-05, Phase 1 kickoff, recorded 2026-08-08 after the blocking investigation the ruling itself required was delivered) Day-anchor base is the account Balance, and the trading day starts at 01:00 Israel time. The base clause composes with Q2 as already frozen rather than replacing it: the base is the anchor-moment balance, reconstructed live as current Balance minus the sum of all deals since the day anchor, all deal types alike, never Equity and never cached. The 01:00 clause supersedes SPEC section 4.1's "floor of TimeCurrent to server-day midnight" and requires a SPEC amendment, scheduled as A5. The clock source is unchanged and unchangeable here: the anchor is computed from TimeCurrent exclusively, per Q7, so the day boundary is a fixed offset applied to server time and never to a local or GMT clock. How "01:00 Israel" is operationalized against the server clock, fixed 01:00 server against an Israel-civil-corrected boundary, is NOT settled by this ruling and is question 1 of the Phase 1 kickoff plan, held in ISSUES.
Reason: Owner's ruling, recorded as given. Two things are worth carrying with it because they were measured rather than assumed and they are what made the ruling safe to record. First, Balance rather than Equity keeps the base invariant intraday, which is the property the whole Q2 identity and the ratchet design both rest on; an Equity base would move with every floating tick and would make the limit itself move under a losing position, tightening or loosening enforcement for reasons that have nothing to do with a deposit or a withdrawal. Second, a 01:00 boundary rather than 00:00 places the anchor clear of the measured nightly quote gap, 00:00:18 to 00:04:48 pinned at the prior second on the 2026-07-31 measurement, so the anchor moment is not routinely inside a stretch where TimeCurrent is frozen. The weekend freeze still spans two anchors every week, which the plan's investigation answers in full: the anchor holds the Friday boundary through the freeze and lands on Monday 01:00 at the reopen, and no trading deal can execute inside the freeze under the FINAL Market Watch composition.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q1, owner ruling 2026-08-08) "01:00 Israel" operationalizes as a fixed compile-time constant, 01:00 server, per Q7's rule that TimeCurrent is the only permitted clock in an anchor decision. In the measured present state (server tracking Israel's own offset) this equals 01:00 Israel. The known consequence is accepted rather than treated as a defect: inside a DST divergence window the anchor maps to 00:00 Israel civil, one hour early, exactly as the plan's section 2.2 enumerated per candidate server peg. The owner commits to a scheduled revisit: after the 2026-10-25 to 2026-11-01 harvest measures which DST calendar the broker server actually follows, this operationalization is REVISITED against that measured fact, not re-argued from documentation alone.
Reason: The offset is a fixed constant precisely because an input able to move the day boundary would recreate the same inflation-adjacent surface the ratchet ruling already closes elsewhere; Q7 already forbids any clock but TimeCurrent from touching an anchor decision, so an Israel-civil correction would need either a second, forbidden clock or a server-DST fact this repository cannot yet measure. Accepting the drift now and revisiting on a measured fact in October is cheaper and more honest than guessing at the server's calendar today.
Status: FINAL, REVISIT scheduled for the 2026-10-25 to 2026-11-01 measurement window

Decision: (Q2, owner ruling 2026-08-08) Interim breach posture for the Phase 1 deployed build, before Phase 2 enforcement exists: on a computed breach, the guardian raises a loud ALERT plus a journal arithmetic line, stays in ACTIVE, and repeats the ALERT and journal line at a bounded cadence while the condition holds. No lock, no sweep, no state change; Phase 1 detects and shouts, it does not enforce.
Reason: Owner explicitly sanctions the no-enforcement window this creates until Phase 2 lands; a real breach during Phase 1 is visible and loud rather than either silent or falsely locked by code that does not yet exist to unlock correctly.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q3, owner ruling 2026-08-08) DEAL_FEE is added to the per-deal value formula everywhere it appears: realized (SPEC 4.2), the Q2 base-reconstruction sum (SPEC 4.3), and the derived-lock replay (design doc item 3). The formula becomes DEAL_PROFIT + DEAL_SWAP + DEAL_COMMISSION + DEAL_FEE uniformly, superseding the three-property formula SPEC 4.2 currently states. SPEC wording lands in amendment A6.
Reason: DEAL_FEE is a real per-deal money field MT5 exposes that the original three-property formula omitted; the Q2 identity needs a deal's full balance effect or Base stops being algebraically equal to the day-anchor balance, which is the property everything else in Phase 1 and the ratchet design rests on.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q4, owner ruling 2026-08-08) The anchor-boundary second convention: DEAL_TIME >= anchor counts to the NEW day. A deal stamped exactly at the anchor second belongs to the day that just started, not the day that just ended. SPEC wording lands in amendment A6.
Reason: Matches HistorySelect's own inclusive-from semantics (the window already reads [anchor, to) treating the anchor instant as the start of the selected range), so the convention needs no special-cased comparison anywhere in the engine, only a documented statement of what the platform already does.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q5, owner ruling 2026-08-08) Withdrawals are UNAVAILABLE on JustMarkets demo 1200252169; deposits ARE available through the JustMarkets personal area. Rows P1-E, P1-F and P1-G are redesigned around deposit-only vectors. The withdrawal-neutrality direction of the deposit/withdrawal-neutrality acceptance test (DN-2, the design doc's P1-2) closes by the Q2 algebraic identity argument alone, marked explicitly as PASS-BY-CONSTRUCTION rather than as a row proven by a demo withdrawal, the same category A5's Phase 0 trade-disallowed row already occupies.
Reason: The identity in design doc item 2 proves withdrawal-neutrality symmetrically to deposit-neutrality (Base_after = Base_before under either a +X or a -Y balance-type deal, same derivation, opposite sign); a platform limitation that makes the demo-side half untestable does not weaken an algebraic proof that holds regardless of which direction the demo can exercise.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q6, owner ruling 2026-08-08) Phase 0's last remaining gate, whether last-used EA inputs were restored to the ruled defaults, is CLOSED. Owner dialog reading, quoted: all eight inputs read at their ruled defaults, including HistoryStablePolls=3, the input the 2026-08-06 attempt could not verify because the attach that was to write it never happened. With this gate closed and worktree-a9-phantom-audit already merged to main (937c604, documented and pushed 2026-08-08), Phase 0 is closed in full: no remainder exists on main. The sequencing question (Phase 1 kickoff plan question 6) is resolved as CLOSED rather than left open.
Reason: This is the owner's own direct reading of the live EA properties dialog, the same class of evidence this ledger has accepted throughout as owner-eyewitness fact (the 2026-08-03 Market Watch reading, the 2026-08-06 chart01.chr byte-identity reading), distinct from a claimed git or filesystem action, which is what Ruling 1 (2026-08-05, session report is not evidence) actually binds. A dialog reading of input values is not a claim about a commit; it needs no independent git-level re-derivation to count as evidence here.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q7, owner ruling 2026-08-08) The XAUUSD.ecn, US100.ecn and XAGUSD.ecn symbol-specification read (GUI, read-only, corroborating A8 and the DST divergence-window analysis of section 2.2) rides the owner's next terminal session, whenever that falls. No specific date is fixed.
Reason: Owner's scheduling ruling, recorded as given. The read is corroboration, not a gate; nothing in Phase 1 or Phase 0 is blocked on it.
Status: FINAL

Decision: (Q8 weekend-reopen amendment, owner ruling 2026-08-09, found during the Stage 5 deploy) ALERT-AND-ADVANCE replaces halt-the-pass. A forward jump of more than one day raises the loud ALERT naming both anchors, then ACCEPTS the fresh anchor and advances the high-water mark, so evaluation continues and the condition self-clears after one pass. No pass is ever halted. The ALERT is cadence-capped at AG_LIFE_INTERVAL_SECONDS exactly as the Q2 breach ALERT is, with the journal line still written on every occurrence. Every other clause of the Q8 entry below stands unchanged: the latch is still in-memory only, never persisted, still seeded on the first pass of a session, and backward steps and same-day recomputes are still unaffected with the mark never receding.
Reason: Owner's ground, on the record: a guardian that stops computing is fail-open, and worse than a widened window, per the A4 precedent that the system errs toward staying live and loud. The defect the amendment fixes was measured rather than argued, and it was deterministic rather than probabilistic. The original rule could not self-clear against a clock that only moves forward, because self-clearing required the fresh anchor to fall BACK within one day of the retained mark. The weekend freeze holds the Friday 01:00 anchor for about 48 hours and lands on Monday 01:00 at the reopen, a three-day advance of 259200 seconds against an 86400 bound, which is precisely the signature the rule treated as an attack. Any instance running across a weekend would therefore have stalled evaluation permanently until restart: no rollover line, no recompute, numbers frozen, while AgAlertEvent, which carried no cadence cap unlike the Q2 ALERT, raised a modal popup on every one-second timer tick. Recorded plainly because it is the governance point: the CODE was faithful to Q8, and Q8 was wrong against a weekend freeze the Phase 1 plan had itself measured and described in its own section 2.3 as producing exactly a Friday-to-Monday advance. Two FINAL rulings collided, and the executor escalated it as a ruling question rather than fixing it unilaterally, per the standing rule that only the owner marks FINAL.
Executor's mechanism, implemented same session, commit 301fc85: AgAnchorSanityCheck sets g_ag_anchor_jump_note naming previous_high, accepted and jump width, then advances g_ag_high_anchor and returns fresh; the string g_ag_anchor_waiting_on is removed. The announcement moved OUT of Clock.mqh into the caller, AgEvaluateActive, because the cadence cap needs the local clock and TimeLocal is forbidden in Clock.mqh by that file's own header (Q7) and by the Stage 3 static acceptance row that greps for it; Clock.mqh now performs no logging at all. Verified after the edit: zero TimeLocal, TimeGMT or TimeTradeServer references survive in Clock.mqh and Pnl.mqh, and zero references to the removed g_ag_anchor_waiting_on survive anywhere in the tree. SPEC A6 and plan 4.1a amended with the superseded halt wording kept on the record rather than deleted; the Stage 2 vectors extended from 20 checks to 26, the four new ones replaying the exact live signature, seed Friday 2026.08.07 01:00:00 then feed Monday 2026.08.10 01:00:00, expecting acceptance, the mark advancing, jump=259200s named, and the note cleared on the following pass.
Status: FINAL, amends the halt clause of the entry below; mechanism as implemented above

Decision: (Q8, owner ruling 2026-08-08) Defense against a forward clock jump moving the day anchor's lower bound and silently excluding real deals: the engine keeps the highest day anchor it has observed in memory. If a freshly computed anchor exceeds that high-water mark by more than one day, the evaluation HALTS loudly, with an ALERT naming both the retained high anchor and the rejected fresh one, rather than silently narrowing the window to the fresh (and untrustworthy) value. The latch is IN-MEMORY ONLY, never persisted, consistent with the never-loaded-never-written decision and with Phase 1 persisting nothing at all; a restart clears it, which is accepted because a restart re-derives everything from history with no anchor to sanity-check against yet.
Executor's mechanism, translating the ruling into the exact primitive (not itself re-opening anything): maintain `g_ag_high_anchor` (datetime, in-memory, seeded to the first computed anchor of the session). Each ACTIVE-state evaluation pass computes `fresh = AgDayAnchor(AgServerNow())` as always (4.1, unchanged) and compares it against the retained high-water mark before using it: if `fresh <= g_ag_high_anchor`, evaluation proceeds normally using `fresh` for the window (this is the unchanged backward-step case of section 2.1, which already widens the window and errs strict; the high-water mark itself does not recede). If `fresh > g_ag_high_anchor` and `fresh - g_ag_high_anchor <= 86400`, this is an ordinary single-day advance (or same-day repeat): evaluation proceeds using `fresh`, and `g_ag_high_anchor` advances to `fresh`. If `fresh - g_ag_high_anchor > 86400`, the jump is anomalous: evaluation for this pass uses the retained `g_ag_high_anchor` rather than `fresh` (so the window is never narrowed by the untrusted value), `g_ag_high_anchor` does NOT advance, and an ALERT plus journal line fires naming both `g_ag_high_anchor` and the rejected `fresh`, repeating every pass while the condition persists. The condition self-clears the moment a later pass's `fresh` falls back within one day of the retained high anchor, with no restart required. SPEC wording (amendment A6) and a Stage 2 synthetic acceptance row (feed a two-day-plus jump, expect HALT with both anchors named and no window narrowing; then feed a legitimate one-day advance, expect normal acceptance and the high-water mark advancing) land in the captured plan document.
Status: FINAL, mechanism as translated above

Decision: (Q9, owner ruling 2026-08-08) Defense against Base moving because current Balance has updated for a deal that local history has not yet synced: on any evaluation pass where the computed result would move abruptly and no new deal is visible in the selected history, the breach declaration DEFERS by exactly one timer pass and re-evaluates, rather than declaring on a possibly-incoherent read. The deferral is one pass and one pass only, so a real breach is delayed by at most one second (the current SweepPeriodSeconds default), never suppressed.
Executor's mechanism, translating the ruling (not re-opening it): the coherence signal is deal count, `HistoryDealsTotal()` read immediately after the same `HistorySelect` call the realized sum already uses, compared against the prior pass's count. A one-shot deferral flag, `g_ag_breach_deferred_once` (in-memory, reset whenever no breach is pending), governs the bound: on a pass where the breach condition evaluates true, if the deal count is unchanged from the prior pass AND this breach has not already been deferred once, the pass logs the deferral (naming the unchanged deal count) and does not declare; the flag is set so the very next pass, whatever the deal count reads, declares if the condition still evaluates true. This guarantees the delay is bounded at exactly one pass regardless of whether the underlying cause resolves. SPEC wording (amendment A6) and a Stage 3 or Stage 4 acceptance row (force two consecutive true breach evaluations with an unchanged deal count between them; expect exactly one deferral then a declaration on the second pass) land in the captured plan document.
Status: FINAL, mechanism as translated above

Decision: (Q10 reconnect-coherence amendment, owner ruling 2026-08-09, found during review of worktree-phase1-pnl-core tip e5d32bc) Recovery from DEGRADED requires the same history-stability discipline as the SYNCING exit condition, rather than the immediate clean re-evaluation the original Q10 ruling below specified. On the first connected pass after DEGRADED, do not run the breach evaluation until `AgHistoryStable(HistoryStablePolls)` passes again; the LIFE line shows the poll count while waiting, prefixed RESYNC to distinguish it from initial SYNCING. State stays ACTIVE, no transition. Q9's one-pass deferral is unaffected and continues to apply only to the connected steady state, since it was ruled for a one-tick sync gap, not a post-disconnect resync. This amends only the "on reconnect, evaluation resumes with an immediate clean re-evaluation" clause of the Q10 entry below; every other clause of that entry (the connection signal, what is skipped while DEGRADED, the DEGRADED marker, no ALERT from DEGRADED data) stands unchanged.
Executor's mechanism, implemented same session, commit `fb82a70`: new in-memory flag `g_ag_resyncing`, set true alongside `g_ag_degraded` on disconnect. On the first connected pass, before the Q8 anchor check, a gate polls `AgHistoryStable(HistoryStablePolls)`; while false the pass returns early (`waiting_on = "RESYNC: polls=<n>/<required>"`, no transition, `g_ag_degraded` stays true). Once stable, `g_ag_resyncing` clears and the pass proceeds through the pre-existing Q8/computation/Q9/Q2 chain unchanged. SPEC A6's Q10 bullet and the plan's section 4.3b and P1-O row updated to the amended wording the same session.
Status: FINAL, amends the reconnect clause of the entry below; mechanism as implemented above

Decision: (Q10, owner ruling 2026-08-08) While the terminal is disconnected, the evaluation marks itself DEGRADED and takes no breach decision from a frozen quote, since no enforcement action is possible without a connection regardless of what the numbers say. On reconnect, evaluation resumes with an immediate clean re-evaluation, no re-sync wait beyond what already exists. No ALERT fires from data computed while DEGRADED, consistent with the Q2 interim-posture cadence only ever firing from a fresh, connected evaluation.
Executor's mechanism, translating the ruling (not re-opening it): the connection check is `TerminalInfoInteger(TERMINAL_CONNECTED)`, the same signal design doc item 1's `AgHistoryStable` already resets on. When false, the ACTIVE-state evaluation for that pass is skipped entirely (no realized/floating/base/limit recompute, no Q8 anchor check, no Q9 coherence check, none of it), the LIFE line and banner show a DEGRADED marker alongside the last-known figures rather than fresh ones, and no ALERT of any kind fires from that pass. The instant `TERMINAL_CONNECTED` reads true again, the very next pass runs the full evaluation exactly as any other ACTIVE pass, with Q8 and Q9 both applying normally to it since nothing about disconnection touches the high-water anchor or the deal-count baseline. SPEC wording (amendment A6) and an acceptance row reusing the existing disconnect procedure (already exercised for the A8 weekend harvest and the kill-session work) land in the captured plan document: observe DEGRADED on the LIFE line through the gap, zero ALERTs during it, and one clean re-evaluation on the line immediately after reconnect.
Status: FINAL, mechanism as translated above
